The owner adopted the recommended decisions except where "Decisions" says otherwise, settled the git path under decision 44, and reversed the deferral of level 1 replacement. Proposals carry the label EG-n, which "Proposal split" defines. "Review history" at the end records how the design reached this form, and "Validation record" records the validation rounds.

Each factual claim about behaviour carries one of these tags where the source matters:

| Tag | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| **[measured]** | A hands-on result from a validation round or a spike, against a stub, a stand-in, or a prototype unless the sentence names another target. |
| **[live]** | Read from a provider's host, anonymously, with no write. |
| **[source]** | Read in a provider's public source code. |
| **[design]** | What this design requires. No run has shown it. |

Most results name the round or spike that produced them in the sentence itself, and the tags mark the claims where the source needs stating.

**Evidence sources.** A *validation round* is one of the numbered rounds of research and hands-on tests that checked this design from 2026-11-01 to 2026-11-03, and "Validation record" lists what each established. The first round verified claims against vendor documentation, source code, the specification, and the Lenny tree. The second and third ran hands-on tests in throwaway containers and a throwaway kind cluster: Envoy 1.40.2 and 1.39.6, Squid 7.7, a Go proxy built on Smokescreen, git 2.49 with git-lfs 3.6, Kubernetes 1.35 on kind, and the client tools Maven, Gradle, `gh`, `glab`, npm, and pip. Each round also included research, and the text calls the first two rounds "the first spike" and "the second spike". The rounds before revision 47 measured a prior loopback git path, which decision 44 calls candidate L, and they stay as history. The current git path was measured in the spike recorded in [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md), and claims that rested on the earlier rounds with no measurement under the current path are listed under "Validation still owed". A *spike* is a hands-on test of named questions, recorded in the design that ran it. A *stub* is a test HTTP server running stock git that stands in for a provider and records what reaches it. It shows what `netagent` forwards and nothing about what a provider would do. A *stand-in* is a test process that plays a Lenny component, such as the mirror, the Token Service, or the decision service, in one run. A *prototype* is a program written to this text for a run, such as a prototype `netagent`, and it is not the component that ships. *A capture* is a recording of a client's traffic through a prototype `netagent`, as [`github-credential-addon-design.md`, "Capture history"](github-credential-addon-design.md#capture-history) records. Rounds of adversarial review and a fact-check examined the revisions that "Review record" lists.

## Requirements

The owner set the following direction:

- Pods may have network access to named destinations. A pod needs some network path to clone a repository.
- Egress filtering applies to all HTTP traffic from pods that enable it, and git is one case of it.
- Runtimes can `git push`.
- Pools can have egress during warm-up, including cloning repositories.
- Lenny ships working defaults. A deployer can replace each default, or augment it with deployer-specific services.
- Different tenants can have different configurations, including different external proxies and git services. Tenant admins get as much flexibility as the platform's safety bounds allow.
- Pull requests and other host operations use the host's standard HTTP APIs through the egress route. Lenny adds no platform tool for them.
- The policy decision service runs separately from the gateway.
- Lenny does not adopt Goblet, which is archived (last commit 2021-07-14).
- This design covers egress and git. Related work lives in separate design documents, each with its own proposals: [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md), [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md), [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md), [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md), [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md), [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md), [`github-credential-addon-design.md`](github-credential-addon-design.md), and [`gitlab-credential-addon-design.md`](gitlab-credential-addon-design.md). A proposal here also carries work outside that scope only when adding it later would force rework of a stored record format, a wire contract, the on-disk layout, or a shipped enforcement point, and then it carries only the hook (see "Reserved for other designs").

## Scope

This design covers how agent pods reach the network and git hosts: the egress path and its decisions, credential delivery to runtimes, the git path and the git mirror, per-tenant routing, replacement of the shipped proxy by a deployer's own, the evidence the path records, and exfiltration controls. It replaces the "Git and egress design under owner review" handoff note in [`gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md`, "10.2 Proposal track"](../gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md#102-proposal-track) as the current statement of the design, and it is the input to the change proposals under "Proposal split". The last item under "Requirements" names the designs that own related work, and "Reserved for other designs" lists the hooks this design carries for them.

## Terms

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| `netagent` | A Lenny container this design adds to every agent pod that uses the local egress endpoint. It runs under its own UID, holds the pod's service account tokens for egress, terminates TLS for the hosts it must inspect, attaches credentials, runs the git path, and applies request rules. See "The `netagent` container". |
| Local egress endpoint (I1) | `netagent`'s HTTP CONNECT proxy on `127.0.0.1:3128`, which every tool in the pod uses as its HTTP proxy. |
| `lenny-egress` | The central egress proxy: an Envoy Deployment in `lenny-system` through which pod traffic leaves the cluster. It asks the decision service about every CONNECT. A deployer can replace it at level 1 or level 2. |
| Decision service | `lenny-egress-authz`, a Lenny service that identifies a pod from its service account token, reads its pod binding, decides each connection from the effective policy, and returns the route to use. It runs one of the mint checks. |
| Git mirror and mirror parent | `lenny-git-mirror`, an optional Lenny service that serves git fetches from per-tenant stores and from public stores. The mirror parent is its long-running server process, which holds the mirror's identity and credentials and runs each `git fetch` and `upload-pack` as a sandboxed child process under another UID. |
| `lenny-evidence` | The one service that writes evidence records. Every producer sends its records to it over mTLS. See "Evidence streams". |
| `pkg/binding` | The package the Token Service, the decision service, and the mirror each run to resolve a pod to its pool, tenant, sessions, and bindings from Kubernetes and the session store. |
| Proxy hop (I2) | The TLS connection from `netagent` to the central proxy, on which `netagent` presents the pod's service account token in `Proxy-Authorization`. |
| Egress decision API (I3) | The Envoy ext_authz API on which a proxy, the git mirror, the gateway at level 1, or the Token Service asks the decision service whether a connection or a token scope is allowed. |
| Egress policy document (I4) | The document Lenny publishes for a level 1 proxy, listing each credential identity with its allowed hosts and credential verifiers. |
| Git endpoint (I5) | The git smart HTTP service `netagent` fetches from for a host: the mirror, a deployer git service, or the upstream host through `lenny-egress`. |
| Upstream chaining (I6) | `lenny-egress` forwarding a CONNECT to a deployer's or tenant's upstream proxy. |
| Pod binding API (I7) | The Lenny gRPC API between `netagent` and the decision service. `netagent` subscribes on it to its pod binding, the covered host set, and the base-destination list, and reports its local decisions on it. |
| Pod credential API (I8) | The Lenny gRPC API on which `netagent`, and the git mirror, ask the Token Service for git tokens, binding secrets, and other credentials. A response carries the binding's authoritative hosts, its `attach` template, `cacheUntil`, which bounds how long `netagent` may hold the credential, `handling`, a list of named steps `netagent` must implement to use it, and any resolved request rules. |
| Pod binding | The pool, tenant, and sessions a pod serves at a moment, which the gateway and the controller record and `pkg/binding` reads. "A pod's binding" and "a binding change" mean this record. A credential binding is a different object. |
| Provisional binding | The pod binding that lets a session being resumed onto a replacement pod run its clone and setup commands before the session is running again. §7.3 records the session on the replacement pod only when the session moves from `resuming` back to `running`, and the clone and setup commands run before that move. The provisional binding, written first, grants read tokens and the secrets of bindings with `delivery: inject`, and it withholds write tokens and the other deliveries until the session is `running`. See "The `netagent` container", **Binding.** |
| Generation | A counter, always named in this document by what it counts. The coordination generation is the session row's `coordination_generation`, which §7.3 and §10.1 increment at each coordinator handoff and each resume attempt, and which fences writes from a stale coordinator. A pod binding carries it, and `netagent` publishes the one it has applied, so the adapter can wait for the current one. A store generation names one version of a mirror store on disk. A credential generation is one rotation of a level 1 identity's credential. |
| Effective policy | The hosts a pod may reach, which the decision service computes from the pool's egress ceiling, the tenant's policy, and the session's host list. See "Effective policy". |
| `gitAccess` and its formula | A pool setting under which a pool with `egressProfile: restricted`, which has no general egress, reaches the mirror and `lenny-egress` for git hosts alone. The `gitAccess` formula under "Effective policy" gives those hosts: the pool's `gitAccess` hosts intersected with the tenant's git hosts, less its denied hosts. |
| `lockEgressToHosts` and `egressLockHosts` | `lockEgressToHosts` is an option on a `userCredential` binding with `delivery: file`, from UC-5, that limits a session to the binding's hosts and the base destinations. The session row records that limit as `egressLockHosts`, which every descendant and derived session inherits, so one setting on the binding becomes one record on each session. |
| Base destinations | The platform's own destinations, which every agent pod reaches whatever its egress policy: the gateway, the object store, and the OTLP collector when one is configured. |
| Egress ceiling | The largest set of hosts any pod of a pool can reach through the proxy, whichever tenant or session it serves. `*` means any public host, and it also matches the hosts a tenant routes to its own outbound connector, which are resolved on the customer's side and never on the platform side, as **Pool ceilings** under [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`, "Which hosts a connector may serve"](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md#which-hosts-a-connector-may-serve) states. |
| Warm-up policy | The hosts and repositories a pool's pods can reach before they are bound to a tenant. Bound pods use the tenant's warm-up additions instead. |
| Tenant egress policy | A tenant's always-allowed hosts, which every session reaches, its session-requestable hosts, which a session can ask for in `egress.allowedHosts`, and its denied hosts. |
| Tenant pool overlay | A record per pool and tenant, written by the tenant admin, that narrows or extends a shared pool's egress and git settings for that tenant alone. The pool's `tenantOverride` flags decide what it may change. See "Tenant pool overlays". |
| Host set | A named, reusable list of host patterns and ports, such as `github` or `pypi`, which ceilings and policies reference. Host data in a host set also lists a provider's alias hosts, LFS hosts, `sshForms`, and credential header names. |
| `proxied` DNS resolver | The CoreDNS instance that pods with `egressProfile: proxied` use as their nameserver. It answers cluster names and returns NXDOMAIN for every other name. "Network policy and placement" states its kind, replicas, and relation to `lenny-agent-dns`. |
| Slot attribution | `netagent`'s mapping of a loopback connection to the slot whose UID owns the connecting socket. See "Slot attribution and fetch grants". |
| Fetch grant | A registration the adapter makes with `netagent` before a `lenny-materialize` run that may fetch. It names the slot, the normalized repository URLs the run may fetch, and a window that ends at the run's deadline, and it lets `netagent` treat the slot's fetch of a named URL inside the window as materialization. See "Slot attribution and fetch grants". |
| Gateway's ref resolver | The gateway's Go client that resolves a `gitClone` ref to a commit SHA at plan bind, with a bounded `ls-refs` call through `lenny-egress`. See "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.** It is unrelated to the `proxied` DNS resolver and to the tenant-object resolver of [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping). |
| Gateway cause assertion | A JWT valid for 60 seconds, signed with the gateway's signing key, with the type `typ: lenny-cause+jwt` and the audience `lenny-cause`. The gateway's `ls-refs` request carries it on the direct path through `lenny-egress` and on the mirror path. It names the tenant, the session's environment E, the session ID, the upstream, and the pool. The decision service verifies it and reads E from it, the mirror forwards it as the cause of the fill that request starts, and the Token Service verifies it. See "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**, and "Git mirror (`lenny-git-mirror`)", **Fill causes**. |
| Route and route target | A tenant setting that sends traffic for matching hosts to a target: `direct`, an upstream proxy, a registered private endpoint, or an outbound connector. Egress routes carry API traffic. Git routes carry git traffic, to a git endpoint, the mirror, or `direct`. Where no route matches, the platform default route applies, which is `direct` unless the platform admin sets another. |
| Upstream and egress group | An upstream is a deployer's or tenant's proxy that `lenny-egress` chains to. An egress group is an additional `lenny-egress` Deployment whose traffic leaves the cluster from a dedicated address, assigned to one tenant. |
| Private destination | A service on a private address that the platform admin registers with an `ipBlock` and grants to tenants. Pods reach it only through `lenny-egress`, which dials the pinned address: the address the decision service returns, so that Envoy never resolves the name. A tenant-registered endpoint is a service on a public address that a tenant registers itself. |
| Level 1 and level 2 | Ways a deployer can replace `lenny-egress` with its own proxy. A level 2 proxy calls the decision service for each connection. A level 1 proxy enforces a host list that Lenny publishes and makes no call per connection. The shipped Envoy proxy is the default. |
| Credential binding | A tenant object that gives sessions a secret for named hosts. Its fields include `secret`, which names what is delivered, `hosts`, `attach`, `scope`, `delivery`, and `requestRules`. See "Credential bindings". |
| Binding `scope` | When a credential binding attaches to a session: `always`, to every session of the tenant; `onRequest`, to a session whose plan names it; or `poolAttach`, to a session on a pool whose add-on attaches it. |
| Delivery | A credential binding's `delivery` field. Under `delivery: inject`, the default, `netagent` attaches the value to requests and the runtime holds only a placeholder. `delivery: federation` writes a short-lived Lenny-issued JWT the runtime exchanges at a provider, `delivery: metadata` serves cloud credentials on a loopback metadata endpoint, and `delivery: file` writes the value to a file the runtime reads. See "Delivery modes". |
| Identity assertion and `identityAssertion` | An identity assertion is a short Lenny-signed token that `netagent` attaches to requests to a service, naming the tenant, user, and session, which the agent never sees. It is a JWT that the Token Service signs with the tenant's identity key. `identityAssertion` is the `secret` kind of a credential binding whose value is an identity assertion, under `delivery: inject` alone. See "Credential bindings" and "Delivery modes", **Identity assertions.**, and [`connector-removal-design.md`, "CR-2 Identity assertion"](connector-removal-design.md#cr-2-identity-assertion). |
| Identity issuer, tenant key set, and deployment key set | A tenant's identity issuer is `https://<lenny>/identity/<tenant-id>`, which publishes the tenant key set, the public keys of that tenant's identity assertions. The deployment key set, at `https://<lenny>/identity/.well-known/jwks.json`, lists every live tenant's keys, each tagged with its tenant, so a service that serves every tenant configures one URL. The Helm value `tokenService.identityAssertion.deploymentKeySet.enabled`, `true` by default, turns the deployment key set off. Both are separate from the federation issuer and its key set. |
| Egress halt, `HaltSessionEgress`, and `CH-EGRESSHALT` | The egress halt is the stop of one slot's egress in `netagent` during a suspension that cannot take hold: `netagent` closes the slot's connections and refuses its new requests with `503` until the halt is lifted. The gateway asks the adapter for it with the RPC `HaltSessionEgress`, and the adapter asks `netagent` on `CH-EGRESSHALT`, an intra-pod channel beside `CH-FETCHGRANT`. See "Slot attribution and fetch grants", **The egress halt (CR-4).** |
| Operator service | A service an operator runs outside Lenny and reaches from sessions through egress, such as an MCP server, an MCP gateway, an agent service, or a memory service. Lenny keeps no record of it ([`connector-removal-design.md`, "CR-3 External services"](connector-removal-design.md#cr-3-external-services)). |
| Gates | Per-tenant grants a platform admin gives, one per risk class, before a tenant can turn on the opt-ins of that class: `storedTokenPush`, `agentReadableDelivery`, `userCredentialDelivery`, and `verbatimClientFiles`. See "Credential delivery to runtimes", **Gates**. In this document "gate" means one of these grants, apart from the archive gate, which is named in full. The checks on a mint are called mint checks, and they are not gates. |
| Mint checks | The independent checks on every mint: the Token Service's own check of the pod, its bindings, and its writable set, and the scope decision it asks of the decision service. A mint happens only when both agree. See "Egress decision service", **Credential requests**. |
| Release | The Token Service handing a secret it already holds, such as a stored API key or a user's token, to `netagent` on the pod credential API. A mint obtains a new token from a provider. |
| Opaque tunnel | A CONNECT tunnel that `netagent` forwards without terminating TLS, so it sees only the host name and port. |
| Terminated host | A host for which `netagent` terminates TLS with the per-pod CA: a host a binding with `delivery: inject` names, a covered host, or a credential-pinned or read-only host. |
| Covered host | A git host that a VCS pool or a git route of the tenant names, or an alias host of that provider. `netagent` terminates TLS for it and runs the git path. See "Git on covered hosts (`netagent`)". |
| Server-name check | A check that a tunnel's TLS server name (SNI) matches its CONNECT authority, which stops an agent from reaching another host on the same CDN through an allowed one. The default proxy runs it in Envoy, and `netagent` runs it at level 1. See "Residual risks and limits", **SNI and domain fronting**. |
| Git path, and T | The handling `netagent` applies to git smart HTTP and LFS requests on a terminated host: classification, path enforcement, minted tokens, ref pattern checks, and push option refusal. T, or "git path T", is the owner's name for the current git configuration (decision 44), in which git keeps its remote URLs and uses the local egress endpoint as `http.proxy`. |
| Terminated marker | A header `netagent` sets on the CONNECT it opens for a terminated connection, and strips from agent input. The decision service refuses a pod's CONNECT to a covered, credential-pinned, or read-only host that lacks it. |
| Per-pod CA and trust anchor | The certificate authority `netagent` generates in each pod to terminate TLS. Its certificate is the trust anchor that clients in the pod load, and its X.509 name constraints, where it carries them, limit the host names it can sign. A chain anchor is a different thing: an event in the §11.7 audit hash chain that holds the digests of evidence batches. |
| Bundles | The pod trust bundle, `/run/netagent/ca-bundle.pem`, holds the chart's public root set, any deployer CA, and the per-pod CA, and every tool in the pod trusts it. The TLS interception bundle, which the configuration calls the CA trust bundle, is the controller's per-deployment or per-pool set of public roots and deployer or tenant CAs from which `netagent` builds the pod trust bundle (see "TLS interception"). The egress CA bundle, the `lenny-egress-ca` ConfigMap, holds only the egress CA, and `netagent` verifies platform hops against it (see "Transport security"). |
| Egress PKI | The certificate authority and leaves this design adds for its platform hops. It is separate from the existing platform mTLS PKI (`mtls-pki.yaml` and `lenny-mtls-ca`), which the chart renders only when `mtls.enabled` is set. See "Transport security". |
| VCS pool | A credential pool whose provider serves git hosts. It names repository prefixes, a repository allowlist, and a provider credential. "VCS pool fields" lists its fields. |
| Provider capability level | What a VCS provider can mint: `mint-scoped`, a short-lived token limited to named repositories; `mint-coarse`, a short-lived or revocable token wider than one repository; or `stored`, a long-lived token the tenant supplies. "A `stored` provider" means a provider at the `stored` level. |
| Mint | A call in which the Token Service obtains a short-lived token from a provider, such as a GitHub App installation token. The mint path is the route those calls take. |
| Readable and writable sets | The repositories a session may fetch and push. Each entry is a record with a `state`, and `granted` marks a writable repository. |
| Warm fill | Work a warm pod does before claim, such as filling a repository store or running `npm ci`, under the pool's warm-up policy. |
| Warm list | The list of repository stores that a pool's pods fill before they report ready, rendered once per pool and runtime revision. It holds the public `gitRepository` entries of the revision's `sharedAssets` and, on a tenant-owned pool, the `gitRepository` assets of the tenant-derived runtime's `sharedAssets`, under one rule set. Each asset's declarer is the role that wrote it, `tenant` for a tenant admin and `platform` for a `platform-admin`, and a `platform`-declared asset keeps the protections an operator's entry has on a tenant-owned pool ([`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Tenant shared assets"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#tenant-shared-assets)). A `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets` is the only way to declare a warm store (decision 67). A pod's warm list is the one for its pool and the revision its template names, and "the warm list" in this document always means that list. [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Warm stores"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#warm-stores) defines its rules, the rewrite of proposal 0111 carries it to every pool, and **Warm list per revision** under [RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods) keys it on pool and revision, because part of it comes from a revision's `sharedAssets`. The stores sit on the pod-wide volume `lenny-git-stores`, which `lenny-store-fill` fills before the pod reports ready (decision 61), which every slot container mounts read-only at `/lenny/git-stores` ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)), and which the adapter mounts read-write at `/adapter/git-stores` only to delete a withheld store. A `gitRepository` entry may carry `prestage: true`, and the pool's pods then stage a checkout of it in each idle slot before the session, which the first `gitClone` source of that repository in the session's workspace adopts at its own `path` (decision 67, which replaces decision 63's `path`). |
| Store fill and `lenny-store-fill` | The fill of the warm list's repository stores, and of each store's pristine tree, before a pod reports ready. It runs in `lenny-store-fill`, an init container of the agent pod that starts after the `netagent` native sidecar, under its own UID, with no credentials and the store volume `lenny-git-stores` as its only read-write mount (decision 61). It writes with the store group, the dedicated store GID of PL-5, which is the primary group of both `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter, as the file group and a umask of `0002`, so directories are `0775` and object files `0444`, and an explicit mode pass after each pristine tree's checkout sets the tree's directories to `0775`, its files to `0444`, and its executables to `0555`, and clears every setuid and setgid bit: every slot reads the stores through the other-class bits, no slot container holds the store group, and the adapter, whose primary group it is, deletes a withheld store through the group class without following a symbolic link ([`agent-pod-layout-design.md`, "PL-5"](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction)). |
| Fetch retry window | A pool setting that bounds how long `netagent` retries the mirror for a repository whose first fetch is still running. |
| Credential pin (`credentialPinned`) and read-only host | Settings on a host set or a binding, from EG-12. On a credential-pinned host, `netagent` terminates TLS and refuses a request when the host has no binding. A read-only host admits only `GET` and `HEAD`. See "Exfiltration controls". |
| Pins | This document uses "pin" in qualified senses: the tenant pin (under "Terms from other designs and the platform"), a credential pin (above), a pinned address, which the decision service returns for a private destination, and a pinned SHA, a full commit SHA a plan names. A pinned idle pod is a recycled pod waiting between sessions, still tied to its tenant. |
| Response scrub | `netagent` removing a bound secret, in each rendered form, from responses on bound hosts, so a host that echoes the value does not hand it to the agent. The pool recycle scrub of §5.2, which cleans a pod between sessions, is a different mechanism. |
| Request rules | Data on a binding with `delivery: inject` that refuses requests before the value is attached. `netagent` applies them in its request inspection stage, and [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) defines them. |
| Preset and flavour | A preset is a named, versioned set of request rules and request forms shipped as data, with paths per API flavour, such as `github-deny`. A flavour names a host's API layout (its API base, REST prefix, and GraphQL paths), which an add-on registers. On a VCS pool's API host the flavour derives from the pool's provider and host. [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) defines both. |
| Evidence stream | A typed, append-only series of records with one common envelope, such as `egress.decision` for each connection decision. A record's `provenance` is `enforced`, `observed`, or `self_reported`. See "Evidence streams". |
| Client proxy profile and launch variables | The environment variables and file templates that point tools in the pod at the local egress endpoint and the bundle. The launch variables are the variables the controller adds to the runtime's launch environment. See "Injected configuration". |
| Network descriptor | `/run/netagent/network.json`, which `netagent` writes for runtimes that configure clients in code. |
| P1 to P7 | This design's security properties, listed under "Security properties". |
| EG-1 to EG-12 | This design's proposals, numbered 1 to 12 under "Proposal split". This document calls proposal n "proposal n" or EG-n, and other designs use EG-n. Proposal 0, the service-mode tenant pin, is withdrawn (decision 62), and its number is not reused. |
| Decision N | A numbered owner decision under "Decisions". |

### Terms from other designs and the platform

This design builds on mechanisms other designs and the specification define. The table gives each one the meaning this document relies on.

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Agent pod, runtime, and adapter | An agent pod runs one runtime, the agent's own program. The adapter speaks to the gateway for it and runs in its own container. Each slot has its own slot container, `runtime-<n>`, which runs the runtime supervisor and, for each session, a fresh runtime process ([`agent-pod-layout-design.md`, "PL-1"](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)). |
| Agent UID and slot UID | The UID the runtime, and every command the agent runs, execute under. Each slot runs under its own UID, the slot UID ([SI-2](session-isolation-design.md#si-2-slot-identity-and-socket-names)), so "the agent UID" of a session means the UID of its slot. |
| Slot | One of the places on an agent pod where a session runs, with its own container, UID, and volumes. A pool's `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, 1 by default, sets the number of slots ([`session-isolation-design.md`, "SI-1"](session-isolation-design.md#si-1-pod-layout)). |
| `lenny-materialize` and the pinned git | The Lenny Go helper that the slot's supervisor worker executes as the slot UID to write a session's workspace plan and to run every git step on a slot repository, and the static `git` that a Lenny release builds from a pinned upstream version. Both are staged with mode `0111` in `/lenny/bin` ([`workspace-materialization-design.md`, "WM-10"](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)). |
| Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild | Owner decisions of 2026-11-12 that [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) owns. The warm-list entries that carry `prestage: true` are checked out by `lenny-materialize` at warm-up and after every slot release into `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/<entry>`, and at bind the first `gitClone` source of the session's workspace that the entry's store serves adopts the staged tree by rename to the source's `path`, with an in-place checkout when its commit moved. Where the filesystem supports reflinks, the helper builds each staged tree from one pristine checked-out tree per store on the store volume by cloning file content, and elsewhere it runs a normal checkout. |
| Gateway | Lenny's API gateway. Every client interaction goes through it, and it binds sessions to pods. |
| Controller | `lenny-controller`, which creates agent pods for pools and renders their spec. |
| Supervisor | The runtime supervisor, the Lenny process that is the first process of every runtime container. It starts each runtime process, building the runtime's environment at launch from an empty environment (§4.7.10), and its forked worker runs setup commands and `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID. |
| Token Service | The Lenny service that stores provider credentials and mints short-lived tokens from them ([§4.3](../spec/04_system-components.md#43-token-service)). Under this design it also answers the pod credential API. |
| Pool and Runtime | A pool keeps warm agent pods for a Runtime, the registered definition of a runtime image and its settings ([§5.2](../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#52-pool-configuration-and-execution-modes), which [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) O7 renames "Pool Configuration" through the anchor-redirect map). |
| Claim, bind, and tenant pin | A claim reserves a warm pod for a session, and bind attaches the session to that pod. The tenant pin is the `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label that ties a pod to one tenant. |
| Pod models | Every runtime process launches at bind, after materialization and setup, and a warm pod is one whose infrastructure is ready ([§6.1](../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#61-what-a-pre-warmed-pod-looks-like)). Lenny runs agent runtimes in session mode alone, because [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) removes `type: mcp` runtimes and service mode. A recycled pod serves sessions one after another, with a slot release between them that ends the slot's runtime process, and waits between them as a pinned idle pod ([SI-3](session-isolation-design.md#si-3-runtime-processes-slot-release-and-setup-commands)). A pool with `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` above 1 runs concurrent slots: each session runs in its own slot container under its own UID, and the slots share the pod's network namespace, adapter, and `netagent` ([SI-10](session-isolation-design.md#si-10-what-stays-shared)). See "Pod models". |
| Tenant-owned pool | A pool with `ownerTenantId`, whose every pod belongs to one tenant from creation. |
| Proposal 0111 | A draft change proposal for warm workspaces, on the branch `draft/0111-warm-workspace`, which has not landed. It defines the warm fill, the `gitRepository` shared asset, private repository stores, and `ownerTenantId`. "Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings" states how this design rewrites it, and the rewrite defines the warm list (decision 45). Its terms that this document uses: `FetchGitPack`, an RPC through which a pod fetched a git pack from the gateway; the slot repository, the git repository `lenny-materialize` builds in a session's workspace, which borrows objects from the pod's repository store through git alternates and records `lenny.gitStoreUrl` and `lenny.storeCommit` in its configuration; its rule 2, tree validation and the materialization bound; the capture copy, which copies into a slot repository the objects it holds only through alternates before its workspace is captured; restore completion, which completes each such repository after a workspace is restored; and the §7.1 **Bind bounds.** paragraph. |
| Credential pool | A tenant object that holds credentials for a provider. Since proposal 0112, each credential's Secret lives in the `lenny-credentials` namespace. |
| Session store and pooler | The Postgres tables that hold sessions and their pod assignments. Components read them through the transaction-mode connection pooler (§12), which offers no `LISTEN` or `NOTIFY`. |
| Archive gate | The gateway's check of a git tree it packed into an archive, before it streams the files into the pod (§7.1 step 12 and §13.4). This design removes it for git, as "Residual risks and limits", **Tree validation**, states, and [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway) moves the extraction of every uploaded archive out of the gateway into `lenny-materialize`. |
| Delegation lease | The record that governs a delegated child session and states what the child receives from its parent (§8). |
| Suspension that cannot take hold, and `UnsealSession` | A suspension whose runtime cannot stop at a safe point. Step 1 of [`session-lifecycle-design.md`, "SL-3 Suspension and the open turn"](session-lifecycle-design.md#sl-3-suspension-and-the-open-turn) then refuses and cancels the session's gateway-side calls and halts the slot's egress before the pod is released. `UnsealSession` is the gateway RPC that lifts a session's seal on its pod when the session resumes there. |
| Workspace plan and `gitClone` | A session's workspace plan lists what to place in the pod before the runtime starts, such as a `gitClone` source naming a repository, a ref, and a lease scope (§14). Materialization (`FinalizeWorkspace`) places them. |
| `deliveryMode` | Today, a pool's LLM delivery: under `proxy` the gateway calls the LLM provider, and under `direct` the pod receives a provider lease and calls the provider itself. [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`, "LU-4"](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#lu-4-removal) removes the setting and direct mode, so the gateway's LLM proxy holds every LLM upstream's credential and the runtime holds only the session's proxy token. |
| Runtime capabilities | The behaviours a runtime declares on its Runtime definition and offers in its handshake with the adapter, such as `interrupt`, `checkpoint`, and `midSessionUpload`. They replace the integration levels Basic, Standard, and Full of [§15.4.3](../spec/15_external-api-surface.md#1543-runtime-integration-levels) ([`runtime-protocol-design.md`, "RP-2"](runtime-protocol-design.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies)). |
| `userCredential` binding and token trust record | A credential binding whose `secret` names a tenant-defined user credential type, so each session receives its own user's token. A type's source is `registered`, whose tokens a tenant backend or the user writes to Lenny, or `oauth2Link`, whose tokens come from a linked account, and its type hosts are the hosts it declares or the provider's API hosts. The token trust record holds everything that decides where a stored user token can go. A link provider is a tenant's OAuth2 client at a provider. [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) defines all three. |
| VCS layer settings | Fields [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) adds to a VCS pool. `userAuthorization: required` makes each repository need the user's own access at the provider, `prAuthoring: user` has pull requests authored as the user, and `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials` admits a binding with `delivery: file` on the pool's covered hosts. |
| Entry states and session fields from other designs | A writable entry's state `pendingApproval`, from [SO-5b](security-oversight-design.md#so-5b-repository-write-approvals), admits no push until approved. `userRefused`, from [UG-2](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-2-repository-authorization), marks a repository the user's own access at the provider no longer covers, and it denies reads and writes. `retireOnRelease` is a mark on a pod claim under which every release path retires the pod ([UC-3](user-credential-delivery-design.md#uc-3-the-usercredential-binding)). A session's caller type (`caller_type`) says whether its request principal is a human, and a user's invalidation level, from [TS-4](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#identity-model-ts-2-ts-4-and-ts-6-to-ts-9), records that the user's sessions and tokens must stop. |
| Guardrail, posture, and `suspendedBy` | A guardrail is a setting a `security-admin` writes in the tenant security record that limits what a tenant admin can configure, and posture is that record's report of a tenant's security-relevant settings ([`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md)). `suspendedBy` is a field on every opt-in object, and every component treats an object with it set as absent. |
| Environment | A scope inside a tenant, from [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md). A tenant keeps per-environment policies in `environmentPolicies`, and `crossedFromEnvironment` marks a session reached through a declared sharing path from another environment. |
| Outbound connector | A customer-run agent that dials out to the platform, so traffic reaches the customer's private network with no inbound firewall rule ([`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md)). |
| Derived runtime and template pool | A tenant's runtime derived from a platform runtime under a platform grant, with owned pools copied from a template pool ([`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md)). |
| `PreRoute` chain, post-chain point, and last modifier | At session creation the gateway runs its `PreRoute` interceptors once, in priority order, and a built-in route interceptor or another interceptor can change the session's runtime and pool. The post-chain point is the step after that chain and after revision selection, and before the pod claim, at which every check that depends on the runtime, the revision, or the pool runs, on the final runtime and pool. When the chain changed either and a check fails, the refusal names the last modifier, the interceptor that last changed the payload, in `details.interceptor_ref` ([`runtime-routing-design.md`, "RT-2 The interceptor contract"](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-2-the-interceptor-contract)). |
| Runtime revision and pod set | A revision is an immutable, numbered copy of a runtime's versioned fields, such as its image and `sharedAssets`. A session runs on one revision, which the gateway chooses after the `PreRoute` chain. A pod set is the warm pods of one pool built from one revision, so a pool holds one pod set per active revision during a rollout ([`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md), RV-2 and RV-3). |
| Quarantine | A pod state from [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md), set by a `lenny.dev/quarantine` label or a `pod_quarantines` row, under which the pod gets no egress, mints, or mirror reads. |
| R14 | The remediation step that gives agent pods an mTLS client identity and a per-pod serving certificate ([`gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md`, "R14. Agent-pod mTLS client identity"](../gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md#r14-agent-pod-mtls-client-identity)). It sits outside this design. |
| F-n.n.n and T-n.n.n | An F-n.n.n identifier is a finding filed in [`BUILD-GAPS.md`](../BUILD-GAPS.md) at the repository root, named for the specification section it concerns. A T-n.n.n identifier is a test coverage finding in [`TEST-GAPS.md`](../TEST-GAPS.md). |
| NET-nnn | A network rule identifier in the specification's network isolation section ([§13.2](../spec/13_security-model.md#132-network-isolation)). |
| `LNK-` and `CH-` identifiers | The canonical names of links and channels in the specification's communication channels section (§28), such as `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` and `CH-LLMPROXY`. |
| Tenant security settings | The operator-set controls on a tenant that replace `workspaceTier: T4`, among them `encryption.tenantKey`, the tenant's own KMS key, and `placement.nodeTenancy`, which puts the tenant's pods on nodes dedicated to it ([`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md)). |
| Data classes | Public, Internal, Confidential, and Secret, the classes of §12.9 under their new names, formerly T1 to T4 ([`tenant-security-settings-design.md`, "TSS-8"](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-8-data-classes)). |
| Node class, offering, and node tenancy | A node class is a set of nodes that one or more members (Lenny's system components, or the agent pods of one isolation profile) share. A placement offering is a platform-defined `nodePlacement` a pool references. Node tenancy decides whether a pod shares nodes with other tenants' pods ([`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md)). |
| `complianceProfile` | A tenant setting. Its regulated values are `soc2`, `fedramp`, and `hipaa` (§11). A follow-up design replaces it with deployment-level settings and per-tenant settings under TSS O9 (b), and this design keeps it unchanged until then. |

### Abbreviations

| Abbreviation | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| ARN | Amazon Resource Name |
| CA | Certificate authority |
| CEL | Common Expression Language, which Envoy's RBAC filter evaluates |
| CNI | Container Network Interface, the Kubernetes network plugin that enforces NetworkPolicy |
| EDL | External Dynamic List, a plain-text list of domains that firewalls fetch over HTTPS |
| ext_authz | Envoy's external authorization filter and its wire protocol |
| GHES | GitHub Enterprise Server |
| HBONE | Istio's tunnelling protocol, which carries traffic in HTTP/2 CONNECT |
| IMDS | A cloud's instance metadata service, such as `169.254.169.254` |
| JWKS | JSON Web Key Set, the public keys an OIDC issuer publishes |
| JWT | JSON Web Token |
| KMS | Key management service |
| LFS | Git Large File Storage, served through a host's batch API and transfer URLs |
| mTLS | Mutual TLS, in which the client also presents a certificate |
| NPEP-133 | A Kubernetes network policy enhancement proposal that selects destinations by domain name |
| OCSF | Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework, a common format for security events |
| OIDC | OpenID Connect |
| PAC | Proxy auto-config, a file that tells a browser which proxy to use |
| PKI | Public key infrastructure: a CA and the certificates it issues |
| PKIX | X.509 certificate path validation, as Java implements it |
| SAN | Subject alternative name, the name a TLS certificate is valid for |
| SIEM | Security information and event management system |
| SNI | Server Name Indication, the host name a TLS ClientHello names |
| SDS | Envoy's secret discovery service, through which it loads and reloads certificates |
| SSE | Server-sent events |
| SSRF | Server-side request forgery: a request steered at an internal address |
| STS | A cloud security token service, such as AWS STS |
| ULA | An IPv6 unique local address (`fc00::/7`) |
| WPAD | Web Proxy Auto-Discovery |
| xDS | Envoy's discovery service APIs, through which a control plane changes its configuration at run time |

### Labels from other designs

Proposals, properties, and spikes in other designs carry a label whose prefix names the design. This document cites them by label and links each citation to its definition. The table names what each cited label stands for.

| Labels | Design | What they name |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| LU-1, LU-4, and LUR-11 | [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#lu-1-llm-upstreams) | LU-1 the LLM upstream records that replace LLM credential pools, LU-4 the removal of LLM credential pools, leases, and direct delivery mode, and LUR-11 the rule that a tenant egress route never claims the endpoint host of an upstream record. |
| SO-1, SO-2, SO-3, SO-5a, SO-5b, SO-6, and SO-7 | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) | SO-1 security roles, including `security-auditor`; SO-2 the tenant security record, which holds a tenant's guardrails, and posture; SO-3 a per-tenant SIEM destination; SO-5a tool-use approvals; SO-5b repository write approvals; SO-6 the fix for F-25.6.9, the `lenny-ctl doctor` certificate remedy; and SO-7 the fix for F-25.4.40, the rights of `lenny-ops`. |
| FE-1, FE-2, FE-3, FE-4, and FE-6 | [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md) | FE-1 quarantine, FE-2 the mirror store hold, FE-3 platform-only holds, FE-4 the evidence reconciler, and FE-6 agent activity streams. |
| TS-1, TS-3, TS-4, and TS-6 | [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) | TS-1 environment scoping, TS-3 the session environment context and cross-environment sharing, TS-4 user lifecycle and invalidation, and TS-6 identity source records and browser login. |
| OC-1, OC-2, and OC-3 | [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`, "Proposals"](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md#proposals) | The outbound connector's gateway, agent, and identity (OC-1), its registration, hosts, and policy (OC-2), and its operations, including the liveness probe (OC-3). The connector's in-cluster gateway is called the outbound gateway in this document, to keep it apart from Lenny's API gateway. |
| DR-1 to DR-5, DR-9, and DR-11 | [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#dr-1-to-dr-3-prerequisites) | DR-1 to DR-3 are prerequisites of derived runtimes: DR-1 makes platform runtimes and pools unwritable by tenants, DR-2 gives a derived runtime its own pods through `podRuntimeRef`, and DR-3 runs setup commands at the slot UID. [DR-4](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#dr-4-tenant-runtime-records) defines tenant runtime records, [DR-5](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#dr-5-owned-pools) owned pools, [DR-9](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#dr-9-default-git-clones) default git clones, and DR-11 shared assets and warm stores ("Tenant shared assets" and "Warm stores" under DR-9). |
| UC-1 to UC-7 | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) | Proposals of the user credential design: UC-1 credential types and the `registered` source, UC-2 the `oauth2Link` source and account linking, UC-3 the `userCredential` binding, UC-4 request rules and presets, UC-5 `delivery: file` for a user's token, UC-6 leak scanning, and UC-7 add-ons. |
| UG-1, UG-2, UG-3, and UG-4 | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) | Proposals of the VCS layer: UG-1 VCS link kinds, UG-2 repository authorization, UG-3 pull request authoring, and UG-4 the independent approval check. |
| GL-1, GL-2, S13, and S17 | [`gitlab-credential-addon-design.md`](gitlab-credential-addon-design.md) | GL-1 the GitLab kind and GL-2 user-authored merge requests. S13 and S17 are GitLab spikes that design owes. |
| RT-2 | [`runtime-routing-design.md`, "RT-2 The interceptor contract"](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-2-the-interceptor-contract) | The `PreRoute` payload, the single chain, and the post-chain point, with the checks each owning design runs there. |
| RV-2 and RV-3 | [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-2-selection) | RV-2 revision selection, which runs after the `PreRoute` chain and before the post-chain point. RV-3 pod sets per pool and revision, including the warm list per revision. |
| SD-1 | [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md#sd-1-one-route) | The derive route and its shared create path, whose plan check DR-9 reuses. |
| SI-1, SI-2, SI-3, SI-7, SI-10, SI D9, SI O9, and SI O11 | [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md#si-1-pod-layout) | SI-1 the pod layout with a container and UID per slot, SI-2 slot identity, SI-3 the slot release, SI-7 credentials and the modes of shared volumes, and SI-10 what slots still share, the network namespace among them. D9 is the file-descriptor broker, under which every entry of a slot's workspace belongs to the slot UID. O9 asks how `netagent` attributes a connection to a slot, which this design answers under "Slot attribution and fetch grants". O11, settled on 2026-11-12, runs every git step on a slot repository as the slot UID. |
| PL-1, PL-2, and PL-3 | [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint) | The pod blueprint, the removal of the embedded deployment model, and the runtime-visible paths: `/workspace`, `/session`, `/tmp`, `/lenny/assets`, `/lenny/git-stores`, `/lenny/bin`, and `/run/lenny`. |
| WM-7, WM-9, and WM-10 | [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git) | WM-7 ordering and collisions, WM-9 archive extraction in `lenny-materialize` with the rule that replaces §13.4's archive sentence, and WM-10 every git step on a slot repository in the helper, with the fetch grant this design carries. |
| IC-1, IC-5, and IC D6 | [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#ic-1-capture-in-passes) | Capture through the slot's broker, including the capture copy of a slot repository, and restore. D6 turns off automatic git maintenance in the rendered configuration and lets a runtime override it at its own checkpoint cost. |
| RP-2 and RP-B | [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies) | The runtime capabilities that replace the integration levels, and the proposal that removes the platform MCP socket. |
| PM-1, PM-4, PM-5, and PM-8 | [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md#pm-1-node-classes-and-presets) | Node classes and presets, placement requirements, rendering with `componentPlacement`, and the placement, replicas, and disruption budgets of system components. |
| TSS-6 and TSS-8 | [`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-6-placement-settings) | The tenant's placement settings, `placement.nodeTenancy` among them, and the data classes. |
| AR-2, AR-B, ARR-8, and AR O7 | [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) | AR-2 the removal of service mode, AR-B the proposal that removes it and withdraws this design's proposal 0, ARR-8 the name-constrained trust anchor on every agent pod, and O7 the renames of §5.2 to "Pool Configuration" and of §4.7.9 to "Runtime Startup Sequence". The owner settled its open decisions on 2026-11-13. |
| CR-1 to CR-4, and O5, O7, O8, and O9 | [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) | CR-1 the removal of connectors, which leaves §9.3 as a successor pointer; CR-2 the identity assertion; CR-3 the guidance for reaching operator services through egress; and CR-4 the egress halt at suspension. O5 lets an `identityAssertion` binding share a host with one other `inject` binding, O7 refuses one on a pool without slot attribution, O8 keeps egress traffic out of session activity, and O9 halts the slot's egress at a suspension that cannot take hold. |
| SL-3, SL-4, and SL-6 | [`session-lifecycle-design.md`](session-lifecycle-design.md#sl-3-suspension-and-the-open-turn) | SL-3 suspension and the open turn, whose step 1 runs when a suspension cannot take hold; SL-4 tree suspension; and SL-6 idle pods and dormancy, which states what counts as session activity. |
| S19 | [`github-credential-addon-design.md`, "Validation owed"](github-credential-addon-design.md#validation-owed) | The GitHub add-on's run of the request inspection stage's attack table and bypass analysis on a stub. |

## High-level design

An agent in a Lenny pod runs tools that need the network, such as package managers, git, API clients, and LLM SDKs. Without a control on that traffic, any of them can reach any host, including the cluster's internal services, and a credential the agent can read can be copied anywhere. This design gives each pod one controlled way out. A small proxy inside the pod, `netagent`, takes every outbound connection and passes it to a central proxy, which asks a separate decision service whether this pod, for its tenant and session, may reach that host. By default the agent never holds a credential: `netagent` obtains short-lived tokens from Lenny's Token Service and adds them to requests on their way out, so the agent sees only a placeholder. Git works for the agent as it does anywhere else, and `netagent` checks which repositories and branches a session may push to. Every connection decision is recorded for the tenant and the operator.

The main limit is that filtering works on host names. An allowed host that accepts uploads can still carry data out, a pool that grants raw internet access bypasses the proxy, and a deployer who replaces the central proxy with one that cannot call the decision service gets weaker guarantees. "Residual risks and limits" states each limit.

The rest of this section states each part in the design's terms, which "Terms" above defines. The diagram under "Architecture" shows the components.

The design has a generic egress part and a git part. Both live in this document, and the table at the end of this section maps each to its sections and proposals.

**Egress (EG-2, EG-9, and EG-11).** Every pod that uses the local endpoint runs `netagent`, a Lenny container under its own UID that holds the pod's identity token. Tools in the pod reach the network through `netagent`'s local endpoint, which they use as their HTTP proxy. `netagent` forwards each connection to `lenny-egress`, an Envoy proxy that asks `lenny-egress-authz` for a decision on each CONNECT. The decision service identifies the pod from its service account token, reads its binding (pool, tenant, and sessions), and applies the effective policy: the pool's egress ceiling intersected with the tenant's and the session's allowed hosts, less the tenant's denied hosts. A pool's `egressProfile` selects its network posture: `restricted` has no general egress, `proxied` reaches the network only through `lenny-egress`, and `internet` keeps raw sockets for unfiltered egress and non-HTTP protocols.

**Credentials for APIs (EG-4).** A tenant admin attaches a credential binding that names a secret, the hosts it may reach, and a delivery. Under `delivery: inject`, the default, `netagent` attaches the value to requests for those hosts and the runtime sees only a placeholder, and the binding can carry request rules that refuse a request before the value is attached. `delivery: federation`, `delivery: metadata`, and `delivery: file` place a credential within the agent's reach, each behind a platform grant and a tenant opt-in. The Token Service mints short-lived tokens for bindings whose secret is a minting provider, and for git.

**Routing and replacement (EG-6 and EG-7).** A tenant can chain destinations to its own upstream proxies, leave from a dedicated address through an egress group, register private endpoints, and reach a private network through a tenant outbound connector. A deployer can replace `lenny-egress` with its own proxy: at level 2 a proxy that calls the decision service, and at level 1 a proxy that enforces a host list Lenny publishes.

**Evidence and exfiltration (EG-8, EG-10, and EG-12).** Each decision, tunnel, local request, mint, and git operation produces an evidence record for the tenant admin, the customer's security team, and the operator's forensics team. Hostname filtering leaves opaque tunnels, and EG-12's credential pins and read-only hosts narrow what an allowed host can carry out.

**Git (EG-1, EG-3, and EG-5).** Git keeps its remote URLs and uses `netagent`'s endpoint as `http.proxy`. `netagent` terminates TLS for VCS-covered hosts, attaches a token minted per operation, and enforces repository paths, ref patterns, and push options. A VCS pool names repository prefixes and a provider credential, and the provider's capability level (`mint-scoped`, `mint-coarse`, or `stored`) decides what Lenny mints and allows. A write token is minted only for a repository the session declared writable and the pool allows. `lenny-git-mirror` serves fetches from its stores and resolves refs through an upstream outage within an age limit. The gateway and the adapter run no git on a session's repositories: `lenny-materialize` clones in the slot as the slot UID with a pinned git, and the gateway resolves refs with a bounded `ls-refs` call through `lenny-egress`.

| Part | Sections | Proposals |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Generic egress | "Architecture", "Credential delivery to runtimes", "Interfaces", "Configuration by scope", "Effective policy", "Injected configuration", "Runtime SDKs", "Pod models", "Network policy and placement", "Defaults, replacement, and augmentation", and "TLS interception" | EG-2, EG-4, EG-6, EG-7, EG-8, EG-9, EG-10, EG-11, and EG-12 |
| Git | "Git", with "Git on covered hosts (`netagent`)", "Git mirror (`lenny-git-mirror`)", "Credentials", "Git configuration", and "VCS pool fields" | EG-1, EG-3, and EG-5 |

Other designs build on both parts. [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) builds on the credential bindings, and [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md), [`github-credential-addon-design.md`](github-credential-addon-design.md), and [`gitlab-credential-addon-design.md`](gitlab-credential-addon-design.md) build on the git part.

## Security properties

The design holds these properties. Each later section states which component enforces them.

- P1. A pod reaches only the destinations its effective policy names.
- P2. Long-lived credentials never enter a pod, with these bounded exceptions: `netagent` memory holds a `stored` provider's read token for a fetch and, under the `storedTokenPush` opt-in, for a push (see "Credentials"); `netagent` memory holds a stored API secret or a user's token for a binding with `delivery: inject`, which never names a git host a VCS pool covers when the value is stored; the agent-readable deliveries place a credential in the agent's reach under their opt-in, including a user's token under `userCredentialDelivery` (see "Credential delivery to runtimes" and [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md)); under level 1 replacement, `netagent` memory holds the identity's rotating proxy credential (see "Level 1 replacement"); and under the same gate as stored-token push, `netagent` memory holds a reused GitLab token (see **Push limits and commit identity**). Each opt-in sits behind its own gate (see "Credential delivery to runtimes", **Gates**), except a user's token under `delivery: inject`, which the owner's decision leaves to the tenant's binding write, because no process in the runtime or adapter container can read it.
- P3. Egress and VCS credentials are never readable by the agent UID. The exceptions are the credential binding deliveries that place a credential in the agent's reach by design: `file`, `federation`, and `metadata` (see "Credential delivery to runtimes"). A `userCredential` binding under `delivery: file` is such a delivery of a person's token, with limits [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) states. Each needs its class's gate and a tenant opt-in (see "Credential delivery to runtimes", **Gates**). Provider keys registered on LLM upstream records reach a provider only from the gateway, because LU-4 removes direct delivery mode. A provider key that a tenant places in an `inject` binding is an ordinary binding credential, with no LLM-specific grant.
- P4. One tenant's data and credentials never reach another tenant's pods.
- P5. Every allowed or denied egress connection is attributable to a pod, a pool, and a tenant once the pod is bound to one, and to a session where the pod model allows it.
- P6. Failure of any egress component blocks traffic.
- P7. A pod's load cannot remove another tenant's egress. Quotas per pod and per tenant bound tunnels, connection rate, and decision calls before any shared limit is reached.

These statements describe the default proxy and level 2 replacement. A deployment that selects level 1 replacement holds P1, P5, and P7 in a weaker form, which "Level 1 replacement" states.

## Architecture

```
  agent pod (one network namespace shared by every slot; NetworkPolicy applies to the whole pod)
 +------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | slot container (slot UID)      adapter container      netagent container    |
 |  HTTPS_PROXY=127.0.0.1:3128 ------------------------> local egress endpoint -+--> lenny-egress (central proxy)
 |  git, http.proxy=:3128      ------------------------>  (terminates covered  --+--> git endpoint (lenny-git-mirror,
 |                                                         hosts)              |      deployer service, or upstream
 |                                                         | projected SA token  |      through lenny-egress)
 +---------------------------------------------------------+--------------------+
                                                           | binding API (I7)    | credential API (I8)
                                                           v                     v
                                                   lenny-egress-authz <--scope-- Token Service (minting)
 lenny-egress --(ext_authz, per CONNECT)--> lenny-egress-authz
 lenny-egress --> internet, or --> upstream proxy chosen per tenant (forwarded CONNECT)
```

The design separates these responsibilities:

- **Network enforcement** happens in `lenny-egress` and the git endpoint. The pod's NetworkPolicy allows only those services, `lenny-egress-authz` on the pod binding API (I7), the Token Service on the pod credential API (I8), the `proxied` DNS resolver, and the existing base destinations, each on its port. The runtime can reach those services directly, so the operative control is that every one of them acts only on a request that carries the pod's service account token, which only `netagent` holds. The NetworkPolicy bounds what the pod can address, and the token bounds what it can obtain.
- **Decisions** happen in `lenny-egress-authz`. It identifies the pod from its service account token and looks up the pod's current binding (pool, tenant, and sessions) from the records the controller and gateway already write.
- **Identity, credential use, and routing inside the pod** happen in `netagent`. This is a new Lenny container in every pod that uses the local endpoint, running under its own UID. It holds the pod's identity token and any upstream token, and it attaches them on the hops that leave the pod. It also enforces which repositories a credential may be used for, because it is the only component that sees both the plain git request and the credential.

### The `netagent` container

The local egress endpoint, and the git path on it, run in a dedicated container rather than in the existing adapter.

|                       | In the existing adapter                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | In a separate `netagent` container                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| :-------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Identity before claim | The adapter already mounts a projected service account token with the deployment's gateway audience (`podspec/sa_token.go`, 900 s), so an identity exists before claim in both designs. That token is the adapter's gateway identity, so the egress identity would share a container with it. | The container mounts its own projected tokens at pod creation, with audiences the agent's container never holds. The identity exists from the first second of the pod's life, independent of claim timing and pod model, and it is distinct from the adapter's. |
| New channels          | A lease handoff inside the adapter, plus a new delivery RPC timed before setup.                                                                                                                                                            | No intra-pod channel. `netagent` talks only to `lenny-egress-authz`, the Token Service, `lenny-egress`, and the git endpoint.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Failure isolation     | A crash in proxy code takes down the adapter and the session.                                                                                                                                                                              | A crash restarts `netagent` only. Egress fails closed while it restarts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Cost                  | No extra container.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | One extra container per pod, including warm pods. It is a small Go process (a prototype on the git path peaked at 29 MB `memory.peak` over 64 MiB clones and a push, and at 41.7 MB under the request rules' earlier bounds). A pool's resource class states the whole pod's total, `netagent` included ([`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md) decision 7): `netagent` takes its own request from a chart value, and the class mapping subtracts it from the class before it divides the rest among the adapter and the slot containers. Today `applyResources` (`pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/resources.go`) copies the class onto every container, and the test-only egress-capture sidecar sets no request and falls back to the namespace LimitRange. |
| Specification         | No new container rules.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | A third reserved UID and a §13.1 container-identity row. The container does not mount `/run/lenny`, so the `lenny-cred-readers` boundary is unchanged.                                                                                                                                                    |

Recommendation: the separate container. The egress identity and every upstream token stay in a container that neither a slot UID nor the adapter can read, and a crash in proxy code leaves the adapter and its sessions running. Every agent pod has the adapter in its own container and one slot container per slot ([PL-1](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)), so the earlier argument from embedded runtimes, which ran inside the adapter at the agent UID, has no case left after the embedded deployment model's removal ([PL-2](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model)).

**Identity.** The pod spec mounts a projected volume with two service account tokens into `netagent` only: audience `lenny-egress-<cluster-name>`, presented on the proxy hop, the egress decision API, the git endpoint, and the pod binding API, and audience `lenny-token-service-<cluster-name>`, which `netagent` presents to the Token Service directly on the pod credential API (see "Egress decision service", **Credential requests**). The runtime and adapter containers do not mount either. The adapter keeps its existing gateway-audience token, and every validator rejects a token whose audience is not its own, so the gateway-audience token buys nothing on the egress hops. The audiences carry the cluster name because §10.3 requires deployment-specific audiences, so a token from another Lenny deployment on the same cluster fails. Kubernetes binds each token to the pod (the JWT carries the pod name and UID) and rotates it; the design sets `expirationSeconds` to 600. `lenny-egress-authz` verifies the signature offline through the cluster's OIDC discovery keys, checks the pod UID against its watch of live pods, and requires the connection's peer address to be one of the pod's addresses (`status.podIPs`, which covers dual-stack) from the same watch, so a copied token is useless from any other source. The check assumes the pod-to-Service path is not source-translated; preflight verifies that kube-proxy does not masquerade pod sources (`masqueradeAll` and IPVS configurations), and the check is disabled with a warning where it would reject every pod. The mirror and the Token Service apply the same peer check. Server TLS protects the token in transit on these hops (see "Transport security"). R14 (agent-pod mTLS) does not change this model: its certificate lives in the adapter container and identifies the adapter, so it does not serve as the egress identity.

Round 3 validated this on kind (Kubernetes 1.35):

- The runtime and adapter containers have no path to the token. §13.1 forbids `shareProcessNamespace` on every Lenny pod and the `lenny-pod-security` webhook rejects it, so the spike's shared-namespace run was a control for a misconfigured cluster: even then, `/proc/<pid>/root`, `environ`, and `fd` of `netagent` return permission denied. The protection rests on the separate mount and the distinct UID: a container at `netagent`'s UID read the token. `netagent`'s command line and mount table stay visible, so `netagent` takes no secret as an argument.
- A stdlib Go validator verified signature, issuer, audience, expiry, and the pod, node, and service account claims, and rejected a wrong audience, a token without a pod binding, a tampered payload, and an expired token. The default `system:service-account-issuer-discovery` binding was enough.
- After the pod was deleted, the validator rejected its unexpired token 55 to 72 ms after the watch delivered the deletion. The API server's TokenReview kept accepting the token for about 6 seconds after the pod was gone, so the offline check is faster than TokenReview.
- The kubelet rewrote the token at 80 to 88 percent of its 600-second lifetime with an atomic swap, and the lifetime was not extended.
- Anyone allowed to `create serviceaccounts/token` on the agent service account can mint a pod-bound token that the validator accepts, and anyone with `pods/ephemeralcontainers` or node access can read the mounted one. The chart grants none of these to a Lenny component, preflight gains a check that no role bound in the agent namespaces holds them, and the peer-address check above limits what a copied token can do.
- The protection is the mount boundary, not file modes. At round 3 the pod's `fsGroup` (the `lenny-cred-readers` GID, `base_pod.go`) made every volume group-readable, and the agent UID was in that group, so any volume mounted into both containers was readable by the agent. Under [PLR-10](agent-pod-layout-design.md#requirements) an agent pod sets no `fsGroup` and no pod-level `supplementalGroups`, apart from the Kata `block-plain` exception that pool type carries, and the rule below does not rely on either. `netagent`'s token volume and its private emptyDir (CA key and state) are mounted into `netagent` only, and the `lenny-pod-security` and `lenny-ephemeral-container-cred-guard` webhooks extend their `/run/lenny` rules to those two volumes, so no other container or ephemeral container can mount them. Inside its private emptyDir, `netagent` creates its directories with mode 0700 and a 077 umask.

`netagent` runs as a native sidecar (an init container with `restartPolicy: Always`), so it starts before the slot containers and the adapter and stops after them. Round 3 confirmed that ordering, and that a `netagent` restart leaves the runtime and adapter running. `netagent` writes `/run/netagent`, which every slot container, the adapter, and `lenny-store-fill` mount read-only and no other container mounts, as [PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout) and [PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction) render it, with the modes [SI-7](session-isolation-design.md#si-7-credentials-manifests-and-other-shared-files) states for shared files: directories `0755` and files `0444`, so every slot UID reads the bundle, the git configuration, and the network descriptor and none can change them. Its startup probe calls a readiness endpoint rather than testing for a file, because after a restart a file check passed on the previous instance's file. `netagent`'s private emptyDir, its pod-wide volume at `/run/netagent`, and each `slot-<n>-netcred` are memory-backed emptyDirs with a `sizeLimit` each: 16 MiB for each of the first two (decision 66), and 8 MiB for each `slot-<n>-netcred`, which PL-5 sets: the first holds the CA key and `netagent`'s state, the second the bundle, the git configuration, the descriptor, and the tenant's client config files, and each slot's volume one session's binding list, git configuration file, `netrc` file, and credential directories. Their pages count against `netagent`'s memory, within the 512 MiB memory limit that `netagent.resources` sets by default (decision 66).

**Binding.** A pod's binding is the pool, tenant, and sessions it serves at a moment. `lenny-egress-authz` resolves the pod UID to its Sandbox and pool, reads the tenant pin (`lenny.dev/tenant-id`) and any tenant ownership of the pool, and reads the pod's current session assignments from the session store. The feed from the gateway is explicit:

- The session store is the source of truth, read through the §12 transaction-mode pooler. Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` is unavailable through that pooler (§12.6), and the pod registry polls for the same reason, so the design does not use it.
- The gateway pushes every binding change (bind, unbind, reclaim, and recycle) to every `lenny-egress-authz` replica, over a gRPC call on the gateway's egress PKI leaf to each endpoint of a headless Service, after the store write commits. Envoy spreads a pod's decisions across replicas and `netagent` subscribes to one of them, so a push to one replica would leave the others serving the old binding until their re-read. The push is a hint: a replica never updates its cache from the payload. It re-reads the session row and acts on what the store says, so a forged or replayed push changes nothing, and only the gateway identity is accepted on that call. The push never blocks the commit; the gateway retries each endpoint for a bounded time and then relies on the re-read.
- A decision for a pod whose binding is not in the cache reads through to the store before it answers, and every cached binding is re-read at a bounded age (30 seconds by default) whether or not a push arrived. A lost push therefore costs at most that bound, and the normal bound between a bind commit and the decision service seeing it is one gRPC round trip.
- Each binding carries the §7.3 coordination generation, and the decision service keeps the highest coordination generation it has seen per session. A pod presenting a lower coordination generation is refused, which closes replay of an old binding.
- A session resumed onto a new pod gets a provisional binding, so its clone and setup commands can run before the session is running again, while write tokens and agent-readable credentials wait until it is. A pod's session-scoped requests are honoured only when the session's current binding names that pod. When a session resumes on a replacement pod (§7.3), the gateway's resume driver publishes no binding for that pod until the commit that moves the session from `resuming` to `running`, yet `FinalizeWorkspace`, the materialization step that now runs the clone in the pod, precedes that commit. The resume driver therefore writes a provisional binding (pod, session, and coordination generation, with state `materializing`) before `FinalizeWorkspace`, and the commit promotes it.
