A connector does add Envoy configuration: TLS to the outbound gateway needs one `ORIGINAL_DST` cluster with an upstream TLS context, and one route per tunnel lifetime entry, so EG-6 renders the forwarded route from a template that takes a cluster and an optional upstream TLS context, and [OC-1](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md#proposals), the connector proposal, adds a value. The forwarded route has no server-name check there.

- **Route targets are typed.** A route names a target by identifier, with a kind (`direct`, `upstream`, `private`, and later `outboundConnector`, whose qualifier dates from when §9.3 bound the bare word "connector" to MCP connectors, which [`connector-removal-design.md`, "CR-1 Removal"](connector-removal-design.md#cr-1-removal) removes, leaving §9.3 as a successor pointer). Egress routes, git routes, and the ref resolver and mirror paths all name targets the same way, so a route to a connector needs no new field.
- **The existing route carries it.** The outbound gateway is an in-cluster service reached by the forwarded CONNECT route, at an address the decision pins, with a signed one-time grant for that connection as the upstream credential, which the decision service issues. The CONNECT authority stays the customer's name, and the name is resolved on the customer's side, so the SSRF filter and the proxied DNS resolver never see it.
- **Network rules.** The outbound gateway is an in-cluster service, so it joins the `lenny-egress` policy through a selector peer, as an in-cluster git service does (see "Network policy and placement").
- **Mints and `inject`.** A mint for a host behind a connector takes the path under "Credentials", through `lenny-egress`, so a private GitLab's project token is minted over the connector. A binding with `delivery: inject` on a private host carries its own CA, because it has no endpoint registration to hold one.
- **Registration validator.** The tenant-registered endpoint validator is written per target kind, so the connector kind can skip the public-address check that applies to the others, with its own rule.
- **Other features.** Per-endpoint CAs, `allowedRefPatterns`, and `credentialPinned` act in `netagent`, so they work on a connector route. The evidence streams record the route target.
- **Level 1.** A connector needs a decision at the proxy, so validators refuse it at level 1, as they do chaining.


### Level 1 replacement

Level 1 lets a deployer use a proxy that cannot call the decision service. The proxy authenticates each connection with a static credential for an identity and enforces a host list that Lenny publishes, and Envoy does not run. It exists for deployers whose policy forbids running Envoy, or whose proxy cannot call the decision API and cannot sit behind `lenny-egress`. Lenny does not recommend it. Level 2, or chaining the deployer's proxy behind `lenny-egress` as an upstream, keeps the guarantees listed under **What level 1 gives up**.

**Selecting it.** The choice is deployment-wide: `centralProxy.contract: level1` with `centralProxy.level1.acknowledgeLimitations: true`, and the chart refuses to render without the second value. Every tenant in the deployment is affected, so the effective-policy API reports `enforcement: level1` and the tenant admin can see the weaker enforcement.

**Identities.** Each identity has a name, a host list, and credentials:

- One identity per pool and tenant, whose list is the pool ceiling intersected with the tenant's egress policy, including any host a session may add, the tenant's warm-up additions, a tenant-owned pool's warm-up overlay, and the hosts of the pool's warm lists.
- One warm identity per pool, whose list is the pool ceiling intersected with the pool warm-up policy and the hosts of the pool's warm lists, as the unbound case under "Effective policy" states.
- An identity's list covers the warm list of every active revision of the pool, because the identity is per pool and a pod set warms for one revision while another serves sessions ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)).
- On a pool with `egressProfile: restricted` and `gitAccess`, the pool-and-tenant identity's list is the bound case of the `gitAccess` formula under "Effective policy", and the warm identity's list is that formula's warm case, because the pool has no ceiling to intersect.
- One service identity per tenant for the gateway's ref resolver, the mirror's fetches, and the Token Service's mints, whose list is the tenant's VCS pool hosts, its providers' declared mint endpoints, and its public git routes.
- One public identity for the mirror's public stores, whose list is the public git hosts the platform default route allows.

The warm and public identities span tenants, and they carry no tenant credential or tenant data. Every identity that carries tenant traffic belongs to one tenant, which closes the cross-tenant shared secret the revision 4 review found. The number of identities is the number of pools times the tenants that can use them, and the document and metrics report it. Identity names are generated from opaque identifiers and contain no tenant or pool string.

The Token Service derives the identity from the pod's state, and a request never names one:

| Pod state | Identity released |
| :-- | :-- |
| Warm pod of a shared pool that holds no tenant pin | The pool's warm identity |
| Warm pod of a tenant-owned pool | The pool-and-owner identity |
| Provisional binding (`materializing`) | The pool-and-tenant identity, under the session's policy |
| Bound session, pinned idle pod, or recycled pod (§5.2 keeps its tenant pin) | The pool-and-tenant identity |

The gateway obtains service and public identities from the Token Service on its existing lease path, and the mirror on the pod credential API with its client certificate.

**Credentials.** The Token Service generates a 256-bit random credential for each identity and rotates it every 24 hours with a 1-hour overlap, both as Helm values, and an admin API call rotates one at once. `netagent` obtains the credential on the pod credential API after both mint checks, holds it in memory only, and flushes it on a binding change, so the agent UID cannot read it. Each credential generation, one rotation of the identity's credential, has its own username (`<identity>.g<n>`). Round 10 ran the probe against Squid over TLS in each configuration above and caught every deliberately broken one, once these rules were added. Round 6 showed that stock Squid's Basic helper keeps one verifier per username, so two passwords on one name cannot overlap, and that a retired generation is refused at once, despite a 7,200 s credential cache, because the access rule names the username. A new generation is published in the document and released to pods only after the contract's apply window has passed, so a healthy proxy already knows it. A `netagent` that gets a 407 or a 403 from the proxy refreshes its credential once and retries, because a retired generation in Squid's credential cache answers 403 and 3 of 3 CONNECTs failed in round 12 with a refresh on 407 alone. It also refreshes before its generation's overlap ends, so a long-lived pod does not hold a retired generation.

**Enforcement.** Enforcement runs in `netagent` and in the proxy, and `netagent` holds the finer layer:

- For each CONNECT, `netagent` asks the decision service on the pod binding API and dials the proxy only on an allow. The decision uses the same effective policy, session host lists, quotas, and audit as the other levels. The egress decision API stays unreachable from pods.
- The proxy then enforces the identity's host list. The reference renderer uses `dstdomain -n`, because Squid's default reverse lookup admitted an unlisted address whose PTR matched a listed name.
- The gateway's ref resolver and the mirror make the same decision call on the egress decision API, carrying the tenant, and then use the tenant's service identity.
- `netagent` enforces the pool's tunnel lifetime, closes a session's tunnels when its binding ends, and reports tunnel opens and closes on the pod binding API, which gives the decision service per-tenant concurrency.
- `netagent` reads the ClientHello of each tunnel and closes the tunnel when its server name differs from the CONNECT authority, under the same per-host-set setting as the default proxy's check, because stock Squid lets a prompt-injected agent front through a listed host without any compromise. A client using Encrypted Client Hello remains the documented limit.
- At level 1, a CONNECT the decision allowed and the proxy then refuses (403 or 407) means sync lag or a contract failure. It is a retryable error with an alert, and it maps to `GIT_CLONE_REF_RESOLVE_TRANSIENT` for the ref resolver. A CONNECT the decision denied maps to `egress_denied`.

**The egress policy document (I4).** The gateway admin API serves it to a platform role that holds only this read. It carries `version`, `expiresAt` (1 hour after the document is generated, by default), and for each identity `allowAll`, `hosts`, `denyHosts`, ports, and each live credential generation's username and SHA-crypt verifier. `allowAll` with `denyHosts` represents an open ceiling, and a proxy applies the denials first. Hosts come from tenant policies, host sets, VCS prefixes, and routes, and a sync job renders them into proxy configuration, so a newline or proxy syntax in a host would inject rules that widen reach for every tenant. A strict grammar therefore applies on write and again when the document is generated: lower-case DNS labels with an optional leading `*.`, at most 253 characters, and a final label that is neither numeric nor `0x`-prefixed, which excludes address literals such as `169.254.169.254`, `2130706433`, and `0x7f.1`. Lenny ships a reference renderer for Squid; round 6 used about 40 lines of Python, and a policy edit reached new CONNECTs 0.21 s after `squid -k reconfigure`.

A separate per-identity endpoint serves the hosts alone in External Dynamic List format to the same role, with no verifiers. EDL is a perimeter feed for firewalls: an EDL consumer has no credentials or expiry and cannot meet the proxy contract, so EDL alone does not make a proxy a level 1 proxy.

**The proxy contract.** A level 1 proxy must:

- serve its proxy listener over TLS with a certificate `netagent` verifies, because the shared credential works from any source and must not cross the network in cleartext;
- accept HTTP CONNECT to TLS ports with HTTP Basic authentication by credential generation username, and refuse a connection with no valid credential;
- apply each new document within 60 seconds, and refuse all traffic once the document it holds has passed `expiresAt`;
- refuse any destination that resolves to a private, loopback, link-local, metadata, cluster, IPv4-mapped, or NAT64 address. The ranges match `lenny-egress`'s list, and the reference renderer expresses them in Squid's terms, because `::ffff:0:0/96` in a Squid `dst` rule refuses all IPv4;
- keep `Proxy-Authorization` from reaching any upstream; and
- log the identity and the host of every connection.

A proxy that identifies clients by source address alone does not meet this contract, because any process in the pod could then use it with no credential, bypassing `netagent`. The checks below refuse it, and a corporate gateway of that kind belongs behind `lenny-egress` as an upstream.

Preflight runs as a pre-install hook, before any gateway can serve the document to a sync job, so it checks only what needs no identity: TLS against the configured CA, and a 407 for no credential and for an invented one. After install, `lenny-ops` runs the full probe through probe identities, once at start and then periodically. `lenny-ops` accepts no cluster-internal traffic (§25.4), so it writes the result to a `lenny-level1-probe` ConfigMap, with new RBAC rows for its write and the controller's read. The controller does not fill warm pods on any pool whose pods use `netagent` until the ConfigMap shows a pass, and a later failure raises an alert without draining pools. `lenny-ops` gains a client certificate at level 1, where it has none by default, and obtains its probe credentials from the Token Service on the pod credential API with it, so its egress row gains the proxy address and the pod credential API port, and the Token Service's ingress row for that API gains `lenny-ops`, on both sides as NET-051 requires. The full probe checks:

- **Authentication.** No credential and a wrong password must return 407. A retired credential generation must be refused: stock Squid answers it with a 403 from its access rule if it had cached the credential, and a 407 otherwise.
- **Positive control.** The probe identity transfers data from an allowed public host, so a proxy that refuses everything, or returns 200 and closes, fails.
- **Separation.** A probe identity gets a 403 for a host only another probe identity lists.
- **Address rule.** A separate probe identity lists `127.0.0.1`, `169.254.169.254`, and a deployer-supplied name that resolves privately. Each must be refused before any connection is attempted, and the identity also holds a wildcard, because a list entry dropped by the grammar produces a 403 that hides a missing rule. The probe covers every range in the list rather than a sample. A 503 from stock Squid means it attempted the connection, so it fails. The grammar's exclusion of literals does not apply to this identity alone.
- **Sync health.** A canary credential generation retired, and a canary host added, more than one apply window ago must be in effect, so a dead sync job is detected within two windows and normal timing raises no false alarm.

The probe samples the contract and cannot prove it. Expiry in particular is enforced by the same sync job whose failure it guards against, and stock Squid has no expiry primitive, so the canary is what detects a stale proxy.

**Refused configuration.** Validators refuse the settings that need a decision at the proxy: upstream chaining, egress groups, private destinations reached through the proxy, tenant-registered upstream proxies, and outbound connectors.

**Private destinations.** A level 1 proxy must refuse private addresses and no Envoy exists to tunnel to a pinned address, so a private destination, such as a self-hosted git service or a tenant's CRM, needs a network path of its own:

- These registrations and grants are Helm values at level 1, because the chart must render a policy from them. From them the chart renders the decision service's grant table and a NetworkPolicy that admits pods labelled `lenny.dev/tenant-id` in the granted set, on a pool whose egress profile permits it (`egressProfile: proxied`, `egressProfile: internet` with `netagent`, or `egressProfile: restricted` with `gitAccess` for a git endpoint), to that address, or to the registration's selector for an in-cluster service. A revocation is a Helm change and a rollout. Pods of other tenants are dropped, and a warm pod of a shared pool carries no label and cannot reach it.
- `netagent` dials the address the decision returns directly, on the git path and on the egress endpoint alike, so `inject`, API tools such as `glab`, and LFS transfers where the provider admits `info/lfs` work on a private host. The decision call on the pod binding API still applies to each connection, and it carries the terminated marker and the asserted route.
- The gateway's ref resolver and the mirror dial the endpoint directly after their egress decision API call, through `ipBlock` or selector rows the chart renders for them. Each checks the decision's address against its own read of the registration, so a compromised decision service cannot send a read token to another registered address.
- A tenant-registered git endpoint on a public address goes through the proxy, which loses the decision's address pinning, so its SSRF protection rests on the deployer's address rule.

**Network rules.** The NetworkPolicies of pools with `egressProfile: proxied`, of pools with `egressProfile: restricted` and `gitAccess`, and of pools with `egressProfile: internet` that run `netagent` gain the level 1 proxy's address as an `ipBlock` peer on its port, in place of the `lenny-egress` selector, and the gateway's, the mirror's, the Token Service's, and `lenny-ops`'s `lenny-system` rows gain the same peer, with the Token Service also holding the direct row to private endpoints.

**What level 1 gives up.**

| Guarantee | Default and level 2 | Level 1 |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Destination enforcement outside the pod | Every CONNECT is decided per session at the proxy. | The proxy enforces the identity's list only. Session host lists and binding state are enforced in the pod, so a compromised `netagent` reaches the whole list. |
| Proxy credential | A pod-bound service account token that lasts 600 s, checked against the live pod and its peer address. | A static credential shared by every pod of the identity. Round 6 confirmed it works from any container that can reach the proxy, until it rotates: up to 25 hours while the sync job is healthy, and until the canary alert is acted on when it is not. |
| Revocation | The proxy refuses the next CONNECT when a binding ends. | `netagent` stops, and the proxy keeps accepting the identity. A tunnel open before a list edit stayed open in round 6. |
| Tunnel lifetime | Envoy's `max_stream_duration` caps every tunnel, whatever the pod does. | `netagent` enforces the cap, so a compromised `netagent` has no outside limit unless the proxy adds one. `client_lifetime` did not end active tunnels on Squid. |
| SSRF protection | The Envoy address filter, fixed by the conformance suite. | The deployer's configuration. Stock Squid reached a listed name that resolved privately until the rule was added. The probe samples this. |
| Server-name check | Enforced by the default proxy, outside the pod. | Enforced by `netagent`, so a compromised `netagent` bypasses it. Stock Squid alone passed a CONNECT to a listed host with TLS naming an unlisted one in round 6. A deployer can add Squid's own peek check. |
| Audit and attribution (P5) | The decision and the connection are recorded at the proxy, per session. | Decision records come from `netagent`'s calls, and the proxy's log names an identity (pool and tenant). A compromised `netagent`'s connections appear in the proxy log with no session. |
| Quotas (P7) | Rates at the decision service, and per-tenant concurrency from the access log stream. | Rates at the decision service, per-pod tunnels in `netagent`, and per-tenant concurrency from `netagent`'s reports, which a compromised `netagent` can understate. The shared limits are the deployer's. |
| Private destinations | A pod has no network path to one. Envoy tunnels to the address the decision pins, for a granted tenant. | A granted tenant's pods have a network path, so a process at the agent UID can open a raw socket to the endpoint with no credential, skipping `netagent`, the decision, audit, and quotas. A mirror subprocess under a hostile pack has the same reach to every registered endpoint, across tenants. |
| Per-tenant routing | Upstream proxies, egress groups, and private destinations per tenant. | One route per deployment, which is the proxy's own. |
| Policy freshness | The next decision. | The sync interval at the proxy, plus the document's expiry. An EDL consumer has no expiry. |
| Corporate gateways | Source-address identity through egress groups. | Unavailable. No surveyed gateway documents per-workload Basic authentication, so these gateways do not meet the contract. They work behind `lenny-egress` through chaining. |
| Warm and public traffic | Decided per pod at the proxy. | The warm and public identities span tenants, so their lists are the reach of any holder. |

P6 holds for unavailability: if the proxy, the decision service, or the credential is unavailable, `netagent` fails closed. It does not hold for a stale proxy, which keeps enforcing its last list and accepting its last credentials until the sync job recovers.

**Why it is not recommended.** Level 1 moves the per-session boundary into the pod, and it replaces a credential bound to one pod with one shared across every pod of an identity. Compromise of one `netagent` gives reach across that pool's list for that tenant for up to 25 hours, from any source that can reach the proxy, whereas the default bounds it to that pod's current policy for the token's life. A dead sync job extends that until an operator acts on the alert. It also makes SSRF protection, which protects the cluster and every tenant, depend on deployer configuration Lenny can only sample. Level 1 suits a single-tenant deployment, or tenants that trust each other, on a proxy that cannot do better.

## TLS interception

Without TLS interception, `lenny-egress` filters by hostname only. A deployer proxy can intercept TLS: corporate gateways do, and so do Squid with `ssl_bump` and mitmproxy. Interception adds path filtering, data loss prevention, and inspection, so a deployer who needs those can close that gap. Lenny's part is distributing the deployer's CA so that tools in the pod trust it:

- **Bundle.** The controller assembles the TLS interception bundle, a combined PEM bundle per deployment or pool, from a public root set the chart ships (the `netagent` image cannot read another container's `/etc/ssl`) and the deployer CA, and mounts it read-only into `netagent`. `netagent` appends only its own per-pod CA and writes the result to `/run/netagent/ca-bundle.pem`. A compromised `netagent` writes that file, so this limits an honest one, and the per-pod CA carries constraints in the modes "Delivery modes" states. The PKCS12 truststore for the JVM holds the whole bundle, because `javax.net.ssl.trustStore` replaces `cacerts`. The deployer CA must satisfy Python 3.13's strict X.509 checks: it needs an Authority Key Identifier and RFC 5280 conformance.
- **Environment variables.** The client proxy profile points these at the bundle: `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, `PIP_CERT`, `GIT_SSL_CAINFO`, `CARGO_HTTP_CAINFO`, and `AWS_CA_BUNDLE`. `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` is set to the deployer CA and the per-pod CA, because with the deployer CA alone Node refuses the per-pod CA's leaves. `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` points the JVM at a PKCS12 truststore. Firefox ignores these variables and needs its policy file or an NSS database, which a runtime image sets up. `SSL_CERT_DIR` alone is never set, because .NET and OpenSSL replace their search path with it.
- **Scope.** The warm-up work, the store fill and the other warm fills, runs before any tenant and reads the bundle, so the TLS interception bundle is per deployment or pool. It cannot vary per tenant. A pool that serves a tenant behind an intercepting gateway carries that gateway's CA.
- **Breakage.** Tools with compiled-in roots break under interception and are listed as unsupported: rustls with webpki-roots, certificate-pinning tools, and some Electron apps.

## Residual risks and limits

- **The LLM host reservation on a pool with `egressProfile: internet`.** The reservation runs at each decision, and a raw socket has no decision. Under LU-4 no operator LLM credential reaches a pod: the gateway's LLM proxy attaches each upstream's credential, and the runtime holds only the session's proxy token, which the proxy alone accepts. On a pool with `egressProfile: internet` a process in the pod can send that token to any host, and its use is bounded by the session's credit budgets of [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#lu-6-credit-budgets). The reservation keeps a tenant route from claiming the endpoint host of an upstream record, so no tenant proxy receives a request that carries an operator's LLM credential (LUR-11).
- **Exfiltration.** Hostname filtering limits exfiltration but does not prevent it. Any allowed host that accepts uploads remains a channel: a package registry's publish endpoint, a git host, or an issue tracker. The documentation states this for each shipped host set. A `*` ceiling allows port 443 only unless the pool names other ports.
- **Operator services.** Lenny calls no external MCP server, agent service, or other operator service for a session. A runtime's harness reaches such a service through `netagent` and `lenny-egress` like any other host, so the effective policy, the evidence streams, the per-session bytes-out alert under "Exfiltration controls", and `egressLockHosts` apply to it, and a service on a private network is reached through a tenant outbound connector route ([`connector-removal-design.md`, "CR-3 External services"](connector-removal-design.md#cr-3-external-services)). A tool call's arguments can still carry source code to an allowed service, as to any allowed host that accepts uploads. Tool rules, approvals, and per-call records for such a service are the service's own: `egress.tunnel` records the connection and its bytes and no tool call, and `credential.mint` records each secret or identity assertion `netagent` attached. The MCP connector calls of §9.3, which ran on the gateway outside these controls, left Lenny with connectors (CR-1).
- **Harness traffic outside the MCP path.** The harness-egress spike of 2026-11-15 found two behaviours a runtime author meets **[measured]**. The OAuth discovery probe of `codex mcp list` ignores the proxy and the custom CA, so in a pod it fails closed, because the pod's NetworkPolicy admits no direct egress, and it reports the server's auth as unsupported. Codex 0.136.0 and Claude Code 2.1.323 contact vendor hosts at start: Claude Code opens a connection to `api.anthropic.com` unless `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1` is set, and Codex opens connections to `chatgpt.com`, `github.com`, `api.github.com`, and `api.openai.com`. The egress policy refuses each one unless a pool allows it, and in the spike the refusals left the MCP connections unaffected. The runtime author guide states both, and a runtime author sets the Claude Code variable in the runtime's `env` and allows only the hosts the harness needs.
- **Logging.** Neither Envoy's access log nor `netagent`'s log records `Proxy-Authorization`, injected headers, or decrypted bodies. The conformance tests fix the log formats.
- **Node versions.** `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY` exists from Node 22.21 and 24.5. An image on an older Node fails fast on every fetch with ENOTFOUND, and the runtime author guide states the floor.
- **SNI and domain fronting.** Without a server-name check, the proxy cannot confirm that a tunnel's TLS server name matches its CONNECT authority. An agent can CONNECT to an allowed host and then send a ClientHello naming another host on the same CDN, which domain fronting exploits on CDNs that do not enforce matching names.
  - **Mechanism.** Envoy's CONNECT termination hands the tunnel's bytes to a TCP proxy. Listener filters such as `tls_inspector`, which read the ClientHello, run only on new downstream connections. The check therefore routes the tunnel's bytes into an internal listener (`envoy.bootstrap.internal_listener`), a listener inside the same Envoy process that receives a connection from another Envoy filter rather than from the network. The CONNECT route passes the expected host to it through shared filter state. The internal listener runs `tls_inspector`, then an RBAC filter whose CEL condition compares the server name with the expected host, and then forwards with `sni_dynamic_forward_proxy` and `tcp_proxy`.
  - **Round 3 result.** The check works at about 175 lines of YAML. A matching server name passes. A different name, a missing name, or an IP-literal authority is refused. The cost is about 0.2 ms at p50 and 15 to 25% more Envoy CPU per tunnel.
  - **Caveats.** The decision service lower-cases the authority, because Envoy does not. Non-TLS payloads close or pass by policy, and server-first protocols wait for `listener_filters_timeout`, which defaults to 15 s. Missing headers render as `-` in `set_filter_state` until `omit_empty_values: true` is set. On this route an SSRF refusal arrives as 200 followed by EOF rather than 503. Encrypted Client Hello defeats the check when the client sets the outer public name to the authority: the server received a different inner name.
  - **Status.** Proposal 2 carries the check as a per-host-set setting, on by default for TLS host sets, on for a `*` ceiling, and off for hosts that carry non-TLS protocols. Squid can make the same check by peeking without decrypting.
- **DNS channel.**
  - Today one dedicated agent CoreDNS, `lenny-agent-dns`, forwards every name upstream. An agent can encode data in queries for a domain it controls, and the attacker's DNS server receives them. The rate limit (10 responses per second per source) and the TXT size filter slow this, but A and AAAA queries still carry data.
  - Pods in pools with `egressProfile: proxied` have no need for external names, because clients send the hostname inside CONNECT. The second spike confirmed this for Go, Node's undici, .NET, and CPython. Java's `URL.equals` resolves names, but it falls back to comparing strings when resolution fails.
  - The fix is a second CoreDNS Deployment for pods with `egressProfile: proxied`, beside `lenny-agent-dns` ("Network policy and placement", **The `proxied` DNS resolver.**). It answers cluster names and returns NXDOMAIN immediately for everything else. Dropping queries would stall Java.
  - The controller gives pods with `egressProfile: proxied` that instance's address as their nameserver. That needs a per-profile DNS address, since today one address applies to every pod. The `proxied` NetworkPolicy allows only that instance.
  - The cost is one small Deployment plus a controller change for the per-profile address. Pods with `egressProfile: restricted` carry the same DNS channel today, so the gap is not new. The channel matters more once pods with `egressProfile: proxied` run agents with open web access, and round 3 found no client that needs external names behind the proxy, so proposal 2 carries the `proxied` DNS resolver.
  - Round 3 ran it on kind. Cluster names resolved in 0.3 to 4 ms, external names returned NXDOMAIN in about 1 ms after walking the search list, and curl, Python requests and pip, Node 24 fetch, and Go reached public HTTPS sites through the proxy with no local lookup of the target name. Node without `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY` failed fast with ENOTFOUND rather than stalling.
- **Level 1 replacement.** See "Level 1 replacement", **What level 1 gives up**.
- **Platform trust boundary.** `lenny-ops` and the controller can reach the egress PKI through their existing rights (see "Transport security").
- **Stored-token push.** See "Credentials".
- **Mirror fill causes.** The Token Service checks the cause of each mirror fill itself ("Git mirror (`lenny-git-mirror`)", **Fill causes**). A compromised mirror can still name any live pod cause in a tenant whose route names the mirror, and it can replay a gateway cause assertion within its 60-second expiry for the same upstream and pool. This is the same limit as the mirror's tenant assertion on its upstream fetches.
- **Uncovered git hosts.** On a host no VCS pool, git route, or binding of the tenant names, git goes through an opaque tunnel with no git enforcement. `netagent` sees the CONNECT authority alone, so there is no path check, no refusal of a push, no `allowedRefPatterns`, no push option refusal, no `git.operation` record, no mirror route, no `sshForms` mapping, and no fetch size cap. A pinned-SHA `gitClone` also skips the ref resolver's host check. A token the agent holds reaches the host **[measured]**, a run's push ended non-fast-forward, so a push completing there is inferred from candidate P of decision 44, which rewrote `https://` remotes to `http://` through a loopback `http.proxy`, and a push to a server an attacker runs needs no token. This is a loss against the earlier loopback git path (candidate L), which rewrote remote URLs, and the owner took it with decision 44. A hybrid that keeps the prior path for these hosts was not measured, and the design has none. What the design gives instead:
  - The effective policy still decides which hosts a CONNECT can reach, and `egress.tunnel` records each tunnel with bytes in each direction, except at level 1, where `egress.local` carries it.
  - A tenant that needs enforcement on a host adds a git route to `direct` or to the mirror, which makes it covered. A fetch of a public repository then passes and a push is refused unless a pool covers the host. It costs a CA entry.
  - Under EG-12, `credentialPinned` on the host refuses it with no binding.
  - The minted token and a binding's secret never go there, so what reaches the host is a token the agent already held.
  - **The alias limit.** Coverage names host names. An allowed sibling name for the same service, or on a `*` ceiling an agent's own DNS name at the host's public address, is opaque, and an agent's own token works there if the host accepts it. Shipped host data adds a provider's alias hosts, and the server-name check, which is on for a `*` ceiling, refuses a mismatched name and an IP literal. How GitHub and GitLab edges treat an unknown server name is not measured. On an open ceiling the agent can already send data to its own host.
- **Tree validation.** Pod-side git skips the gateway's tree validation, which happens at materialization:
  - Today the gateway clones a `gitClone` source itself, packs the tree into a tar, decodes it inside the gateway through the archive gate (§7.1 step 12, §13.4, `gatedExtract`), and streams the files into the pod. The gate rejects absolute paths, `..`, excessive depth and length, hardlinks, device files, FIFOs, and sockets. It confines symlink targets to the slot and away from `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, and `/run/lenny`. It strips setuid, setgid, and sticky bits, and it bounds total size, entry size, entry count, and compression ratio.
  - When the pod fetches with git, most of these threats cannot arise, because git trees hold only regular files with mode 644 or 755, directories, symlinks, and submodule links. git's checkout always rejects `.git` entries in any spelling, `..`, and writes through symlinks. With `transfer.fsckObjects=true`, fetch also rejects malformed trees and unsafe `.gitmodules`.
  - Git does not cover the gate's checks on a symlink that points outside the tree, which git checks out as written, or on a repository's size, which nothing bounds before checkout.
  - The design closes these gaps. No component that runs as the adapter or `netagent` UID reads a slot's files except through the slot's broker, whose every resolution refuses a symbolic link (`RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS`), and `lenny-materialize` refuses a link whose target leaves `/workspace` when it creates one and never writes through a link ([WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway)). `netagent` and the mirror cap fetch size per pool. The helper's tree-size check bounds what a materialization checkout writes, and the adapter's per-slot disk watch is its backstop. [§4.4.4](../spec/04_system-components.md#444-checkpoint-slos-and-scheduling) **Hard workspace size limit.** also requires `emptyDir.sizeLimit` on the slot volume, but `podspec/volumes.go` sets none today, so the kubelet does not bound a fetch the agent runs. That gap is filed as a finding and closed by the slot volumes of [PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction), each with its `sizeLimit`, because the design relies on it.
  - §13.4's sentence that pod binaries neither decompress archives is replaced by the rule of [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway): no platform-privileged process parses untrusted content. A platform-privileged process holds a credential or reaches beyond one session: the gateway, the adapter, `netagent`, the controllers, and the git mirror. Archive bytes, git objects and trees, and anything a slot UID wrote are decompressed, unpacked, checked out, and written only by `lenny-materialize` at the session's slot UID, and a platform-privileged process exchanges data with a slot only in formats the platform defines, decoded strictly under fixed bounds. Every git step on a slot repository runs in the helper, and when the agent runs git itself the result is equivalent to files the agent could create at runtime. Two parsers of hostile git input stay outside the rule by construction: `netagent` reads ref commands and LFS batch bodies on the git path under bounded parsing, and the mirror runs `git fetch` and `upload-pack` in a sandboxed subprocess per store ("Git mirror (`lenny-git-mirror`)"). The store fill parses git as well, and it runs in `lenny-store-fill` under its own UID, with no credential and the store volume as its only writable mount, so it is no platform-privileged process under the rule (decision 61). It writes only bare stores and their pristine trees, with hooks and configuration ignored and `transfer.fsckObjects=true`. The adapter mounts the store volume read-write only to delete a withheld store, and it parses no repository data.

## Specification sections affected

- §2 Goals and Non-Goals: "gateway-mediated file delivery", "Git-based or object-store-based workspace population by pods", and "no standing credentials in pods", which the gated opt-ins qualify.
- §4.7.1 and §28.5.1: the materialization RPC carries a `gitClone` source with its resolved SHA to the pod instead of streamed files.
- §4.7: the `netagent` container, the local egress endpoint, slot attribution with the per-slot scopes of decision 60 and their fallback, the fetch grant, the `lenny-store-fill` init container and `netagent`'s treatment of its UID (decision 61), `/run/netagent` and the network descriptor, the per-slot `delivery: file` volumes, the launch variables in the supervisor environment, and the removal of `git-credential-lenny`.
- §4.7.10 and §6.1: the name-constrained trust anchor on every agent pod, which the removal of pre-launched runtimes and of service mode and `type: mcp` runtimes allows (decisions 54 and 62).
- §4.9: provider capability levels, minting, token caching, repository routing, and credential bindings with their delivery modes.
- §10.2: the `tenant_id` pattern narrows to a valid Kubernetes label value, at most 63 characters with alphanumeric ends, because level 1 policies select on `lenny.dev/tenant-id`, the label stamp already fails on a longer identifier, and no deployment depends on the wider form.
- §5.1 and §5.2 (which [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) O7 renames "Pool Configuration"): the pool egress ceiling, the warm-up policy, `gitAccess`, the client proxy profile, pool ownership, policy granularity per pod model with per-slot scopes on a pool with several slots, `prestage: true` on a `gitRepository` warm entry of an operator's Runtime or a tenant-derived runtime (decision 67, which replaces decision 63's `path`), and the reservation of the launch variable names in the Runtime `env` rule.
- §7.1: steps 11 to 13 (clone in the slot by `lenny-materialize`, whose steps [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) owns) and 0111's **Bind bounds.**, and the order of the creation steps, under which `gitClone` ref resolution runs after the post-chain point of RT-2, and before the pod claim where the plan arrives with the create request.
- §14.1 **Plan binding.**: `gitClone.ref` resolution at a request that binds the plan runs after the post-chain point on every creation path, and before the pod claim where the plan arrives with the create request. A plan bound at finalize resolves on the pod claimed at create, so a bad ref fails the finalize. Every resolution carries the gateway cause assertion, which the decision service and, through the mirror, the Token Service verify.
- §7.3: the provisional binding during a resume rebuild, and the 30 s fetch wait on that path.
- §7.1 `sessionIsolationLevel`: the field `perSlotScopes`, `false` on a pod with several slots whose runtime class does not answer slot attribution and so keeps the union scope, and `true` where each slot has its own scope.
- §4.7, the rendered git configuration: `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`, and a runtime may override the rendered maintenance settings from its global or repository configuration or through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, at the cost of its own next checkpoint (IC D6).
- §6.1 and §6.4: warm-up egress, the warm fill, and the readable set before a session binds, which is the pool's warm list plus the warm-up policy's repositories.
- §6.2: the `git_fetch_pending` materialization failure reason and the fetch retry window against the finalize bound.
- §7.5: setup-phase network access, which replaces "network blocked by default during setup" for pools with `egressProfile: proxied`.
- §10.2: platform-admin-only `egressProfile` writes on shared pools, and the tenant pool overlay in the "own tenant" scoping note.
- §10.3 and §13.2: the egress PKI, separate from the `mtls.enabled` PKI, and TLS on the proxy hop, the egress decision API, the git endpoint, and the pod binding API, in line with the cross-namespace posture §13 already states for the OTLP and object-store links.
- §10.3: the two `netagent` token audiences beside the gateway audience, and the Token Service as a per-tenant OIDC issuer.
- §12.8: purge of tenant stores in the mirror under `DeleteByTenant`.
- §12.9: classification of mirror contents, federation tokens, and credential bindings.
- §11.7, §12, §12.8, §12.9, §15, and §16: the evidence streams, `EvidenceStore`, their anchor in the audit chain, erasure, classification, retention, and read APIs.
- §13.1: the egress, file delivery, and credentials rows ("No standing credentials" gains the gated exceptions), the `netagent` container identity, the webhook coverage of `netagent`'s volumes, and the mirror container's capability exception.
- §13.2.2: the base policy, which `proxied` and `gitAccess` extend.
- §13.2.5: the exclusion list gains the ranges listed under "Central egress proxy", and the SSRF check on tenant-registered services.
- §13.2.7: the second DNS resolver for pods with `egressProfile: proxied`, `lenny-proxied-dns`, beside the dedicated agent CoreDNS `lenny-agent-dns`.
- §13.3: credential bindings and federation tokens in the credential flow.
- §13.2.4: `lenny-system` rows for `lenny-egress`, `lenny-egress-authz`, `lenny-evidence`, and `lenny-git-mirror`, including the Token Service's path to `lenny-egress` for mints and every producer's path to `lenny-evidence`; the Token Service's ingress row gains the agent namespaces on the pod credential API port, and its egress gains the decision service, the pool and session stores, the API server pod watch over the agent namespaces and `lenny-system`, and the provider APIs.
- §4.9 and §10.3: the Token Service's new reads (binding definitions and session bindings through `pkg/binding`), its pod credential API listener beside the gateway-only mTLS listener, and its check of a mirror fill's cause, including the gateway cause assertion.
- §13.2.6: the `proxied` egress profile, the removal of `provider-direct` (NET-006 and NET-044), the DNS resolver for pods with `egressProfile: proxied`, and the IPv4-mapped and NAT64 exclusions.
- §13.4: the rule of [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway) in place of "pod binaries neither decompress archives", with the parsers this design keeps outside it (`netagent`'s git-path parsing and the mirror's sandboxed subprocess), and the store fill in `lenny-store-fill`, which holds no credential and so needs no exception (decision 61). The materialization design carries the edit.
- §14: the `gitClone` row (pod-side clone, ref resolution through `ls-refs` in Go, and no gateway clone), `writableRepositories`, `egress.allowedHosts`, session credential bindings, the `origin` remote, the reserved `env` names, and the removal of the in-pod credential helper.
- §15.1: the `GIT_CLONE_REF_*` rows keep their meaning with `ls-refs`, and the new egress, binding, and clone error codes.
- §15.7: the network accessor in the runtime SDKs.
- §16: the metrics and alerts of the new components.
- §17.2 and §17.8: the namespace layout, the admission inventory (the third UID), the mirror container's capability exception, its Pod Security prerequisite, its anti-affinity and `hostUsers` value, and sizing. The placement of these components, their priority classes, replicas, and disruption budgets are [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md)'s changes to §17 (PM-8).
- §18: the materializer entry for `gitClone`, which names `lenny-materialize` and the pinned git.
- §21.9 and §26.3: the in-pod token-helper references.
- §24 and §25: `lenny-ctl` commands and operability tools, as the documentation section lists.
- §15: admin API resources for host sets, upstreams, git endpoints, tenant egress policy, tenant pool overlays, credential bindings, per-class gate grants, client proxy profiles, tenant client config files, and mirror prefetch.
- §13.1, §13.2, §15, §16, and §17: level 1 replacement, which covers the policy document and EDL endpoints, identities and their rotation, the decision call on the pod binding API, the validators' refusals, the probe, the alert, and the Helm values.
- §15.1: the `egress_denied` and `no_egress_path` reasons on `GIT_CLONE_REF_UNRESOLVABLE`.
- §23: "pods can only reach the gateway".
- §26.2: the removal of category-keyed defaults (egress, isolation, delivery mode, and setup command policy), descriptive `defaultPoolConfig` text per reference runtime, and the `vcs.<provider>.*` scopes.
- §28: the new links and channels. The labels I1 to I8 are design-local; the proposal assigns `LNK-` and `CH-` identifiers under the naming law, which forbids positional names. §28.5.5's register of the pod-egress boundary (`CH-LLMPROXY` and `CH-OBJSTORE`) gains the new channels, and `CH-LLMPROXY` itself is unchanged. The intra-pod register gains `CH-FETCHGRANT`, the adapter's fetch grants to `netagent` ("Slot attribution and fetch grants"), and `CH-EGRESSHALT`, the adapter's egress halts, which CR-C of [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) adds.
- §4.3, §4.7.1, §6.2, §11.3, §12.8, §13.3, §15.1, §16.1, and §28: the `identityAssertion` secret kind, the identity issuers and the deployment key set with their rotation and the setting that turns the deployment key set off, `HaltSessionEgress` with its 5-second deadline, `IDENTITY_ASSERTION_UNSUPPORTED_POD`, `impersonation_identity_assertion`, `lenny_identity_assertion_mints_total`, and `lenny_egress_halts_total`. [`connector-removal-design.md`, "Specification changes"](connector-removal-design.md#specification-changes) stages these edits through its CR-B and CR-C, and this design's bindings and `netagent` text is their mechanism.

## Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings

Proposal 0111 is rewritten around this design.

- **Kept unchanged**: the slot repository rules (alternates, capture copy, and `storeCommit`), which `lenny-materialize` now runs as the slot UID ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)), with the index refreshed more than 1 s after checkout, store immutability, the §7.1 **Bind bounds.**, and the defect filings. 0111's `ValidateGitURL` is replaced by the shared canonicalizer. 0111's `Dockerfile.git` for the gateway is dropped, because the gateway runs no git; F-14.1.37 closes with the clone path's removal.
- **Kept with changes**:
  - The warm fill and `gitRepository` shared asset are fetched by `netagent` over smart HTTP, and every pre-filled store on a pod comes from its warm list (decision 45).
  - A `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime is public only. A private store comes from a `gitRepository` asset of a tenant-derived runtime, which reaches that runtime's tenant-owned pool alone, and it is authorized through the egress decision API (decisions 46 and 67).
  - The mirror semantics move into `lenny-git-mirror`.
  - Restore authorization is kept. With `FetchGitPack` gone, nothing server-side binds a restore fetch to a repository, and `lenny.gitStoreUrl` is agent-writable, so restore completion fetches only a URL the gateway passes for that bind: the plan's repositories, and any the lineage rule authorizes while the rewrite keeps that rule. The gateway passes that set on `FinalizeWorkspace` and on Resume, which replaces 0111's statement that Resume gains no field. A derive copies the stored plan, so every URL a restored repository can legitimately carry is in the plan, and the rewrite drops the lineage rule and `REG-GITLINEAGE` if it confirms that no case remains. The same calls carry the store directories that the adapter withholds for the session.
  - The §13.4 exception is rewritten as above, under the rule of [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway).
  - The `git-https` provider becomes a `stored` provider.
  - The pod identity prerequisite is met by the projected service account token instead of R14.
- **Dropped**: the `FetchGitPack` RPC, gateway pack serving, the gateway mirror limiters and memory table, and the gateway memory limit decision.
- **Dropped from the rewrite**: `destPath`, private entries on a Runtime, and rule 2's ownership key of owner and URL on a Runtime (decision 46).
- **Requirements for the rewrite**: [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Relationship to draft proposal 0111"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#relationship-to-draft-proposal-0111) lists what the rewrite of 0111 must do, which the owner settled on 2026-11-08.
- **For the warm list and a tenant's default clone**: [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Warm stores"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#warm-stores) builds on this rewrite and defines the rule set of the warm list, which "Terms" defines, with the sized store, a full commit, the age limit and drain, backoff for every declarer, and the environment rule. Before a session binds, a pod's readable set is its warm list plus the repositories of the warm-up policy that applies to it, and the decision service and the Token Service read the warm list for the pod's pool and revision (decision 45, and "Warm list per revision" under [RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). A store serves any `gitClone` source in the plan whose normalized URL matches it, unless the source sets `depth` or `submodules: true` (decision 47). A store whose read checks a session fails is withheld for that session on a pod that serves one session: the adapter deletes the store and its pristine tree, without following a symbolic link, and the helper's first run for the session removes any tree the warm-up checkout staged from it in the slot ([WM-15](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-15-warm-up-checkout-and-reflink-rebuild)), both before restore completion runs. On a pod with no store, a new session takes a shallow fetch by SHA from `lenny-materialize`, and restore completion fetches shallow first and fetches history only when its listing finds a missing object, as for a repository whose pinned commit was amended or reset, within the slot's remaining bytes.
- **Warm-up checkout and the reflink rebuild**: owner decisions of 2026-11-12, which [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) owns, change when the checkout of a warm-list entry with `prestage: true` runs and leave every rule above in place. The warm-list entries that carry `prestage: true`, an operator's `gitRepository` entries on every pool of the runtime and a tenant's `gitRepository` assets on a pool owned by that tenant (decision 67), are checked out by the helper at warm-up and after every slot release into `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/<entry>`, with the index refreshed, under the pool's or tenant's warm-up policy and with no session credential. At bind the first `gitClone` source of the session's workspace, from the defaults or the plan, whose URL the store serves and that sets neither `depth` nor `submodules: true` adopts the staged tree by rename to its own `path`, with an in-place checkout when the session's commit differs, and an unused tree is renamed aside and deleted in the background. Where the store volume and the slot volume share a filesystem that supports reflinks, the helper builds each staged tree from one pristine checked-out tree per store, which `lenny-store-fill` writes on `lenny-git-stores` (decision 61), by creating slot-owned files and cloning their content, and on gVisor and Kata it runs a normal checkout. A staged tree from a store withheld for the session is removed before the session starts, and the checkpoint skips the reserved names.

Findings closed or reshaped by this work:

- F-4.9.44 (minting).
- F-14.1.37 (git binary).
- The §26.2 and §13.2.6 contradiction (F-26.2.12).
- The unenforced mode and missing plan check in `lenny/vcs_token` (F-26.2.11).
- The Token Service breaker exposure from in-pod token requests.
- The tenant-admin write of `egressProfile` on shared pools (F-5.2.67).

These defects exist in the code today, independent of this design, and are filed in [`BUILD-GAPS.md`](../BUILD-GAPS.md):

| Finding | Severity | Defect | Closed by |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| F-5.2.65 | High | Service-mode tenant pins have no arbiter across gateway replicas, and a label failure does not stop the request (`tenantaffinity.go` and `statelessproxy.go`), so a service-mode pool with more than one gateway replica can route one tenant's request to a pod pinned to another. §5.2's in-memory mapping cannot arbitrate either. | Closed by removal under [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) proposal AR-B, which removes service mode and withdraws this design's proposal 0 (decision 62) |
| F-5.2.66 | High | The service-mode request path is not wired: `pkg/adapter/statelessslot` has no production caller, and the chart's policies do not admit the gateway's dial to the runtime port. | Closed by removal under AR-B, as F-5.2.65 |
| F-25.6.9 | High | `lenny-ctl doctor --fix` (`doctorremediator.go`) matches every 24-hour leaf against a 7-day window and deletes its Secret, with no `isCA` filter, so it can rotate a CA's key out of order, and the health API reports every healthy install as `unhealthy`. §25.6 specifies this, so the specification changes too. | [SO-6](security-oversight-design.md#so-6-f-2569-certificate-windows-and-the-remedy) in [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| F-25.4.40 | Medium | `lenny-ops` holds `get`, `list`, `watch`, and `delete` on every Secret in the release namespace (`ops-rbac.yaml`), which includes the `lenny-mtls-ca` key, alongside the Job and Certificate rights that reach it another way. | [SO-7](security-oversight-design.md#so-7-f-25440-lenny-ops-rights) in [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| F-5.2.67 | High | A `tenant-admin` can change `egressProfile` and every other field of a shared pool (`applyPoolUpdate` has no role check), so one tenant sets the network posture of pods that serve other tenants. | Proposal 9 |
| F-26.2.11 | High | `lenny/vcs_token` returns the pool's stored token to any session in the tenant, with no check of the plan, the repository, or the mode. | Proposal 3, with minting in proposal 1 |
| F-26.2.12 | Medium | §26.2 describes an `egressProfile` allowlist and an `unrestricted` value that §13.2.6 does not define. | Proposal 9 |
| F-13.2.28 | Medium | The label webhooks do not cover `pods/status`, so a holder of that subresource can change every immutable label and `lenny.dev/tenant-id` with no webhook consulted. | Proposal 6 |
| F-13.2.29 | Low | `DecideImmutableLabels` treats an unset label and an empty value as equal, so an `UPDATE` can add or remove an immutable label with an empty value. The new decider under egress groups checks map presence and does not change the immutable list. | A direct fix |
| F-13.2.31 | Medium | The NET-003 creation guard is not enforced: any principal that can create a pod in an agent namespace can set `lenny.dev/managed: "true"` and the egress labels. The chart grants that right to `lenny-controller` alone, and an agent pod has no path. | A direct fix, with §13.2's identity through the proposal pipeline |

## Reserved for other designs

These hooks are in this design's proposals because adding them later would be rework. Each names the document that owns the feature. None of them does anything until that feature lands, except where stated.

| Hook | Proposal | Why now | Owner |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `owner_tenant_id`, `template_pool`, and `pod_runtime_ref` on the pool row | EG-9, and [DR-2](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#dr-1-to-dr-3-prerequisites) for the last | Stored format | [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md) |
| A `quarantined` binding state in `pkg/binding`, set by a `lenny.dev/quarantine` label or an active `pod_quarantines` row and taking precedence over every other state: the decision service refuses all egress, the Token Service mints nothing, and the mirror serves nothing. Level 1's tenant policies carry the same selector exclusion. | EG-2, EG-7 | It touches the enforcement point in three components, and it denies traffic as soon as a state is set | [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md) |
| A held flag on mirror stores in the store index, which the sweep's re-chown, repacks, and purge honour. The index persists the fetch UIDs that own files in a held store and keeps them out of the pool across a restart, and the on-disk path includes a store generation, so a held store generation and its successor coexist. The fetch UIDs that held stores keep out of the pool are capped by `gitMirror.heldUidBudget`. A hold that would pass the cap records an ownership manifest, which names the fetch UID that owned each file, and then re-chowns the store, so its UIDs return to the pool, as **Fetch UIDs** under [FE-2](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md#fe-2-mirror-store-hold) states. | EG-5 | Those paths would otherwise need rework once shipped | [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md) |
| One write path for every opt-in (bindings, gates, VCS pool write settings, tenant-registered endpoints, routes, always-allowed and session-requestable hosts, warm-up additions, client config files, overlays, derived runtimes and the derivation grant, and the shadow policy), which applies a tenant policy check, empty today, and emits an audit event | EG-9 | Guardrails need a single choke point | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| A nullable `suspendedBy` on every opt-in object, which the effective policy, the Token Service, `netagent`, and the decision service treat as absent, except that a running session's qualification under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials` still counts a suspended binding, with the effective-policy read naming the guardrail layer, a withdrawn gate, a credential pin, or a VCS pool that suspended it | EG-9, EG-2, EG-4 | A tightening must reach existing objects, and the check would otherwise be added to several components | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| Writable set entries as records with a `state` (`granted`, the only value EG-3 sets, and later `pendingApproval`) and an optional `expiresAt`, with any other state read only to both mint checks and `netagent`, except that `userRefused` also denies reads of that repository. An unknown state on a readable entry denies access. | EG-2, EG-3 | The writable set is in the binding record and feed | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| `pkg/binding` returns the session's environment and `crossedFromEnvironment`, and the decision service, the Token Service, and the mirror reach tenant objects through one tenant-object resolver taking tenant and environment, which filters nothing until [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping) | EG-2 | The enforcement point exists in several components | [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) |
| Named permissions on every new read (`evidence.read.<stream>`, `egress.effectivePolicy.read`), which `tenant-admin` holds by default | EG-8 and EG-9 | A new role is then a new grant, with no handler changes | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) |
| The evidence envelope, its `provenance` values and `payloadKeyRef`, per-stream retention and permissions, and a held marker on manifests | EG-10 | New streams, such as agent activity, are then additive, and a hold needs no format change | [`forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md`](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md) |
| Typed route targets and a per-kind registration validator | EG-2 | A connector then needs no new route or contract | [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md) |
| A write refuses two VCS pools on one host whose flavours differ. The flavour derives from provider and host, as [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Request rules on VCS hosts"](user-git-identity-design.md#request-rules-on-vcs-hosts) states, with no stored field. | EG-1 | The pool write is a shipped validator. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| An expected route and CA on pod credential API responses for a `userCredential` binding, with the route asserted on the CONNECT by `netagent` and by the Token Service on a link provider call, in the level 2 contract, and with the decision service refusing a disagreement, its cache key holding the asserted list, and the level 1 decision call on the pod binding API carrying it | EG-2, EG-4, EG-7 | A wire contract on the proxy hop and the pod credential API, and a shipped decision. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| A `handling` value and a mint refusal for a session qualifying under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`, limited to `netagent`'s requests, so an image without the attach rule refuses the release rather than minting | EG-3, EG-4 | The git path drops a client's credential in the first shipped image. [UG-3](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-3-pull-request-authoring) owns the field and the rule under "Git and hosts", building on [UC-5](user-credential-delivery-design.md#uc-5-delivery-file). | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| `cacheUntil` on pod credential API responses, honoured by `netagent` | EG-2, EG-4 | A wire contract and a cache rule. An image that ignores it holds a token past revocation with no error. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| `handling` on pod credential API responses, with `netagent` refusing what it does not implement | EG-4 | The refusal has to be in the first shipped image, or an older one attaches a credential without a required step. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| The request inspection stage in `inject`, able to refuse and to replace the forwarded path, headers, body, and query string, evaluating `requestRules` from the pod credential API | EG-4 | `inject` is a shipped enforcement point on both transports, and canonical forwarding is what closes a parser difference a capture found. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| One binding with `delivery: inject` per host and session, with the exception of an `identityAssertion` binding beside one other `inject` binding whose `attach` header differs (O5 of [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md)), one `userCredential` binding per type host, and no binding with `delivery: file` beside one with `delivery: inject`, on type hosts, and `scope: poolAttach` | EG-4 | Which binding answers on a shared host is otherwise unspecified, and fixing it later changes a shipped decision. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| `secret` as a typed union, with `userCredential` and unknown kinds absent | EG-4 | Stored record format. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| The `userCredentialDelivery` gate, empty until [UC-5](user-credential-delivery-design.md#uc-5-delivery-file) | EG-9 | One write path and one gate model. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| A pre-mint step before the mint cache lookup, empty until [UG-2](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-2-repository-authorization) fills it as [UC-7](user-credential-delivery-design.md#uc-7-add-ons)'s release check | EG-1, EG-2 | The order of a check and the cache is a shipped enforcement point. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| Readable entries as records with `verifiedAt`, and a declared-only input on the readable formula | EG-2, EG-3, EG-9 | The binding record and feed are stored and on the wire. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| The repository named in the mirror's second check on the egress decision API | EG-5, EG-2 | The mirror is the path with no mint. [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping)'s mirror filter needs the same field. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| `pkg/binding` returns the session's user, root session, caller type, and the user's invalidation level. `caller_type` becomes a field on the session row, because only the request principal carries it today, and a delegated child copies its parent's value. | EG-2 | The same reason as the environment hook. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| A `userCredential` binding excluded from the provisional binding until `running` | EG-2 | The §7.3 edit is staged there. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| A delegated child receives a `userCredential` binding only when the lease names it | EG-9 | The rule for children is under "Effective policy". | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| A VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` as a source of `credentialPinned`, read across the tenant unfiltered by environment | EG-12 | The credential pin's sources are read by a shipped enforcement point. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| A link provider's endpoints on the mint path, through `lenny-egress` whatever the route | EG-2 | The decision service's tenant assertion is a shipped check. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| Push option refusal, with bounded parsing past the ref commands | EG-3 | The push path is a shipped enforcement point. | [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) |
| A head read deadline, and refusal of a bare LF, on `netagent`'s listeners | EG-2, EG-4 | A defect of the listener with or without rules. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`, "Bounds and cost"](user-credential-delivery-design.md#bounds-and-cost) |
| `lockEgressToHosts` in the session formulas | EG-9 | A formula every enforcement point reads. | [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) |
| `lenny.dev/doctor-optout` on the egress CA and leaf Certificates | EG-11 | Today's `doctor --fix` remedy deletes a matched Certificate's Secret, and the annotation is a hard skip. It protects these certificates until the remedy is fixed, so this design does not wait for that fix. | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) ([SO-6](security-oversight-design.md#so-6-f-2569-certificate-windows-and-the-remedy)) |

Region and tenant key fields on mirror stores and evidence objects are in scope here rather than hooks. The mirror honours a tenant's `dataResidencyRegion`: a tenant whose region the mirror cannot serve routes `direct` for its private repositories, as a non-`mint-scoped` provider does.


## Proposal split

Proposal 0, the service-mode tenant pin, is withdrawn (decision 62). [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) removes service mode in its proposal AR-B, which closes F-5.2.65 and F-5.2.66 by removal and lands before the proposals of this design that touch the pod, so no proposal here rewrites service-mode code.

1. **Git prerequisites**: GitHub App minting with provider capability levels and the token cache (F-4.9.44), repository identifiers, the shared repository-path canonicalizer, repository routing, and write-scope checks. The `github` provider keeps accepting a stored `token` until proposal 3 removes its consumers, so proposal 1 lands without breaking `gitClone`.
2. **Pod network agent and proxied egress**: the `tenant_id` pattern, the removal of `provider-direct`, server TLS on the proxy hop and the pod binding API and mTLS on the egress decision API, that API's operations by client SAN, with the chart's reload, readiness, mount, CPU, and alert rules, the producers of `egress.decision`, `egress.tunnel`, `egress.local`, and `credential.mint`, the `quarantined` binding state, typed route targets and the per-kind validator, the Token Service's mint path through `lenny-egress`, the binding push and cache rule for credential pins, `netagent`, service account identity, `lenny-egress` on Envoy, `lenny-egress-authz` with the egress decision API and the pod binding API, the binding feed with fan-out and the read-scoped provisional resume binding (§7.3), the owner label on tenant-owned pods at creation (§5.2), port scoping, the Kubernetes issuer verifier, the pod credential API listener, the `pkg/binding` resolution package with each session's slot index, slot attribution and the per-slot scopes of decision 60 with their fallback, and the Token Service's new RBAC and ingress rows, the CA bundle assembly, the tunnel lifetime menu, quotas, the `proxied` egress profile, and the DNS resolver for pods with `egressProfile: proxied`.
3. **Pod git path** (F-26.2.11): GitLab token reuse, the `git.operation` stream from `netagent`, `allowedRefPatterns`, commit identity, `gitAccess` and its effective-policy formula, the covered host set with its push and its alias data, the per-pod CA and its name constraints, termination and the git path on the egress endpoint with its fail-closed rules and LFS response handling, the terminated marker for covered hosts in decisions and in the decision cache's key, the coordination generation wait before materialization, the pod trust bundle in git's trust, push with minted tokens, `writableRepositories`, the rendered git config and per-host `sshForms` rules, the `origin` remote, LFS, redirect handling, `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` in the rendered configuration, slot attribution and the fetch grant with `CH-FETCHGRANT`, `netagent`'s handling of the helper's fetches, the gateway's Go `ls-refs` resolver through `lenny-egress`, the removal of the gateway clone path (which closes F-14.1.37), and the removal of `git-credential-lenny` and `lenny/vcs_token`. The pod-side clone itself, the pinned git, and the helper's git steps land with [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md)'s proposals, and the workspace ownership item needs no fix of its own (decision 51).
4. **Credential delivery**: credential bindings with `inject` delivery (plain-HTTP upgrade, and EG-3's termination extended to bound hosts), `federation` delivery with the Token Service as OIDC issuer and the AWS, GCP, and Azure templates, `metadata` delivery with the AWS plugin, and `file` delivery with the generic directory and the `netrc` projection. It also covers the network descriptor, the SDK network accessor, and tenant client config files.
5. **Git mirror and warm fill**: the store fill in `lenny-store-fill` with `netagent`'s attribution of its UID (decision 61), `prestage: true` on a `gitRepository` warm entry (decision 67), the held flag and region and key fields on stores, the `git.operation` stream from the mirror, `lenny-git-mirror` serving TLS, with its anti-affinity, `gitMirror.hostUsers` and its start check, the ID ranges and fetch UID reuse rule, its sandbox and capability exception, per-store ownership, the fetch retry window, the mirror prefetch resource, and the rewritten 0111.
6. **Per-tenant routing and replacement** (F-13.2.28): upstream registrations and chaining, private destination pinning, egress groups with API assignment, exclusivity, and the `lenny.dev/egress-group` label, per-endpoint and per-binding CAs, tenant-registered public services, and deployer CA trust distribution.
7. **Level 1 replacement**: identities and their rotation, the policy document, its host grammar, the EDL endpoint, the reference Squid renderer, the decision call and tunnel reports on the pod binding API, the `netagent` server-name check, the service identities for the ref resolver and the mirror, the Helm-rendered private destination grants and tenant-scoped policies, the direct path for `netagent`, the ref resolver, and the mirror, the validators' refusals, the acknowledgement, the install order with its preflight and post-install probe, the canary, and the documentation of its limits.
8. **Tenant visibility**: decision queries and export, the effective-policy read, the shadow policy, the egress address read, and fixed-name files for more registries.
9. **Egress policy objects** (F-5.2.67 and F-26.2.12): pool ownership (`ownerTenantId`, taken from draft 0111 because the formulas depend on it), ceilings, warm-up policies, tenant egress policy, session `egress.allowedHosts`, tenant pool overlays, host sets, the client proxy profile, the removal of category-keyed defaults from §26.2, the `egressProfile` write restriction, the per-class gate grants, one write path for opt-ins, and named permissions on the new reads.
10. **Evidence ingest**: the envelope, `lenny-evidence`, `EvidenceStore`, the compactor, the chain anchor, retention, erasure, the held marker, and the regulated write-ahead path.
11. **Egress PKI**: the egress CA, its Issuer and leaves with their `lenny.dev/doctor-optout` annotation, the `lenny-egress-ca` ConfigMap, and the rotation procedure, with no consumer until the others use it.
12. **Exfiltration controls**: `credentialPinned` and read-only hosts, added to EG-3's terminated marker, and name constraints that cover those hosts. The other proposals do not wait for it, and until it lands the opaque-tunnel limits under "Residual risks and limits" apply to every host EG-3 does not cover.

Proposals 1, 9, and 11 are independent of one another. Proposal 10 depends on proposal 11 for its certificates and lands with no producer until the others use it, so a decision service never ships without its evidence format. Proposal 2 depends on proposals 1, 9, 10, and 11, and on AR-B, since its Token Service minting path needs proposal 1, and it lands the pod credential API, `pkg/binding`, and that API's listener with no pod-side caller until proposals 3 and 4 use them, which its tests cover through a test client. Proposal 3 depends on proposals 1, 2, and 9. Proposals 4, 5, and 6 depend on proposals 2 and 9, and proposals 4 and 5 also depend on proposal 3. Proposal 8 depends on proposals 4, 6, and 10. Proposal 12 depends on proposals 2, 3, and 4, because it adds credential-pinned and read-only hosts to EG-3's terminated marker. The shared gateway key-source proposal, which fixes F-7.1.31 (each gateway process seeds its own `uploadToken` HMAC key ring, so another replica refuses an upload token it issued), is unaffected and can run first.

Proposal 7 depends on proposals 2 and 3, and on proposal 5 only for the mirror's service identity. Later work: push forwarding through the mirror, and GCP and Azure `metadata` plugins. R14 is a separate remediation step that this design does not absorb.

**Requests from other designs on the proposals above.** EG-1 carries the pre-mint step and the flavour refusal. EG-2 carries `cacheUntil`, the asserted route and its level 2 contract row, with its level 1 decision call in EG-7, the readable entries, `pkg/binding`'s user fields, the provisional exclusion, the link provider endpoints, and the head read deadline. EG-3 carries the push option refusal, client-visible remotes, and the exception for a session under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`. EG-4 carries `handling`, the asserted route and its pod credential API fields, the inspection stage, the typed `secret`, the one-binding rule, and the granted-entries rule on a binding mint. EG-5 carries the repository in the egress decision API check. EG-9 carries the `userCredentialDelivery` gate, the delegated child exception, and `lockEgressToHosts`. EG-12 carries a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` as a credential pin source. From [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md) and [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md), EG-3 and EG-9 run their pool checks at the post-chain point ("Effective policy", **Where the pool checks run**), and EG-5 and EG-9 key the warm list, its hosts, and the readable set before binding on pool and revision. Neither creates a dependency: until RT-2's post-chain point lands, the checks run on the final pool where the create path checks it today, and until revisions land every runtime has one revision. From [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#changes-requested-of-egress-and-git-designmd), EG-5 carries the mirror's fill causes and the prefetch record's pool, EG-2 carries the Token Service's own check of each cause ("Git mirror (`lenny-git-mirror`)", **Fill causes**), and EG-3 carries the gateway cause assertion and ref resolution after the post-chain point ("Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**). Until RT-2's post-chain point lands, ref resolution runs after the last `PreRoute` segment and the experiment rewrite, where TS-1 runs its final-row checks.

**Requests still open.** These requests from other designs are recorded here and land with the proposal named:

| Request | From | Proposal |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| The decision service honours the outbound gateway's assertion for a tenant's outbound hosts on a probe, and for an instance named by the same identity. | [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`, "Changes requested of egress-and-git-design.md"](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md#changes-requested-of-egress-and-git-designmd) | EG-2, with OC-3 |
| The 1.5-second CONNECT reply budget of the ref resolver depends on the route target kind, if the outbound connector's spike shows that wide-area dials exceed it. | The same | EG-3, after that spike |
| `tenantOverride.outboundHosts` joins the overlay table under "Tenant pool overlays". | The same | EG-9, with OC-2 |
| The outbound gateway's namespace becomes a target of the `lenny-egress-ca` ConfigMap, with the leaf copy and its narrow Secret rights. | The same | EG-11, with OC-1 |
| A binding with `secret: {kind: none}` is refused on a covered or credential-pinned host. | The same | EG-4, with OC-2 |
| `pkg/binding`'s informer covers the outbound gateway's namespace, for the decision service alone. | The same | EG-2, with OC-1 |
| The ref resolver maps a certificate failure after the CONNECT's 200 to `GIT_CLONE_REF_RESOLVE_TRANSIENT`, which **Errors** under "Credentials" omits. | The same | EG-3, with OC-1 |
