| The chart renders an egress PKI leaf for the outbound gateway and one for PgBouncer, whose hop from the outbound gateway uses TLS with `verify-full` ("Transport security", **The egress PKI.**). | The same | EG-11, with OC-1 |

The outbound connector design's request that a ceiling of `*` match outbound connector hosts, which its pool-ceiling decision settled, is applied under "Terms" and "An open ceiling".

The other GitHub and GitLab add-on proposals depend on UC and UG proposals and on no EG proposal directly. [`github-credential-addon-design.md`, "Dependencies"](github-credential-addon-design.md#dependencies) and [`gitlab-credential-addon-design.md`, "Dependencies"](gitlab-credential-addon-design.md#dependencies) state their dependencies.

These orderings with other designs hold, the first two since 2026-11-12 and the last two since 2026-11-13:

| Order | Why |
| :-- | :-- |
| EG-3 lands before RP-B of [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md) | `cmd/git-credential-lenny` dials the platform MCP socket, which RP-B removes, and EG-3 removes `git-credential-lenny`. |
| EG-3's fetch grant lands with the `lenny-materialize` proposal of [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) | The helper's fetches need the grant's treatment, and the grant has no caller without the helper. |
| AR-B of [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) lands before EG-2 | AR-B removes service mode and withdraws this design's proposal 0, so EG-2's `pkg/binding` reads no service-mode pin record and no proposal here changes service-mode code. |
| EG-5's store fill lands with the `lenny-store-fill` container of [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) | The fill runs in that container (decision 61), and `netagent` attributes its UID as the warm fill. |

No other proposal in this design depends on a proposal in another design, apart from the rows above.

## Documentation

The documentation has no owner page for egress or for VCS credentials today. Egress profiles live in a section of the operator guide's security page and are restated, with drift, on several other pages. VCS credentials appear as one paragraph in the concepts page and one row of the workspace plan reference. Lenny's documentation rules say that each concept has one owner page and every other page links to it ([`doc-content.md`](../.claude/rules/doc-content.md)), so this subsystem gets a dedicated chapter, and the existing pages shrink to summaries that link into it. The pages describe behaviour for readers who have not read the specification, with no section numbers, so the proposals' spec changes land before the pages that document them. Each proposal in "Proposal split" carries the documentation changes for its own surface. The chapter's landing page and the egress policy page land with proposal 2.

### New chapter in the operator guide

A chapter titled "Network egress and credentials" sits in the operator guide after "Security". Its landing page states the reading order. The pages below are listed with the subject each owns. The detail they carry comes from this design and the landed specification, and is not repeated here.

| Page | Owns | Notes |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Overview (landing) | The components and the path of a request from a tool in the pod to the internet, to a git host, and through an upstream proxy. Also the security properties in reader terms, and the reading order. | Diagrams, under `doc-diagram-style.md`, of the request path, the decision flow, and the git path. |
| Egress policy | Egress profiles, ceilings, host sets, warm-up policies, tenant egress policies, session host lists, and how the effective policy combines them. | Uses a subset of the scenarios as worked examples. States the exfiltration limit of hostname filtering. Becomes the owner of the content now in the security page's "Network Policies" section. |
| Evidence and visibility | The evidence streams, reading and exporting them, the effective-policy read, the shadow policy, and how to debug a refusal. | States what `self_reported` means, and that an abuse investigation on a shared address needs cloud NAT logs. |
| Tenant configuration | Who manages each setting, tenant pool overlays and their flags, tenant-registered public services, and platform-registered private services. | The reader-facing form of the scope table. |
| Git access | VCS credential pools, provider capability levels, repository prefixes, write scopes, stored-token push and its risks, which hosts are covered and what an uncovered host loses, SSH remotes, `gitAccess`, LFS, and the git mirror (enabling, sizing, prefetch, the fetch retry window, its Pod Security prerequisite, its anti-affinity and node count, what `hostUsers` does and does not protect against with its node requirements, and purge). | Includes a task section for setting up a GitHub App. Replaces the concepts page's description of gateway clones and the in-pod credential helper. |
| Credential bindings | The delivery modes, how to choose between them, placeholders, response scrubbing and its limits, cloud credentials, and the `identityAssertion` kind. | States for `identityAssertion` what [`connector-removal-design.md`, "Changes to Lenny's documentation"](connector-removal-design.md#changes-to-lennys-documentation) lists for this page: `delivery: inject` only, `lifetimeSeconds`, the default `attach` header and the exception to one `inject` binding per host, availability at the `running` commit, the refusal on fallback pools, the impersonation refusal, and the `503` in a Token Service outage. Links to that design's verifier page for operator services. Distinguishes credential bindings from account links (UC-2) and from LLM upstream records (LU-1), and links both. |
| Upstream proxies and corporate gateways | Chaining, egress groups and their grants, TLS interception and CA distribution, and replacing the default proxy with one that speaks the decision protocol. | States which identity methods each documented gateway accepts, as recorded in this design. |
| Level 1 proxies | The proxy contract, the policy document and the EDL output, identities and credential rotation, the probe, and the settings that select level 1. | Opens by stating that level 1 is not the recommended path. It then gives the table of what level 1 gives up against the default and level 2, the reasons, and when level 1 fits, and it directs most deployers to level 2 or chaining. Every page that describes a guarantee level 1 weakens links here. Runtimes need no change at level 1, and the runtime author guide says so. |
| Deploying and operating | CNI requirements, including AWS VPC CNI strict mode and the kindnet limits. Also the egress PKI (issuance, supplying Secrets without cert-manager, rotation, the readiness probe, and the expiry alert) and the `netagent` CPU limit rule. Also Envoy sizing (circuit breakers, timeouts, and the tunnel lifetime against LLM stream length), version pinning and advisories, metrics and alerts, and troubleshooting. | Troubleshooting covers a 403 traced through the decision log, a failed in-pod clone, and a tool that ignores the proxy. Links to the runbooks. |

### Runtime author guide

The runtime author guide gets one new page, "Network and credentials in the pod", which owns the runtime's view of the subsystem:

- the launch variables and the fixed endpoints;
- the `/run/netagent` layout and the network descriptor;
- the tools that need extra configuration, and baking tool configuration into an image;
- keeping the proxy and trust variables for spawned commands;
- git from inside the pod, including the pod trust bundle in git's trust, git in the image for `gh` even with `-R`, the libgit2 proxy option and hang on a constraint mismatch, JGit's lack of constraint enforcement, and the SSH failure on an uncovered host;
- credential bindings as the runtime sees them;
- the SDK accessor;
- what a runtime on a pod with several slots shares with the other slots, what `netagent` attributes to its slot, and the per-slot scopes with their fallback on a runtime class without attribution;
- the rendered git configuration (`GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/run/netagent/gitconfig`), its `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`, and how a runtime overrides them at the cost of its own next checkpoint;
- the name-constrained trust anchor, which every agent pod carries, and what an enforcing client refuses;
- reaching an operator service, such as an MCP server, through the proxy: which proxy and trust variable each measured harness and MCP SDK reads ("Injected configuration", **Harness clients.**), the vendor hosts Claude Code and Codex contact at start with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1` for Claude Code, and the `codex mcp list` probe that fails closed, with the rest of the pattern on the page [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) adds;
- the egress halt: a slot's requests are refused with `503` from a suspension that cannot take hold until the slot's release or its resumption on the same pod, and egress traffic does not count as session activity;
- behaviour under local development.

Existing pages change as follows:

| Page | Change |
| :-- | :-- |
| `index.md` | The reference runtime table loses the category-keyed framing ("same workspace layout, same pre-installed toolchains, same sandbox profile"). The `coding` scaffold template description drops platform defaults. The pod diagram gains `netagent`. Links to the new page. |
| `runtime-configuration.md` | `defaultPoolConfig` lists `proxied` and drops `provider-direct`. The `env` field states which variable names are reserved on pools with `egressProfile: proxied`. |
| `lifecycle.md` | **Runtime Supervisor → environment** adds the launch variables. **Filesystem Layout** adds `/run/netagent`. **Workspace Materialization** describes the clone by `lenny-materialize` in the slot instead of gateway streaming for `gitClone` sources. The warm-up checkout notes that warm-up egress applies before claim. |
| `local-development.md` | States how `lenny up`, `make run`, and the compose stack run `netagent` and the egress proxy, and which enforcement is absent locally. |
| `integration-levels.md`, or the capabilities page that replaces it under [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md) | States that the network and credential mechanisms work for every runtime, whatever capabilities it declares, and need no capability-specific code. |
| `runtime-sdk.md` and `sdk-examples/` | Document the network accessor, with one example per language. |
| `publishing.md` | The pool configuration table gains the egress fields, or links to the egress policy page. |
| `testing.md` | Describes the new conformance suite for network and credentials. |
| `platform-tools.md` | No change. `lenny/vcs_token` was never documented. |

### Facts the pages state

Under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13 (IC D13) that every design's documentation lists every setting, default, node requirement, limit, failure mode, and runtime-facing rule it introduces, the pages above carry at least the following. Each item names the page that owns it.

**Settings and defaults** (operator guide "Deploying and operating", "Egress policy", "Git access", "Level 1 proxies", and `reference/configuration.md`):

- the pool fields `egressProfile` (`restricted`, `proxied`, or `internet`), the egress ceiling, the warm-up policy, `gitAccess` (any host by default), the fetch retry window (180 s by default, below `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` and `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` less 60 s), the tunnel lifetime chosen from the chart's menu (15 minutes, 1 hour, 2 hours, and 8 hours by default), and `tenantOverride` flags, which default to `narrow`;
- a `gitRepository` warm entry, the only way to declare a store, and its `prestage: true`, with adoption by the first `gitClone` source of the repository in the session's workspace (decision 67);
- the VCS pool fields under "VCS pool fields", including `allowedPushOptions` (empty by default), `allowWorkflowEdits`, and GitLab token reuse with its 7-day rotation;
- the tenant settings: tenant egress policy (the shipped default policy allows nothing beyond the default host sets), tenant pool overlays, routes, upstreams, endpoints, egress group assignments (exclusive by default, with a 30-day address cool-off), and the shadow policy (7 days by default);
- the per-tenant gates `storedTokenPush`, `agentReadableDelivery`, `userCredentialDelivery`, and `verbatimClientFiles`;
- the `identityAssertion` binding's `lifetimeSeconds` (300 by default, from 60 to 900) and default `attach` header (`Authorization: Bearer {{secret}}`), the Helm values `tokenService.identityAssertion.keyRotationDays` (30 by default) and `tokenService.identityAssertion.deploymentKeySet.enabled` (`true` by default; with `false` the deployment key set route answers `404`), the key set cache period (300 s), and the `HaltSessionEgress` deadline (5 s);
- the Helm values: `centralProxy.caBundle`, `centralProxy.serverName`, `centralProxy.level2.pinnedDestinations` (`false` by default), `egressCIDRs.excludePrivate`, `gitMirror.hostUsers` (`true` by default), `gitMirror.heldUidBudget` (a quarter of the mirror's fetch-UID range by default, decision 66), `tokenService.mirrorFillLifetimeSeconds` (3600 by default), `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries`, the mirror's 6-hour confirmation limit, the level 1 credential rotation (24 hours with a 1-hour overlap) and policy document lifetime (1 hour), `netagent.resources` and `storeFill.resources`, the CPU and memory requests of `netagent` and `lenny-store-fill`, which the class mapping of [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md) counts within the pool's resource class (`storeFill.resources` defaults to a request of 500m CPU and 512 MiB with a 1 GiB memory limit, the values [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`, "What the documentation states"](agent-pod-layout-design.md#what-the-documentation-states) states, which the owner confirmed on 2026-11-13; `netagent.resources` defaults to a request of 100m CPU and 128 MiB with a 512 MiB memory limit and no CPU limit, and `netagent`'s memory-backed volumes take a `sizeLimit` of 16 MiB for its private emptyDir, 16 MiB for its pod-wide volume, and 8 MiB for each `slot-<n>-netcred`, decision 66), and the LLM proxy host;
- the fixed internal values a deployer cannot change but needs for debugging: the binding re-read age and the decision cache TTL (30 s each), the `netagent` head read deadline (10 s), the label-visibility retry (5 s), the resubscription limit (one per 10 s per pod) and its 30 s tunnel grace, the service account token lifetime (600 s), the 15-minute cache lifetime of a stored secret, the 30 s fetch wait on a resume, and Envoy's 15 s `listener_filters_timeout`.

**Node and cluster requirements** ("Deploying and operating" and `operator-guide/installation.md`): a CNI that enforces NetworkPolicy (AWS VPC CNI strict mode, and the kindnet limits); kube-proxy that does not masquerade pod sources, or the peer-address check disabled with a warning; cluster OIDC discovery for the offline token check; the mirror's `baseline` Pod Security prerequisite, its anti-affinity against the Token Service and the decision service, and the node and Kubernetes version that `gitMirror.hostUsers: false` needs, with the check in the running pod; Envoy 1.39.6 or later; and, for per-slot scopes, a runtime class on which `netagent`'s `sock_diag` or `/proc/net/tcp` lookup answers, which preflight reports per class: runc answers through `sock_diag`, and gVisor and Kata through `/proc/net/tcp`. The deployer guide recommends building the Kata guest kernel with `CONFIG_INET_DIAG` and `CONFIG_INET_TCP_DIAG`, states that it is a recommendation rather than a requirement, and states that without those options Kata pools attribute through `/proc/net/tcp`, which accepts connections more slowly under a burst (decision 68).

**Limits** (the owning page of each mechanism): hostname filtering cannot stop data leaving through an allowed host that accepts uploads; a pool with `egressProfile: internet` bypasses `netagent`; level 1 holds P1, P5, and P7 in weaker form; opaque tunnels and the alias limit on uncovered hosts; the union scope, E = ∅, and the refusal of `egress.allowedHosts` and `egressLockHosts` on a pool whose runtime class lacks slot attribution, under `acknowledgeSharedPod`, which `sessionIsolationLevel.perSlotScopes` reports as `false`; a mid-session overlay's fetch window shared with the session's processes; tunnels to an old egress group running to the pool's cap; the API server outage bound, after which every pod's egress fails closed about 600 s in; the egress halt's absence on a fallback pool and its lapse between a `netagent` restart's ready report and the adapter's reconnection; the deployment key set's disclosure of every live tenant's identifier to anyone who reaches the `/identity/` path; and the absence of any per-call record or tool policy in Lenny for a call to an operator service.

**Failure modes** (troubleshooting and the error catalog): every egress component failing closed (P6); `netagent` refusing before its first binding answer and after a restart; a 403 with an `egress.local` record for an unattributed loopback connection; `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` on a pool without attribution, for an `egress.allowedHosts` list, an `egressLockHosts` limit, or an `onRequest` or `poolAttach` binding, and on a pod with several slots and attribution, for a session whose environment denies the host of a warm-list entry, as [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decision 41](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#decisions) settles; a CONNECT carrying the platform marker refused at a destination other than the gateway, object storage, or the git mirror; a new pod that fails readiness because its `netagent` finds no attribution reader on a pool whose status records attribution, its replacement under backoff, and the `NetagentAttributionUnavailable` alert; the `git_fetch_pending` materialization failure after the fetch retry window; `GIT_CLONE_REF_UNRESOLVABLE` with its `egress_denied` and `no_egress_path` reasons and `GIT_CLONE_REF_RESOLVE_TRANSIENT`; `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` for `git_tree_limit`; a setup command on a snapshotless rebuild that needs a write token or an agent-readable credential sending the session to `awaiting_client_action`; evidence gap records, and a regulated tenant's CONNECT refused when its record cannot commit; `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` for an `identityAssertion` binding whose delivery is not `inject` or that shares a host with another `inject` binding under the same header name; `422 IDENTITY_ASSERTION_UNSUPPORTED_POD` on a fallback pool; `403 FORBIDDEN` with the reason `impersonation_identity_assertion`; a `503` from `netagent` for a bound host when the Token Service is unavailable and the cached assertion has expired, and for every request of a halted slot; and a `lenny-store-fill` failure, which keeps the pod from reporting ready.

**Runtime-facing rules** (the runtime author guide page): the fixed proxy endpoint `127.0.0.1:3128` and the launch variables, with the reserved variable names, and the session launch variables the supervisor adds from the binding list; `/run/netagent` and its files (the pod trust bundle, the pod-wide git configuration, the network descriptor, and the per-slot credential directory at `/run/netagent/credentials` with the session's binding list `bindings.json`, its git configuration file, which selects a commit identity file per VCS pool by the repository's remote URL and applies only with git 2.36 or later, and the `netrc` file that `NETRC` always names), all read-only to the runtime; the placeholder `lenny-injected` under `delivery: inject`; the git configuration in the system scope and the runtime's right to override its maintenance settings; the removal of `git-credential-lenny` and `lenny/vcs_token`; the constrained trust anchor and the clients that enforce constraints; and that the runtime needs no capability, SDK, or code change for any of these mechanisms.

### Existing inconsistencies to fix in the same change

- Agent DNS is described three ways: as a dedicated CoreDNS in the agent namespaces (the security page), as `lenny-coredns` in `lenny-system` (the DNS outage runbook), and as `lenny-agent-dns` (the alert rules). The pages adopt the specification's name, and they add the `proxied` DNS resolver beside it.
- `api/mcp.md` shows a workspace source of type `git`. The type is `gitClone`, and the MCP adapter's documentation, which replaces the page under [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md), carries the correction.
- `GIT_CLONE_AUTH_UNSUPPORTED_HOST` and `GIT_CLONE_AUTH_HOST_AMBIGUOUS` appear in the REST pages and are missing from the error catalog.
- `llm-egress-anomaly.md` uses a `lenny-ctl admin egress-policies` command that the CLI page does not document. Its name collides with the new tenant egress policies, so one of them is renamed.
- `pool_admission_isolation_test.go` in tier 9 checks the `provider-direct` rejection. It changes with the profile's removal, together with the security page's text.

### Documentation tests

The tier 11 tests that check phrases and paths on these pages constrain the edits, and they change in the same change as the pages:

- the credential path sweeps (`credentialsPath` and the slot credential file as the only credential path), with which the `/run/netagent` credential directory must stay consistent;
- the filesystem layout reconciliation for `lifecycle.md`;
- the runbook structure, trigger, step, and index-count tests;
- the alert-to-runbook resolution;
- the chaos runbook map, which needs an entry for each alert-driven runbook;
- the code-block parser, which every new YAML and JSON example must pass;
- the tier 0 fragment-link test, which guards the inbound anchors listed above.

## Tests

Each proposal in "Proposal split" carries the tests for its own surface, at the tiers that `test-coverage.md` maps that surface to. This section states what the tests must establish. The proposals turn it into test lists with `// spec:` annotations, and the implementation workflow writes and runs them. The validation rounds in this document are design evidence only. They ran against stand-ins such as mitmproxy and throwaway fakes, so each behaviour they established is tested again against the Lenny components.

### Tier 0: static

- The Envoy bootstrap that the chart renders passes Envoy's own configuration validation for each supported combination: the default, chaining, the server-name check, and ext_authz in HTTP mode.
- The schemas of the new CRD fields and admin API resources validate, and so do the network descriptor, the binding list, and `binding.json`.
- Every flag the pod-spec renderer emits for a container is defined by that container's binary. This turns the environment-preflight check on flag drift into a static test.
- No code path renders a secret into an environment variable or a command-line argument of `netagent` or of the runtime.

### Tier 1: unit

- **Fetch grants.** A grant names one slot, the normalized URLs, and a window that ends at the run's deadline. A second grant for a slot that holds one, a grant for an unknown slot, and a grant whose URL list passes the bind bounds are refused. A fetch of a named URL from the granted slot inside the window takes the materialization rules, and the same fetch after `revoke`, after the window, from another slot, or of an unnamed URL takes the session's rules. A granted URL outside the session's readable set is refused.
- **Slot attribution.** A connection maps to its slot through the owner UID and `pkg/binding`'s slot index, the adapter UID and `netagent`'s UID map to platform connections, whose CONNECTs carry `x-lenny-platform` and are admitted only to the gateway, object storage, and the git mirror, a slot connection whose request carries `x-lenny-platform` leaves `netagent` without it, `lenny-store-fill`'s UID maps to the warm fill before READY and is refused after it, and an unknown UID is refused with an `egress.local` record, as is an unanswered lookup on a pool whose status records per-slot attribution; on a fallback pool no connection is attributed and each takes the union scope. A reader's answer in any state other than `ESTABLISHED`, such as `FIN_WAIT2` with UID 0, or with a UID outside `netagent`'s configuration, is refused. Without `sock_diag`, accepts that complete before a read of `/proc/net/tcp` starts are all resolved from that one read, and an accept that completes during it waits for the next read. The start-up check passes a reader that answers its own connection with `netagent`'s UID and `ESTABLISHED`, fails one that is refused, finds no row, or answers another UID or state, reports attribution as answering when one reader passes, and uses only the readers that passed. No listener binds `::1`.
- **Per-slot scopes.** On a pod with two slots, each slot's connection is decided against its own session's readable and writable sets, E, `egress.allowedHosts`, `egressLockHosts`, and bindings, a cached credential or decision of one slot's session serves no connection of the other slot, a CONNECT whose `x-lenny-session` names a session the pod's binding does not list is refused, and a slot release flushes that session's cache entries and closes its tunnels. On a pool marked as lacking attribution, the union scope, E = ∅, and the refusals of the fallback apply, and a pool with no recorded result is treated the same way at the post-chain point. A pod whose `netagent` finds no reader on a pool marked as answering attribution never reports READY and is replaced under backoff, with the pool's status unchanged, and consecutive failures raise `NetagentAttributionUnavailable`. On a fallback pool, a session naming an `onRequest` binding or attaching a `poolAttach` binding is refused at the post-chain point, a `scope: always` binding applies pod-wide, a fetch grant names the pod, and a change of the runtime class or the `netagent` image clears the pool's status.
- **Per-session files.** On a pod with two slots, each slot's `bindings.json`, git configuration file, `netrc` file, and session launch variables carry its own session's values, the pod-wide `network.json` and git configuration name no session, the supervisor sets a `federation` binding's variables at launch from the slot's binding list, `NETRC` is set on every pod and names an empty file when the session has no Basic binding, and the adapter's wait reads the applied coordination generation from `netagent`'s `applied` message on `CH-FETCHGRANT`.
- **Commit identity per VCS pool.** With repositories under two VCS pools, the session's file includes each pool's identity file under `includeIf "hasconfig:remote.*.url:<pattern>"`, a commit with the pinned git in each repository takes its own pool's author and committer, an SSH remote form matches as its HTTPS form does, and a repository whose remotes match no pool's prefix takes no identity from the files (decision 65).
- **Rendered git configuration.** The file carries `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`, and a repository-level `gc.auto`, a global `maintenance.auto`, or a `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` entry overrides it (IC D6).
- **Effective policy.** The tests cover each binding state under "Effective policy":
  - ceilings, including `*` and its match of a host the tenant routes to its own outbound connector;
  - the warm-list hosts of the pod's pool and revision, so a pod of one revision's pod set reaches no host that only another revision's warm list names;
  - tenant pool overlays under each `tenantOverride` value;
  - the precedence of denied hosts;
  - the validation of `egress.allowedHosts` at create.
- **Host matching.** The tests cover:
  - wildcards, ports, letter case, internationalized names, and trailing dots;
  - IP literals in canonical and non-canonical forms (decimal, octal, hexadecimal, IPv4-mapped, and NAT64);
  - the lower-casing of authorities that the server-name check depends on.
- **Repository handling in `netagent`.** The tests cover:
  - the per-host rules the git configuration renders for each remote form;
  - `sshForms` host and path templates, and a covered host with no entry rendering no rule;
  - encoded and traversing path segments;
  - readable and writable set enforcement;
  - the redirect rules;
  - classification on every terminated host, on the decoded, case-folded path before any filter: a canonical git path takes the git path, a marker match in any other spelling is refused, including `git-receive-pack/`, `.json`, `//`, and `%2d`, and any other path on the host takes `inject` or is forwarded unchanged;
  - a bound host with no pool: a fetch carries no credential, and a push is refused;
  - one `service=` form, override headers, a compressed `git-receive-pack`, and a `push-cert`, each refused;
  - LFS batch responses: every credential header name removed from each action, in any case and under `_links`, attached on a matching transfer alone, an unparseable response refused, an undeliverable action replaced with a per-object `error`, repeated failures refused, and `info/lfs` refused on `mint-coarse` and `stored` providers;
  - the routing override list refused on every terminated host, and the stated false positives on API hosts;
  - coverage matching under the canonicalizer, on any port, with alias hosts;
  - `allowWorkflowEdits` refused on a provider with no such permission.
- **Minting.** The tests cover token cache keys, refresh timing, and per-App rate accounting.
- **Setup bound.** A base Runtime write that raises the setup bound is refused when the fetch retry window of any pool that reads the bound, platform or tenant-owned, no longer fits, and the refusal names the pools and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` at which they fit. A template write that raises the window is refused the same way, and a tenant's write that raises its derived runtime's setup bound past its owned pool's window is refused to the tenant.
- **Binding feed.** The tests cover notification handling, read-through on a cache miss, the policy-version cache key, and the coordination generation rule for a doubly bound session.
- **Private destination pinning.** The tests cover the registration-time resolution and refusal rules, the periodic re-resolution, and that no private address ever reaches Envoy's name resolution path.
- **Credential attachment.** The tests cover:
  - `attach` templates for Bearer, Basic, and custom headers;
  - header overwrite whatever the placeholder holds;
  - stripping of hop-by-hop headers.
- **Response scrubbing.** The tests cover:
  - whole bodies under each content encoding;
  - the sliding window at every split offset of the secret;
  - WebSocket frames and header values.
  - The unscrubbed base64 and hexadecimal reflections are asserted as unscrubbed, so the documented limit is tested rather than implied.
- **File delivery.** The tests cover the atomic directory swap, values written without a trailing newline, the `binding.json` schema, and the `netrc` projection.
- **Launch configuration.** The tests cover the launch variables, the fully qualified `NO_PROXY` list, and CA bundle assembly from image roots, deployer CA, and per-pod CA.
- **SDK accessors.** In each language, the tests cover descriptor parsing, the bounded retry for a session entry that is not yet written, and `env()` and `credential(name)`, which re-read on every call.
- **Identity assertions and the egress halt.** The `identityAssertion` binding's validation: a delivery other than `inject` refused, the `lifetimeSeconds` bounds, the default `attach` header, and the O5 exception with its refusal when the header names match. The claim set built from a session record for a user and for a service principal, with and without an environment, at depth 0 and 2, and the cache key and refresh at two thirds of the lifetime. `netagent`'s halt: a halted slot's new request is answered `503`, its open tunnel closes, a platform connection keeps running, a second `halt_slot` is answered `halted`, `resume_slot` restores egress, and `@lenny-netagent-halt` refuses a peer other than the adapter UID. [`connector-removal-design.md`, "Tests"](connector-removal-design.md#tests) holds the tests of the reference verifier, of the tenant key sets and the deployment key set, and of their rotation.

### Tier 2: component

- **Pod spec (envtest).** The tests cover:
  - `netagent` as a native sidecar with its own UID and a chart-set resource request that the class mapping subtracts from the pool's resource class, so the pod's effective request equals the class;
  - the projected token volume, with the two deployment-specific audiences (§10.3), mounted into `netagent` only;
  - `/run/netagent`, read-write for `netagent` and read-only for every slot container, the adapter, and `lenny-store-fill`, mounted by no other container, with files `0444` and directories `0755`, as PL-3 and PL-5 render it;
  - the pod-wide `network.json` and git configuration naming no session, and each slot's binding list, git configuration file, `netrc` file, and session launch variables on that slot's `slot-<n>-netcred` alone;
  - one credentials volume per slot, `slot-<n>-netcred`, memory-backed with a `sizeLimit` of 8 MiB, mounted read-write into `netagent` and read-only into that slot's container alone;
  - `netagent`'s private emptyDir and its pod-wide volume, each memory-backed with its `sizeLimit`;
  - the launch variables in the supervisor environment of every runtime container, `NETRC` among them on every pod, and the session launch variables the supervisor adds from the slot's binding list at launch;
  - the rejection of reserved variable names in a Runtime `env` on pools with `egressProfile: proxied`;
  - the labels for `proxied` and `gitAccess`.
- **Admission.** The tests cover:
  - the `egressProfile` write restriction for a tenant admin on a shared pool;
  - overlay validation against the `tenantOverride` flags;
  - label immutability with the new profile value;
  - the rejection of the removed `provider-direct` value.
- **`lenny-egress-authz`.** The tests cover:
  - the pod informer, its projection, and binding resolution from the pod labels, the pool record, the tenant pin, and the session store;
  - fail-closed behaviour before the informer syncs, and the stale-cache bound during API server loss;
  - token validation, including the rejection of a deleted pod's token;
  - cache invalidation on a binding change and on a binding definition edit.
- **`lenny-git-mirror` in one container.** The tests cover:
  - store separation per authorization boundary: an object known by SHA in one tenant's store is not served from another tenant's;
  - `fsckObjects` rejection of malformed packs;
  - serialized fetches per repository;
  - per-store ownership: a fetch subprocess can write its own store and no other, a public-store `upload-pack` cannot write, a tenant-store `upload-pack` cannot write where ACLs exist, a second fetch under another UID can write the first fetch's packs, and the parent re-chowns after each fetch;
  - the ambient-capability start: the parent is non-root with exactly three capabilities and its `fsGroup`, and the fetch child has none;
  - UID retirement: a process a fetch leaves behind is killed at fetch exit, before the re-chown and before the fetch is reported complete, a UID returns to the pool only after the sweep is confirmed and the re-chown completes, a fetch waits when no UID is free, a restart re-chowns stores with fetches in flight, and a survivor cannot write the store afterwards or connect to a later fetch's forwarder;
  - the same boundaries with `hostUsers` at each value, every allocated ID inside the pod's range, and on-disk owners unchanged across a switch between the values;
  - with `hostUsers: false`, the mirror refuses to start when UID 0 maps to node UID 0;
  - the mirror is never scheduled beside the Token Service or the decision service outside `global.devMode`;
  - the re-chown walk ignores a planted symlink and changes owners only;
  - the prefetch request, including refusal for `direct`-routed repositories;
  - purge.
- **`netagent` in one container.** The tests cover the CA kept across a restart, the readiness endpoint, and failing closed when the decision service is unreachable.

### Tier 3: contract

- The egress decision API (I3) in its gRPC and HTTP/2 forms, between Envoy and `lenny-egress-authz`. The check carries the authority, `Proxy-Authorization`, and the peer address. The response carries the route header and the dynamic metadata, and removes `Proxy-Authorization` on chained routes.
- The pod binding API (I7): the binding subscription, the proxy Service to dial, the covered host set, the base-destination list, local decision reports, and error codes.
- The pod credential API (I8): credential requests on the `lenny-token-service` audience token, the Token Service's own verification of that token with the peer-address check from its own pod informer, its binding re-read, and its scope call on the egress decision API carrying the pod UID and peer address without the token. A request relayed through any identity other than the pod's or the mirror's client certificate is refused, and the mirror identity obtains read tokens only, never `stored` credentials.
- The git endpoint (I5): git smart HTTP through `netagent` and the mirror, and protocol v2.
- The network descriptor and the credential directory, as a versioned contract that the SDKs consume.
- The OIDC discovery document and JWKS of the Token Service, and the claims of the federation JWT.
- The `metadata` formats, including the AWS container credentials response and its token-file authentication.

### Tier 4: integration

- **Git end to end on the compose stack.** A local git server and the mirror sit behind `netagent`, with git on OpenSSL and GnuTLS builds trusting the pod trust bundle. The tests cover:
  - clone and fetch, including shallow and partial clones;
  - push with a token minted from a fake GitHub App API;
  - a push outside the write scope refused with no mint call made;
  - the read-after-push refresh;
  - LFS batch and transfer on a separate storage host, submodules on covered and uncovered hosts, and redirects;
  - a clone `lenny-materialize` runs as the slot UID under a fetch grant, with the index refreshed more than 1 s after checkout and no duplicate loose objects in the slot, and the same repository's restore completion after a resume;
  - a store fill that `lenny-store-fill` runs with the pinned git before READY under its own UID, which `netagent` attributes as the warm fill, with stores and pristine trees readable by every slot UID through the other class, and the adapter's deletion of a withheld store through the store group, which is its primary group and which no slot container holds;
  - a warm fill and a mirror route, with the host in the CA, and a re-issue reaching the next run;
  - go-git, JGit, and libgit2 with a proxy, and a libgit2 client under a constraint mismatch within its timeout;
  - `gh` and `glab` with unchanged remotes.
- **Client compatibility matrix for `inject`.** Real client tools in containers run against `netagent` and a fake upstream with a test CA. The tools are curl, Python requests, httpx over HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2, aiohttp, Node fetch, Go, Java HttpClient, a rustls-based client, `gh`, `glab`, npm, pip, Maven, and Gradle. For each tool the matrix covers:
  - injection on a bound host and passthrough on an unbound host;
  - placeholder handling;
  - the plain-HTTP upgrade where the tool supports it;
  - scrubbing of reflected secrets;
  - SSE and WebSocket streaming.

  This matrix replaces the round 3 mitmproxy evidence with evidence about `netagent` itself, and it records the latency and memory figures that round 3 could not give.
- **`federation` delivery.** Each supported cloud SDK runs against a fake provider token endpoint and a fake service, exchanges the Lenny JWT, signs with the result, and re-reads a rotated token. The Azure case runs on a tenant-owned pool and through constructor arguments.
- **`metadata` delivery.** Each supported cloud SDK runs against the `netagent` plugin and a fake service, for each shipped format.
- **Minting.** The Token Service runs against a fake GitHub API. The tests cover the `repositories` and `permissions` parameters, cache hits, refresh before expiry, rate accounting, and error mapping.
- **Session create.** The tests cover validation of `egress.allowedHosts`, `writableRepositories`, and credential bindings, including the rejection of `egress.allowedHosts` and per-session bindings on a pool with several slots whose runtime class lacks slot attribution and their acceptance where it has it, and the synchronous `ls-refs` resolution with its two error codes.
- **Pool checks after the `PreRoute` chain.** A route or a `PreRoute` interceptor moves a session onto a pool with no git path while its plan has a `gitClone` source, onto a pool with several slots whose runtime class lacks slot attribution while it carries `egress.allowedHosts` or `egressLockHosts`, onto a pool with `egressProfile: internet` while it carries `egressLockHosts`, or onto a pool whose `gitAccess` set omits a `gitClone` host. Each is refused at the post-chain point with the check's own code and `details.interceptor_ref` naming the last modifier, for `kind: create`, `kind: delegate`, and `kind: derive`, and the gateway does not fall back to the requested pool. The same session on a runtime and pool that no interceptor changed is refused by the same checks.
- **LFS upload authorization.** An LFS batch with `operation: upload` takes the write path and is refused outside the writable set.
- **Bind race.** The first pod-side request after a bind commit succeeds through the read-through, under an injected notification delay.
- **Platform traffic under the launch variables.** Checkpoint uploads, the gateway link, and the LLM proxy path reach their endpoints directly in both pod models.
- **Agent git configuration.** `git config --global` succeeds in the pod, the image's global and system configs apply, and Lenny's rules follow them.
- **Resume with `gitClone`.** A resumed session with a `gitClone` source materializes through the provisional binding.
- **Write scope per slot.** On a pod with two slots, a slot's push to a repository only the other slot's session declared writable is refused with no mint. On a pool whose runtime class lacks slot attribution, the union applies, and it shrinks when a session ends.
- **Fetch grant on materialization.** A first materialization whose repository is still filling in the mirror holds for the pool's fetch retry window, a resume's restore completion waits 30 s at most, and an agent's fetch of the same URL after `setupdone` gets the session's rules. The `git.operation` records name materialization for the helper's fetches alone. `CH-FETCHGRANT` refuses a connection from any UID other than the adapter's.
- **`ls-refs` under tenant routes.** Resolution goes through a private git endpoint and an egress group, with the v0 fallback, and a hostile host hits the byte and time caps. The mirror case, including a repository the mirror has never fetched, lands with proposal 5.
- **Evidence streams.** Each stream's record carries the envelope and its named fields, batches and manifests join to the chain's anchor, a tampered batch fails verification, and a producer past its per-tenant buffer writes a gap record and raises the alert without affecting another tenant. A killed ingest replica produces a gap record for each tenant's unflushed range and a loss window for `egress.accesslog`, a frozen replica's later flush is delivered despite the gap record, a dropped batch fails the verifier, a destroyed held key is refused, a destroyed session key stops ingest using its cached copy, and interleaved sessions on a per-session stream hide no lost chunk. A regulated tenant's CONNECT has a committed record before it is allowed, with the store and the service failing, and is refused past 150 ms. A new stream registers with no change to the store or to export. A decision joins its tunnel record through the decision identifier, and a platform query by address, port, target, and time finds a closed session's tenant after its pod is gone. The compactor keeps the manifest at its stated size, and the chart's drain delivers close records.
- **Evidence trust.** A `netagent` report claiming `enforced`, another producer, tenant, or session is overwritten or rejected, a producer writing a stream outside its SAN binding is refused, no component but `lenny-evidence` holds a store credential, and records carry no query strings and bounded authorities.
- **Tenant visibility.** The decision query returns only the caller's tenant, and the effective-policy read matches actual decisions. A shadow policy records its outcome, in either direction, never changes one, and expires.
- **Push limits.** A push with any ref outside `allowedRefPatterns` is refused whole, a ref deletion is refused unless allowed, and each push writes a `git.operation` record.
- **Egress group assignment.** An assignment through the API stamps new pods and leaves running pods on the default Envoy. A revocation fails a stamped pod's egress closed, and claim and rebind refuse and drain a mismatched pod. Only the gateway and the controller can set the label, once. A group cannot go to a second tenant without the sharing setting and its notice, and a released address waits out its cool-off.
- **Mint path.** A mint for a private host goes through `lenny-egress` under that tenant's route, a mint response past its bounds is refused, and a host that copies another tenant's identifiers does not receive that tenant's cached token.
- **Reserved hooks.** A `quarantined` binding refuses egress, mints, and mirror reads, whatever other state applies. A held store is skipped by re-chown, repack, and purge, and its owning UIDs stay out of the pool across a restart, until `gitMirror.heldUidBudget` is reached. A hold past the budget writes an ownership manifest that names each file's owning UID and then re-chowns the store, and its UIDs return to the pool. A `suspendedBy` object acts as absent in every component, apart from the qualification under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`, a writable entry that is not `granted` is read only, `userRefused` and an unknown state on a readable entry deny reads, and the one tenant-object resolver filters nothing until [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping) (environment scoping). A pod credential API response's `cacheUntil` bounds `netagent`'s hold, an image refuses a `handling` value it does not implement, the pre-mint step runs before the mint cache lookup, the inspection stage admits everything with no rules, `poolAttach` applies per the attach rule, a write refuses differing flavours on one host, the decision service refuses a CONNECT whose asserted route differs and leaves an App or project token mint with no route assertion, on the Initech topology and the connector topology, `netagent` strips that header from agent input, and an image refuses a release naming a `handling` value it lacks. Every opt-in write passes the one path and emits its audit event, and the egress Certificates carry the `doctor` opt-out.
- **`ls-refs` on the egress path.** Calls alternating between tenants produce one decision per call. A stopped replica and a frozen replica each cost one retry with no failed call. Proxy and decision service outages return the transient error within their bounds, and a denial returns `egress_denied`. A `.git` redirect is followed once and every other redirect is refused. The v0 path stops at its match and hits its cap on an oversized advertisement.
- **`ls-refs` through the mirror.** The answer tracks the upstream ahead of the store. Each listed cause answers from the store within its age limit and reports `resolvedFromStore` on the session, and past the limit it returns the transient error. A host 401, 403, 404, or 429 and a CONNECT 403 or 502 never fall back. A decision service outage returns the transient error, and identical calls coalesce. A pinned SHA the store lacks schedules a fetch, under the negative cache, the per-tenant rate, and the minimum interval. A host 401 or a refused mint clears confirmations, a later 503 does not serve from the store, and a confirmed commit the store lacks returns the transient error. Confirmations survive a mirror restart.
- **Mirror fill causes.** A fill names a pod UID, a gateway cause assertion, or a prefetch record. The Token Service refuses a cause that is not live, a cause from another tenant, an assertion with a bad signature or past its 60-second expiry, a pool other than the one the shared selection function picks for the upstream and E, and a prefetch pool other than the record's. A cause from another tenant also writes an audit event and raises the alert. A warm pod selects at E = ∅. On a pod with several slots, a fill selects at the environment of the session `netagent` attributed the fetch to, and the Token Service refuses a fill naming a session that the pod's binding does not hold; on a pod whose pool's status records slot attribution as unavailable, a fill selects at E = ∅. A fill accepted at its first mint returns a fill ID and keeps minting on that ID after its pod is gone, until the fill lifetime ends, and a fill ID presented with another mirror identity, tenant, upstream, or pool, or after the lifetime, is refused. A prefetch record whose pool changes `environments` or prefixes, or is deleted, is void before its fill. A prefetch request that omits the pool stores the one covering pool, and is refused when several pools cover the repository.
- **Ref resolution after the post-chain point.** On `POST /v1/sessions`, `POST /v1/sessions/start`, derive, and delegate, a route or `PreRoute` interceptor that changes the pool changes the pool the gateway cause assertion names, a bad ref is refused with no pod claimed, and the assertion carries the session's settled E. At finalize, a bad ref fails the finalize on the pod claimed at create, and the assertion carries E from the session row. On the direct path, the decision service reads E from the assertion.
- **Private destinations.** At the default level a pod and the ref resolver reach one through a raw tunnel, an in-cluster service works through its selector peer, a pool with `egressProfile: restricted` and `gitAccess` reaches only its git hosts under the `gitAccess` formula of "Effective policy", including LFS when bound and the warm case without it, and never an arbitrary host through the default route, with fetch and push from stock git, a raw socket from any pod is dropped, and an ungranted tenant is refused. Validators refuse private destinations for a level 2 proxy that declares no pinned dialing. At level 1 only labelled pods of granted tenants have a path, `inject` and LFS work on the private host, a Helm revocation removes the path, and the gateway and the mirror refuse a decision address that differs from their registration read.
- **Label visibility.** A request that reaches a component before its informer sees the tenant label is retried, succeeds once the label appears, and fails closed if it never does. 
- **Pool names.** A deleted pool's name is refused for a new pool, deletion cascades to its overlays, grants, and level 1 identities, and an unlabelled pod is refused on a tenant-owned pool.
- **Level 1 on Squid.** A reference Squid and sync job hold per-identity lists, credential generation overlap and retirement, and edit propagation. The ref resolver and the mirror work through it, `netagent` refuses a CONNECT the decision denies before it dials, a fronted tunnel is closed, and the validators refuse chaining, egress groups, and private destinations through the proxy. Each pod state in the identity table receives its stated identity, and a private git endpoint works for pods, the ref resolver, and the mirror.
- **Binding push fan-out.** With several decision-service replicas, every replica serves the new binding within one round trip, and a replica that missed the push repairs itself within the re-read bound.
- **Canonical repository paths.** The shared canonicalizer refuses traversal, encoding, parameter, userinfo, and port tricks identically in the ref resolver, at create, and in `netagent`.
- **Materialization without the gateway clone.** A session with `gitClone` sources materializes through the pod path, and the resolved commit is reported back and persisted.
- **Operator services and the egress halt on the compose stack.** A session reaches a stub MCP server through `netagent` with a tenant secret and with an identity assertion, and the stub verifies the assertion against the tenant key set and refuses one with the wrong `aud`, an expired one, and another tenant's; a stub that uses the deployment key set attributes an `acme` and a `globex` assertion each to its tenant, and with `tokenService.identityAssertion.deploymentKeySet.enabled: false` the deployment key set route answers `404` and a stub that uses the issuer-prefix method attributes both. A suspension that cannot take hold, during a held request to the stub, closes the request, the slot's next request is answered `503`, and the pod releases. The tunnel to the stub appears in `egress.tunnel`, and each assertion in `credential.mint` with its `jti`.

### Tier 5: end to end on Kind

- **The full chart with pools with `egressProfile: proxied`.** Pods reach only `lenny-egress`, `lenny-egress-authz` on the pod binding API, the Token Service on the pod credential API, the git endpoints, the `proxied` DNS resolver, and the base destinations, each on its port, and a direct connection fails. The `proxied` DNS resolver answers cluster names and returns NXDOMAIN for every other name.
- **Warm-up egress before claim.** The tests cover the warm fill from the mirror, the policy change at claim, and the closing of tunnels when a session ends. Before a session binds, a fetch of a repository on the pod's warm list or in the applicable warm-up policy passes, and a fetch of any other repository is refused, on an unbound pod and on a tenant-owned pod. A platform entry whose host the owning tenant denies is absent from that pool's warm list, and no fill reaches the host. During a rollout, a pod warming for one revision cannot fetch a repository that only another revision's warm list names.
- **Pod models.** Each row of the policy table under "Pod models" is tested: one session per slot, recycle with the slot release and the next warm-up checkout, concurrent slots with slot attribution, and tenant-owned pools.
- **Slot attribution on each runtime class.** On a pod with two slots under runc with user namespaces, gVisor, and Kata, `netagent` attributes each slot's connection to its slot, or reports at start that the lookup is unavailable, and the controller marks the pool so that the fallback applies. runc answers through `sock_diag`, gVisor with its own network stack and with host networking and Kata through `/proc/net/tcp`, a burst of several hundred connections per slot is attributed with no mismatch, a client that closes before the lookup is refused rather than attributed, and a connection from a nested user namespace under gVisor or Kata is attributed to its slot.
- **Store fill and platform warm entries.** `lenny-store-fill` fills a pool's stores and pristine trees before READY under its own UID, every slot UID reads them, an operator's `gitRepository` entry with `prestage: true` is staged in every slot of a platform pool before bind and adopted by a session's clone of that repository at whatever `path` the clone names, and an entry without `prestage` is filled and not staged. On a Kata `block-plain` pool the same holds, with each store's directory and its pristine tree's directory set to the store group before the fill writes into them.
- **Pools with `egressProfile: restricted` and `gitAccess`.** A pod reaches the mirror and `lenny-egress` for the hosts of the `gitAccess` formula alone, a CONNECT to any other host is refused, and a pool without `gitAccess` reaches neither.
- **Scope of Kind.** kindnet does not filter traffic to node-local endpoints and fails open without its userspace program. Tier 5 asserts only what kindnet enforces, and the node-local cases move to tier 6.
- **Per-slot assertions and halts.** On a pod with two slots, each slot's assertion names its own session, a connection from slot 1's UID never carries slot 0's assertion, and a halt of slot 0 leaves slot 1's egress running. A fallback pool refuses an `identityAssertion` binding with `IDENTITY_ASSERTION_UNSUPPORTED_POD`, and `HaltSessionEgress` answers that the halt is unavailable there.

### Tier 6: end to end on cloud

- `proxied` NetworkPolicy enforcement on Calico (iptables and eBPF dataplanes), Cilium, GKE Dataplane V2, and AWS VPC CNI in strict mode, including node-local endpoints.
- An egress group's NAT address as seen by an external echo service.
- GitHub App minting against a test organization. GitHub enforces repository scoping and the `workflows` permission, and a real `gh pr create` succeeds through `inject`.
- `netagent` in gVisor and Kata pools, with slot attribution on GKE Sandbox's gVisor node pools and on AKS's Kata node pools, and on Kata with a guest kernel built with `CONFIG_INET_DIAG` and `CONFIG_INET_TCP_DIAG`, where `sock_diag` answers.

### Tier 7: load

- **7a (local).** The tests cover:
  - evidence ingest at the CONNECT rates above with realistic record sizes and many small tenants, the compactor, and the write-ahead path against a pooled production Postgres;
  - TLS cost on the proxy hop, the egress decision API, the git endpoint, and the pod binding API with the Go `netagent`, including its memory per tunnel, throughput at the chart's CPU settings, and a reconnect storm after a decision service restart;
  - decision latency and the decision cache in `lenny-egress-authz` at the CONNECT rates the round 3 Envoy results reached;
  - the mint cache with many sessions on one GitHub App installation.
- **7b (Kind).** The tests cover:
  - Envoy tunnel concurrency above the default limit of 1,024 per cluster;
  - CONNECT setup latency at the target rates;
  - ext_authz timeout behaviour;
  - many intercepted streams through one `netagent`;
  - concurrent warm fills from the mirror.

### Tier 8: chaos

- **Certificates.** A rotation with tunnels open closes none, invalid material keeps the last good certificate, an expired certificate fails closed and `CertExpiryImminent` fires before expiry, and an absent Secret keeps the container from starting. The readiness probe fails for each broken start state and a pod in that state receives no traffic. A CA key rotation in the stated order reaches warm pods and every component and drops no connection, and a routine renewal keeps existing chains valid. `doctor --fix` re-issues only a leaf inside its relative window, the component reloads it, and a CA Certificate is reported and left alone.
- **The egress PKI without `mtls.enabled`.** The Kind overlay, which keeps `mtls.enabled: false`, runs every TLS hop and the binding push, so tier 5 exercises them before R14, and an install with a replacement proxy and no mirror still renders the PKI.
- **The decision service.** When it is down, slow, or partitioned, CONNECTs fail closed and recover within the documented time.
- **A `netagent` crash.** A crash during a tunnel and during a push leaves the runtime running, egress fails closed during the restart, and the CA is unchanged afterwards.
- **Mirror and Token Service loss.** The tests cover the loss of a mirror pod during a fetch, and an unavailable Token Service during a push, at an `always` binding's bind time, at a federation rewrite, and at a `metadata` refresh. Each fails closed, cached credentials serve until expiry, and no token is used past its expiry.
- **Decision replica loss.** `netagent` resubscribes within the grace period and keeps its binding; a rollout of the decision service closes no tunnel.
- **API server loss.** With the API server unreachable, decisions and mints continue on the informer cache for existing pods until their tokens expire, new pods fail closed, the alert fires, and the Token Service stays ready for the gateway lease path.
- **Fetch retry window.** A first fetch longer than the window fails the session at finalize with `git_fetch_pending` before the finalize watchdog fires, with several `gitClone` sources in one plan; a resume with a pending fetch fails after the 30 s wait before the `resuming` watchdog; and the next session is served from the store.
- **Envoy and DNS.** The tests cover an Envoy rollout with live tunnels, and DNS rebinding against a controllable resolver.
- **Token Service loss and `netagent` restarts under assertions and halts.** With the Token Service unavailable, cached assertions serve until they expire and the bound host is then answered `503`. A `netagent` restart while a slot is halted: after the ready report the adapter reconnects and sends `halt_slot` again, and the slot's requests stay refused.

### Tier 9: security

- **`lenny-store-fill` boundary.** The container holds no service account token and no VCS token, mounts no volume read-write but `lenny-git-stores`, and its UID is refused by `netagent` once the pod is ready. A slot UID cannot write a store, no process of a slot container holds the store group, a setuid or setgid file in a filled store or pristine tree carries neither bit, and the adapter's deletion of a withheld store reads no repository data and follows no symbolic link, because it runs `openat2` and `unlinkat` relative to directory descriptors under `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV` ([SI-8](session-isolation-design.md#si-8-shared-repository-stores)).
- **Per-slot scopes.** A process in one slot cannot fetch a repository only the other slot's session may read, push to one only the other may write, or receive the other session's injected credential, and a loopback connection from a UID that `netagent`'s configuration does not list is refused.
- **Cause assertion type.** The Token Service and the decision service refuse as a cause a Lenny-issued JWT of any other type or audience, including a playground bearer, an impersonation bearer, and a token from `/v1/oauth/token`, each signed with the same key. Every other Lenny verifier, on the gateway, the Token Service, and `lenny-ops`, refuses a gateway cause assertion.
- **Anchor constraints.** The trust anchor is constrained on every agent pod, whether it has one slot or several and whether it is recycled (decision 62, ARR-8). The adapter waits for the applied coordination generation before setup and launch, and an enforcing client reaches its bound hosts in each pod model.
- **Hostile repositories.** The suite of "Validation still owed", **Hostile-repository stress suite**, runs on runc, gVisor, and Kata as tests rather than as a one-off spike (decision 61).
- **Platform marker.** A slot process cannot obtain the platform marker by sending `x-lenny-platform`, and a marked CONNECT reaches no destination but the gateway, object storage, and the git mirror (decision 64).
- **Fetch grants.** A process at a slot UID cannot open `@lenny-netagent-grant`, a grant changes no readable set, and the helper's environment holds no service account token or VCS token.
- **Secrets out of agent reach.** The agent UID cannot read the service account tokens, upstream tokens, the CA private key, or `inject` secrets, through the filesystem, procfs, the environment, or command lines. A control case with `shareProcessNamespace` set confirms the webhook rejects the pod.
- **No secrets in the environment.** No credential appears in any environment variable or argument of the runtime or of `netagent`. This extends the existing credential-leakage tests.
- **SSRF.** The tests cover every non-public address range, reached through hostnames, literals, IPv4-mapped and NAT64 forms, and rebinding.
- **Tenant isolation.** A pod of one tenant cannot reach another tenant's private services, upstream proxies, git endpoints, egress groups, mirror stores, or credential bindings, including by object SHA, and including through a hostname the tenant controls that resolves to another tenant's private address.
- **Mirror poisoning.** A repository filled through one tenant's route or credential is never served from the shared public store, and a public store stops serving after its upstream turns private.
- **Host mismatch under `delivery: inject`.** A request whose `Host` differs from the CONNECT authority on a bound host carries no injected header.
- **Federation isolation.** A token issued for one tenant fails verification against another tenant's issuer, and fails at every Lenny endpoint.
- **Quotas.** One pod at its tunnel and rate quotas does not degrade another tenant's CONNECT latency or success rate, and a lost access-log stream expires its counted tunnels within the heartbeat bound rather than refusing the tenant indefinitely.
- **Egress groups.** A pod of an ungranted tenant is dropped by the group's ingress policy and refused by the decision; a pod of a granted tenant is refused at the default Envoy; a forged `x-lenny-egress-group` header from a pod never reaches the decision.
- **Binding feed.** A forged push changes no decision, a lost push is repaired within the re-read bound, and a lower coordination generation is refused.
- **Header hygiene.** Agent-supplied route, identity, and `Proxy-Authorization` headers never leave `netagent`, and a copied service account token is refused from any address but the pod's.
- **Chained upstreams.** The pod's `Proxy-Authorization` never reaches a chained upstream, and the upstream's 407 and 403 replies are normalized.
- **The server-name check.** A mismatched name, a missing name, and an IP-literal authority are refused. The Encrypted Client Hello case is asserted as a documented limit.
- **Token misuse.** A deleted pod's token is refused. Minting a pod-bound token requires the RBAC verb that no Lenny component holds. A credential request on the pod credential API that arrives from any identity other than the pod's or the mirror's is refused, including one relayed by the decision service, and the mirror identity cannot obtain a `stored` credential or a write token.
- **Transport security.** No token, repository content, or binding data crosses a pod-to-platform hop in cleartext. `netagent`, the ref resolver, and Envoy refuse an untrusted, expired, or wrong-name server and a plaintext server, a server presenting a certificate from the TLS interception bundle or a tenant CA is refused on platform hops, the decision service refuses an egress decision API client outside its allowlist or with no certificate, a mirror identity calling the egress decision API receives no credential headers and is refused the full check, a tenant assertion from the mirror is refused when that tenant's route does not name it, and the chart refuses a `netagent` CPU limit below 250m.
- **Exfiltration controls.** On a `credentialPinned` host an agent-supplied credential never reaches the host, whatever its case, port, header, or query parameter, a differing authority and an upgrade are refused, and a host with no binding is refused. A pod's CONNECT to a credential-pinned or read-only host without the terminated marker gets a 403, including from a `netagent` that has not read its list, during a Token Service outage, and within the decision cache's TTL of a marked allow. An agent-supplied marker is stripped, the git path and service identities reach a credential-pinned git host, and a snapshotless rebuild's setup commands use bindings with `delivery: inject`. A read-only host refuses other methods, and validators refuse these settings on pools with `egressProfile: internet`.
- **Git on covered and uncovered hosts.** On a covered host, each override under "Git configuration" stays on the git path, a client's credential never reaches the host unless the session qualifies under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`, and an absolute-form plain HTTP request takes the same path. A pod's CONNECT to a covered host without the terminated marker gets a 403. `netagent` refuses CONNECTs until its first pod binding API answer after a restart, a clone waits for the coordination generation, a pool or route write closes an open tunnel to a newly covered, credential-pinned, or read-only host, git-lfs stops on an undeliverable action rather than retrying, a pooled upstream connection does not outlive a route edit, and an LFS batch response gives the agent no credential. A test records the opaque-tunnel residual on an uncovered host, including a held token reaching it, and the alias limit, so the documentation cannot drift from them.
- **Port scoping.** A pod cannot reach Envoy's admin interface, the TLS readiness listener, the metrics listeners, the egress decision API or access log ports, or any mirror port other than git.
- **Provisional binding.** A resuming pod obtains read tokens for its `gitClone` sources and the `inject` grants the provisional row states, and nothing else, including a pod naming a binding with `delivery: file` under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`, whose rebuild's clone reads as the App with no write mint, and a resume that fails leaves the released pod with no session grant.
- **Mint checks.** The mint checks hold independently: a decision service that answers allow for a host outside the binding's stored `hosts`, or for a repository outside the stored writable set, or that asserts another tenant for the pod, obtains no mint, and `netagent` injects nothing for a host absent from the pod credential API response.
- **Cache bounds.** A credential cached for one session is gone from `netagent` before the next session on the same pod, and a rotated stored secret reaches the pod within its cache lifetime.
- **Mirror store reads.** The mirror derives the requesting pod's tenant from its own `pkg/binding` instance and refuses a store the decision allowed for another tenant.
- **Mirror subprocess boundary.** A stand-in for a compromised fetch subprocess cannot read the mirror's identity material, another tenant's store, or a concurrent fetch's forwarder socket.
- **Routing rule.** A repository under a `mint-coarse` or `stored` pool goes `direct` from `netagent`, resolves upstream in the gateway, and is refused by the mirror, whatever the tenant's git route says.
- **Pod credential API isolation.** Pods at their pod credential API quotas do not raise the gateway's lease-path latency or trip its Token Service breaker.
- **Owner stamp.** A pool created without an owner cannot gain one, and a recycled pod keeps its tenant identity at level 1, and the controller's creation-time owner label passes the webhook, and a gateway re-stamp with another tenant is refused.
- **Placeholders and scrubbing.** A secret is never attached to an unbound host, a placeholder sent to an unbound host carries no secret, and reflected secrets are scrubbed.
- **Stored secrets and git hosts.** A stored-secret binding with `delivery: inject` on a host a VCS pool covers is refused at create, and a `git push` through the egress proxy to such a host carries no credential.
- **Level 1 limits.** The probe fails against a proxy with no address rule, one that ignores authentication, one that refuses everything, one without TLS, and one whose sync job has stopped, and it passes against the reference configuration. Preflight refuses a proxy without TLS or authentication before install, and warm pods do not fill until the post-install probe passes and its ConfigMap shows it. `lenny-ops` obtains probe credentials with its rendered rows. Hosts carrying a newline, proxy syntax, or a numeric final label are refused on write and when the document is generated, and the contract suite's renderer cases cover them. An identity's credential is absent from the agent UID's reach and from the environment, tenants hold separate identities, a warm pod of a shared pool holds only the warm identity, and a bound pod's credential is flushed on recycle. The chart refuses level 1 without the acknowledgement. A test records each residual in the limits table, so the documentation cannot drift from behaviour.
- **Tenant admin limits.** A tenant admin cannot change `egressProfile` on a shared pool, and an overlay cannot extend a setting without the platform flag.
- **Tree validation.** Components at the adapter or `netagent` UID never follow workspace symlinks out of the tree, and the fetch size caps hold.
- **Identity assertions and the halt channel.** No assertion appears in a slot's files, environment, or a reflected response body. An impersonation bearer's input to a tree that holds an `identityAssertion` binding is refused with `impersonation_identity_assertion`. A process at a slot UID cannot open `@lenny-netagent-halt`.

### Tier 10: conformance

- The runtime adapter conformance battery gains a network and credentials suite. It runs for a runtime with no SDK and for one built on each SDK, on a pod with one slot and on a pod with two. Each runtime must:
  - reach a bound API through `inject`;
  - read a binding with `delivery: file`;
  - clone and push on a covered host through `http.proxy`;
  - pass the launch variables to a spawned shell command.
- The SDK accessors behave identically across languages against shared descriptor fixtures.
- The level 1 proxy contract ships as a suite a deployer runs against a candidate proxy. A level 2 suite tests that the proxy passes `x-lenny-tenant`, `x-lenny-session`, the terminated marker, and the asserted route to its egress decision API call, and that a decision-service error or a 5xx deny fails closed, which needs `failure_mode_allow: false` on an Envoy-based proxy.

### Tier 11: documentation

- The examples on the new documentation pages validate against their schemas. These include Helm values, admin API payloads, credential bindings, and the network descriptor.
- The Level 1 proxies page carries the limits table, and the pages that describe a weakened guarantee link to it.
- Every new alert resolves to a runbook. The new alerts cover decision failures, mint rate, mirror errors, `netagent` restarts, and tunnel saturation.

## Validation still owed

- **Slot attribution:** the runtime-class question that the owner asked a spike to settle on 2026-11-13 ("run a spike to test this") was measured on 2026-11-14 with a test listener on one EC2 node under k3s ("Slot attribution and fetch grants"): runc answers `sock_diag`, gVisor with either network mode and Kata with either volume mode answer only `/proc/net/tcp`, and every answering reader gave the right UID. Each pool's attribution status still decides where [`user-credential-delivery-design.md` decision 41](user-credential-delivery-design.md#decisions) and [`user-git-identity-design.md` decision 52](user-git-identity-design.md#decisions) admit user-credential bindings, `lockEgressToHosts`, and user git identity. What stays owed: the production `netagent` with the state and UID rule, the batched read, and the start-up check, under a burst of connections on gVisor and Kata; the managed runtimes of EKS, GKE Sandbox, and AKS's Kata node pools; a Kata guest kernel built with `CONFIG_INET_DIAG` and `CONFIG_INET_TCP_DIAG`, which the spike did not build; and the per-slot scopes and the platform marker of decisions 60 and 64 end to end.
- **Harness clients through `netagent`** ([`connector-removal-design.md`, "Validation owed"](connector-removal-design.md#validation-owed)): the harness-egress spike of 2026-11-15 ran on loopback with a Go stand-in, and a run of Claude Code and Codex through the production `netagent` in a pod on runc, gVisor, and Kata stays owed with that design's CR-B.
- **Identity assertions on pods with several slots** (the same design, O7): slot attribution is measured, and the attachment of each slot's own assertion on runc, gVisor, and Kata is not, which stays owed with CR-B.
- **`lenny-store-fill`** (decision 61): the container fills the stores and their pristine trees under its own UID with only the store volume writable; the stores are written with the store group as the file group, which is the primary group of the fill and the adapter and which no slot container holds, and a umask of `0002` (directories `0775`, object files `0444`), and each pristine tree takes an explicit mode pass (directories `0775`, files `0444`, executables `0555`, setuid and setgid bits cleared), so every slot UID reads them through the other class; its 1 GiB memory limit holds for a fill of the largest store a pool's `warmCloneBytes` admits, which counts each store and its pristine tree; it starts after the `netagent` native sidecar; `netagent` attributes its UID as the warm fill; the adapter deletes a withheld store through its read-write mount without parsing it; on a Kata `block-plain` pool, whose volume root carries the setgid bit and the dedicated volume GID, every store entry still carries the store group; and under user namespaces its UID maps within the pod's range.
- **Hostile-repository stress suite** (decision 61, at the owner's request of 2026-11-13 "to stress test this thoroughly (with actual tests)"): a suite of tests, run on runc, gVisor, and Kata, that fills and stages repositories carrying setuid and setgid files, symbolic-link and hard-link farms, deep and wide trees, mode-`000` directories, name collisions, and very large files, and that deletes a withheld store while a slot reads it. It checks that no slot gains write access to a store, the store group, or a set-ID bit, that neither the fill nor the deletion follows a link out of the volume, and that the deletion under the race completes or fails closed.
- **`netagent.resources` defaults** (decision 66): the request of 100m CPU and 128 MiB and the 512 MiB memory limit, under a Chromium clone and under many concurrent tunnels.
- **`gitMirror.heldUidBudget`** (decision 66): a quarter of the mirror's fetch-UID range, against concurrent fetches while stores are held.
- **Commit identity selection** (decision 65): `includeIf "hasconfig:remote.*.url:<pattern>"` with the pinned git, on SSH remote forms, and on a runtime git older than 2.36.
- **The fetch grant:** a helper run's fetch held for the fetch retry window on a first materialization and limited to 30 s on a resume, a grant re-registered after a `netagent` restart, and the `git.operation` record's cause, none of which has run.
- **The pinned git under `lenny-materialize`:** a clone, a fetch through `netagent` with the pod trust bundle, alternates under `/lenny/git-stores`, the tree-size check, and the index refresh, with git exec-only at mode `0111`, which [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) also lists.
- **The warm-up checkout under the warm-up policy:** a staged checkout after a slot release reaches only the hosts of the pool's or the tenant's warm-up policy and holds no session credential.
- **The constrained trust anchor on a pod with several slots,** where a later slot's binding re-issues the anchor while an earlier slot's runtime process holds the previous one.
- **The git path, under T, in the production `netagent`:** the spike ran a prototype on a stub, which proves forwarding only. No GitHub or GitLab host ran.
