- **LFS on a live GitLab:** batch actions, a `Gitlab::LfsToken`, transfer paths, and the deploy token echo, which are **[source]** from GitLab's public source, with GitLab's form **[measured]** on a stub alone. `info/lfs` on `mint-coarse` and `stored` providers stays refused until this passes.
- **LFS on GitHub beyond one action:** an upload, a request with a token, and a transfer. One anonymous download action was read **[live]**.
- **The per-object `error` on a live store, and a terminated transfer path with no failure refusal,** which did not run.
- **How provider edges treat an unknown server name,** for the alias limit.
- **Claims on the git path that rested on the loopback rounds, with no T measurement:**
  - read after push through the mirror route, which the spike did not implement;
  - the helper's clone in the slot under a fetch grant;
  - a stock git clone through a pinned private address (round 6), and the git path at level 1;
  - the fetch retry window and `netagent`'s hold on a mirror 503;
  - `go get` with `GOPROXY=direct`;
  - a `git-receive-pack` with push options on the terminated connection;
  - git on macOS, Windows, and amd64, the .NET SDK image, and git against `ghe.com` beyond the stub.
- **A rule per declared repository for an `sshForms` path change past the prefix,** which has not run.
- **`allowRuntimeHeldCredentials` on production components:** the attach and each 401 state ran **[measured]** on a Token Service stand-in with no decision service.
- **The route assertion's fall-through, the Token Service's own assertion, the two documented topologies under them, and the upstream pool key and closure on a route write,** which have not run. `netagent`'s assertion ran on a stand-in, and the per-client-CONNECT decision did not run.
- **The `gitAccess` formula computed from a pool,** where a host held as data ran.
- **The request inspection stage under deny rules, on a live host:** deny mode's bypass analysis ran on the stub, as [`github-credential-addon-design.md`, "Validation owed"](github-credential-addon-design.md#validation-owed) records ([S19](github-credential-addon-design.md#validation-owed)), and what a host does with each spelling is owed.
- **`inject` delivery in the production `netagent`:** round 10 ran the client matrix against a Go prototype written to this text, including httpx over HTTP/2 and a rustls client, so what remains is repeating it against the component that ships.
- **Corporate gateways:** a hands-on test against at least one gateway in a deployer's environment, covering source-address identity through an egress group, and whether that gateway reads `X-Authenticated-User` on CONNECT. Public documentation does not settle the second point.
- **GitHub:** whether installation token mints count against the 2,000-per-hour limit, measured against a test App or confirmed by GitHub support.
- **`ls-refs` against hosted providers:** GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket Cloud, Azure DevOps, and AWS CodeCommit with real tokens on private repositories. Round 10 covered the protocol against a local Gitea.
- **Ref resolution after the post-chain point:** confirm in the tree that the `kind: derive` and `kind: delegate` paths can resolve `gitClone` refs after the post-chain point of RT-2 and before the pod claim.
- **Evidence streams:** the refusal rate of the commit bound on a production database disk, tenant-key KMS use and rewrap, escrow, a cloud object store's scan and index-read latency, a consumer read forced across a removed object, and the cause of one undelivered record in round 11.
- **The gateway's group mismatch path,** which has no code yet.
- **Level 1** against a commercial proxy and an EDL consumer.
- **Mirror volumes:** chown and POSIX default ACLs on cloud block volumes, and the normalization helper on a node with `fs.protected_hardlinks=0`, which the chart refuses.

## Instructions for reviewers of other designs

Revisions 75 to 78 ask these changes of other designs, each as a request:

- [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md): O9 is answered here under "Slot attribution and fetch grants" as its option (a): `netagent` attributes a connection by its owner UID through `sock_diag` or `/proc/net/tcp`, and whether gVisor and Kata answer either reader stays owed. Per-slot scopes are settled (decision 60), so the validation error of `acknowledgeSharedPod` names `netagent`'s union scope only as the fallback on a pool whose runtime class does not answer attribution. SI-7's row for per-slot `delivery: file` credentials matches "Delivery modes", with the volume name `slot-<n>-netcred` that PL-5 owns, and the `lenny-pod-security` webhook admits `netagent` as that volume's only writer.
- [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md): the fetch grant's carrier is `CH-FETCHGRANT` on `@lenny-netagent-grant`, its window ends at the run's deadline plus the 5 s status wait, and it covers `materialize` and `overlay` runs with a `gitClone` source and `restore-complete` ("Slot attribution and fetch grants"). The store fill and the pristine trees move to `lenny-store-fill` (decision 61), so WM-9's store fill exception, WMR-2's clause on the adapter's fill, the "Limits" item on the fill parsing git as the adapter, the WM-15 sentence "When the adapter fills a store", and the work table row that gives the fill to the adapter UID change to that container. A `gitRepository` warm entry with `prestage: true`, an operator's or a tenant's, is staged by `prestage` in each idle slot (decision 67, which replaces decision 63's target `path`), and the first `gitClone` source of its repository in the session's workspace adopts the staged tree at that source's own `path`. A staged tree that no source adopts never appears in a checkpoint, download, or export, and is visible only under `.lenny-discard-<id>` until its `discard` ends.
- [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md): render `lenny-store-fill` as an init container that starts after the `netagent` native sidecar, under its own UID within the pod's user-namespace range, with `lenny-git-stores` read-write, the pinned git from `lenny-bin`, and `/run/netagent` read-only, and with resources from a chart value (decision 61). The adapter keeps `lenny-git-stores` read-write at `/adapter/git-stores` only to delete withheld stores, and no longer needs `/run/netagent` for the fill. Settle O5 as superseded by decision 61, and name the per-slot `delivery: file` volume in PL-5. PL-5 names it `slot-<n>-netcred`, memory-backed, mounted read-write into `netagent` and read-only at `/run/netagent/credentials` into slot `n`, and this design uses that name. `lenny-store-fill` writes with the store group, a dedicated GID that is the primary group of `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter and that no slot container holds, as the file group and a umask of `0002`, as PL-5 states, and its mode pass clears setuid and setgid bits.
- [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md): PM-8's `proxied` DNS resolver row is a second CoreDNS beside `lenny-agent-dns`, as PM-8 lists it, and this design names its Deployment `lenny-proxied-dns`. PM-4's table of what `sessionIsolationLevel` reports gains `perSlotScopes`, a boolean that is `false` on a pod with several slots whose runtime class does not answer slot attribution (decision 60), the name that the posture record of [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) already uses. This design's statement of the mirror's `global.devMode` relaxation is a pointer to PM-8.
- [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md): the rendered git configuration carries `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` (decision 59), and a runtime may override it at its own checkpoint cost (D6), so IC-1's sentence that this design "does not carry [gc.auto=0] today" is stale.
- [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md): EG-3 lands before RP-B, as "Proposal split" records.
- [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) and [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md): under decision 60 a pool with several slots whose runtime class answers slot attribution evaluates each session in its own environment and accepts `egress.allowedHosts` and `egressLockHosts`; E = ∅ and those refusals remain only on a pool whose runtime class does not answer attribution, which the post-chain point reads from the pool's status. A mirror fill's pod cause on a pod with several slots carries the session `netagent` attributed the fetch to.
- [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md): decision 52 is unblocked, because decision 60 is settled as (a). A granted fetch on a host with `userAuthorization: required` takes the same per-user check as any fetch ("Slot attribution and fetch grants").
- [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md): `delivery: file` on a pod with several slots reaches only its own slot through the per-slot credentials volume, and decision 41 is unblocked by decision 60.
- [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md): a withheld store's staged tree in the slot is removed by the helper's first run for the session while the adapter deletes only the store and its pristine tree, the stores live on `lenny-git-stores` at `/lenny/git-stores` and `/adapter/git-stores` and are filled by `lenny-store-fill` (decision 61), and no pool refuses a withholding because its runtime process started before the session, since every runtime now launches at bind.
- [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md): `netagent`'s request is counted within the pool's resource class, as its decision 7 mapping states; `lenny-store-fill`'s init-container request also needs a place in that mapping, because an init container's request sets the pod's effective request when it exceeds the sum of the other containers.
- [`tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`](tenant-outbound-connector-design.md): EG-0 is withdrawn (decision 62), so the egress proposal labels row and the precedent for `REG-SITEATTACH` drop the service-mode pin record.
- Revision 78 asks these of [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) and [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md): PL-3, PL-5, and SI-7 state that `slot-<n>-netcred` holds every per-session value (the binding list with the session launch variables and the applied coordination generation, the session's git configuration file and commit template, the `netrc` file, and the credential directories) and that `/run/netagent` names no session; PL-5's volume table gives `netagent`'s memory-backed volumes their proposed `sizeLimit` values; PL-5 and SI-8 state the pristine tree's mode pass and the adapter's deletion under `openat2` and `unlinkat` with `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`; the supervisor of PL-1 adds the session launch variables from the slot's binding list at launch; and the pod builder passes the warm list to both `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter, which still reads it for the warm-up checkout and the contributions, as "Carrier" in [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#warm-stores) states.
- Revision 78 asks [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) to read its open request for UG-3 "Commit authorship" as applied: the session's git configuration file on its slot's credentials volume carries the author, the committer, and the `Lenny-Session` commit template.
- Revision 83 asks [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) (its "Slot attribution per runtime class" item), [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) (V10), [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) (the runtime classes left open in its answer on decision 52), and [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) (the fetch grant's attribution under runc, gVisor, and Kata) to record the runtime-class question as measured on 2026-11-14: runc pools attribute through `sock_diag`, gVisor and Kata pools through `/proc/net/tcp`, and no measured runtime class is a fallback pool ("Slot attribution and fetch grants"). [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md) records the same in its revision 13.

## Decisions

Recorded owner decisions:

- The scope rule (2026-11-02): this document holds egress and git. A proposal here carries other work only as a hook when adding it later would be rework, and everything else goes in its own design document. Proposals interleave across documents, with dependencies cross-referenced from the documents and no shared queue file.
- Proposal 0 stays in this design (withdrawn on 2026-11-13 by decision 62). F-25.6.9 and F-25.4.40 move to [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md).
- The evidence streams belong in this design, with room for later agent activity streams. The tenant outbound connector has its own design document, with its hooks here.
- Level 1 replacement is included (2026-11-02). The owner reversed the deferral, and the documentation must state its limits and why it is not the recommended approach.
- Decisions 1 to 11, 13 to 30, and 32 are adopted as recommended (2026-11-02), along with the four corrections listed under revision 15 in "Review record". Server TLS on I2 (the proxy hop), I5 (the git endpoint), and I7 (the pod binding API) is adopted (2026-11-02), after a spike the owner asked for. R14 stays outside this design. Decisions 33 to 38 are adopted as recommended (2026-11-02), with 6 hours as the default for decision 36 and the note under "Transport security" that R14 may merge the two PKIs. Decision 31 is adopted as recommended (2026-11-02), with the anti-affinity, the `hostUsers` value, and the ID rule below. Decisions 39 to 42 are adopted as recommended (2026-11-02), with the changes the twenty-first review round forced, stated at each.
- A local endpoint is adopted, and it runs in the separate `netagent` container. Decision 44 removed the second, loopback git endpoint.
- The decision service runs separately from the gateway.
- Pull requests and host APIs go through standard HTTP on the egress route, with no platform tool. An in-pod API token is acceptable as a tenant opt-in.
- Policy for concurrent-slot pods is per pool and tenant. Decision 60 replaced this on 2026-11-13 with per-slot scopes, and the per-pool-and-tenant policy remains the fallback where slot attribution does not answer.
- Envoy is the default proxy. The owner made this conditional on hands-on testing, and the third validation round passed it with the configuration changes listed under "Central egress proxy".
- Stored-token push sits behind a gate plus a per-pool tenant opt-in, with the risks stated under "Credentials". Revision 6 made the gate a per-tenant platform grant (decision 19), which supersedes the deployment-wide gate this decision first recorded.
- Tenant admins can register upstream proxies and git endpoints on public addresses, following the connector precedent. Private-address services stay under platform registration. (The precedent was §9.3's MCP connector definitions, which [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) removes under the owner's decision of 2026-11-15. The decision stands without it.)
- `gitAccess` on pools with `egressProfile: restricted` is adopted, so pools with `egressProfile: restricted` keep warm stores without general egress.

Decisions raised in revision 3:

1. **Remove `provider-direct` now.** Recommendation: adopt. Revision 77 drops the pairing of `deliveryMode: direct` with `proxied` that this decision first carried, because LU-4 removes direct delivery mode (see "Consolidating `provider-direct`").
2. **Remove the gateway clone path.** Recommendation: adopt. The gateway runs no git, clones run in the pod through `netagent`, and F-14.1.37 closes by deletion (see "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**).
3. **Remove category-keyed defaults from §26.2.** Recommendation: adopt (see "Runtime categories in §26.2").
4. **`inject` as the default credential delivery, including TLS termination for bound hosts.** Recommendation: adopt, with `file` as the gated opt-in. Round 3 validated both transports with mitmproxy standing in for `netagent`.
5. **Egress groups for corporate gateway identity.** Recommendation: adopt, because no surveyed gateway documents header identity on an undecrypted CONNECT.
6. **Tenant pool overlays with `tenantOverride` defaulting to `narrow`.** Recommendation: adopt (see "Tenant pool overlays").
7. **SSH remotes through `sshForms` data with a git `ERR` fallback.** Recommendation: adopt, with no per-provider code in `netagent`.
8. **SDK scope.** Recommendation: one read-only network accessor per SDK (endpoints, launch variables, bindings, and `file` values read on each call), and nothing else. Every mechanism keeps working for runtimes without an SDK.
9. **Cloud and platform credentials.** Recommendation: adopt `federation` as the generic mechanism, with the Token Service as OIDC issuer and provider templates as data, and adopt `metadata` as a provider plugin for tenants that cannot configure federation trust, with AWS shipped first. Both sit behind the `agentReadableDelivery` grant with `file`.
10. **No tool-specific templates shipped by Lenny.** Recommendation: adopt. Lenny ships the convention variables, the git config, the CA bundle, the descriptor, and the generic credential directory with a `netrc` projection. Tool files (Maven `settings.xml`, `gh` `hosts.yml`) come from the runtime image or a deployer template.
11. **Constant placeholder.** Recommendation: adopt `lenny-injected` as a constant fixed at pod creation, because `netagent` overwrites the header whatever the placeholder holds.

Decisions raised by the revision 4 review:

12. **Level 1 replacement.** The review recommended deferring it. The owner decided to include it, with its limits and the reasons it is not recommended stated in the documentation (see "Level 1 replacement").
13. **Private destinations pinned by the decision, with no filter exemptions.** Recommendation: adopt. It closes the cross-tenant path through tenant-controlled DNS, and it removes the need for SSRF exemptions.
14. **Per-tenant federation issuers.** Recommendation: adopt, because AWS and Azure trust conditions cannot express a tenant claim, and a per-tenant issuer scopes trust by construction.
15. **Egress groups as additional `lenny-egress` Deployments with pod pinning.** Recommendation: adopt, in place of a second proxy tier.
16. **Ref resolution stays in the gateway, through a Go `ls-refs` client.** Recommendation: adopt. It keeps the §14 contract synchronous and trusts no SHA from the pod.

Decisions raised by the revision 5 review:

17. **Binding feed as a gateway push plus bounded re-reads, with a provisional binding on resume.** Recommendation: adopt. Postgres notifications are unavailable through the §12 pooler, and the provisional binding is the smaller change to §7.3.
18. **Egress-group grants as Helm values with chart-rendered ingress policies.** Recommendation: adopt, so no Lenny component writes NetworkPolicies.
19. **One gate per risk class, granted per tenant.** Recommendation: adopt, in place of one deployment-wide gate.
20. **A tunnel lifetime menu rendered by the chart.** Recommendation: adopt, so pools choose a cap without xDS.

Decisions raised by the revision 6 review:

21. **`netagent` requests credentials from the Token Service directly (I8, the pod credential API), and the Token Service consults the decision service for scope.** Recommendation: adopt, because relaying the pod's token through the decision service made that service a bearer-token relay.
22. **The controller stamps the owner tenant on tenant-owned pool pods at creation.** Recommendation: adopt, with the matching label-webhook rule.
23. **One shared repository-path canonicalizer** for the resolver, session create, and `netagent`. Recommendation: adopt.

Decisions raised by the revision 7 review:

24. **Two independent gates for every mint, with the Token Service authoritative for binding definitions and the decision service for policy and liveness.** Recommendation: adopt, with one shared `pkg/binding` package.
25. **I8 (the pod credential API) on an isolated, rate-limited TLS listener of the Token Service.** Recommendation: adopt, so pod load cannot reach the gateway's lease path.
26. **`ownerTenantId` set only at pool creation and immutable afterwards.** Recommendation: adopt.

Decisions raised by the revision 8 review:

27. **A Kubernetes-backed pod view in the Token Service.** Recommendation: adopt. Independent mint checks need independent identity sources, and the Pod label is the only authoritative tenant pin, so the Token Service runs its own informer over the agent namespaces.
28. **The mirror serves `mint-scoped` providers only.** Recommendation: adopt; repositories on `mint-coarse` and `stored` providers route `direct` from the pod under one rule that `netagent`, the resolver, and the mirror share.

Decisions raised by the revision 9 review:

29. **Per-fetch UIDs and per-fetch forwarder sockets in the mirror.** Recommendation: adopt, so a compromised git subprocess is bounded to its own fetch.
30. **A fetch retry window below the session watchdogs, with admin prefetch for large repositories.** Recommendation: adopt, with 180 s as the default.

Decisions raised by the revision 10 review:

31. **A capability exception for the mirror container** (a root entrypoint that raises `CAP_SETUID`, `CAP_SETGID`, and `CAP_CHOWN` as ambient capabilities and drops to the parent UID) so fetches run under per-fetch UIDs. Adopted as the one stated exception to the drop-all and non-root rules, with the `baseline` Pod Security prerequisite. It comes with required anti-affinity against the Token Service and the decision service, an optional `gitMirror.hostUsers: false` that is off by default and checked at start, and store GIDs and fetch UIDs below 65536 in every mode, with fetch UIDs reused only after a confirmed sweep. User namespaces reduce the risk of the exception without removing it (see **Capability posture**).
32. **Per-store ownership in the mirror**, so a compromised fetch can write one store only. Recommendation: adopt.

Decisions raised by revision 15:

33. **Level 1 enforcement in two layers**, with a decision call from `netagent` on I7 (the pod binding API) for each CONNECT and the proxy's list behind it. Recommendation: adopt, because a proxy list alone cannot apply session host lists or binding state, and the call keeps per-session audit.
34. **Level 1 as a deployment-wide choice with an acknowledgement**, per-tenant identities, and validators that refuse the features it cannot serve. Recommendation: adopt, because a silently ignored setting would present a tenant with a guarantee it does not have.
35. **No pod `ipBlock` for private destinations at the default level or level 2**, with pods and the resolver reaching them through a raw tunnel to the pinned address, a level 2 proxy required to dial pinned addresses, and Helm-rendered tenant-scoped policies at level 1. Recommendation: adopt, because a deployment-wide `ipBlock` on pods lets any tenant's agent open a raw socket to another tenant's private service. This replaces the direct resolver path revision 16 proposed.
36. **The mirror answers from its store only for listed causes and within 6 hours of the upstream's last confirmation**, and the session reports it. Recommendation: adopt. A shorter limit returns errors sooner during a host outage, and a longer one keeps sessions running on commits that may be retracted.
37. **mTLS on I3 (the egress decision API) and the access log stream**, the hops from Envoy to the decision service, which carry pod tokens and which the owner's decision did not name. Recommendation: adopt, with operations bound to each client SAN, because TLS on I2 (the proxy hop) alone leaves the same token readable one hop further on, one-way TLS would let a compromised mirror fetch query the decision API, and a flat allowlist would let a compromised mirror parent obtain a tenant's upstream credential. Round 7 found no failed CONNECT under faults and no measurable latency.
38. **An egress PKI separate from the `mtls.enabled` PKI.** Adopted, because the other is off in mesh deployments and in the Kind overlay until R14, which would otherwise make these hops depend on R14 after all. The cost is a second CA to operate. R14 may decide to merge the two if it makes the other PKI always on.
39. **Typed evidence streams with one envelope**, in a store that the audit chain anchors. Adopted, because a new kind of evidence, such as agent activity, is then a new stream with no rework. The review added a single `lenny-evidence` ingest service, because producers had neither the reach nor a safe credential to write directly.
40. **Evidence failure behaviour**: egress continues with a gap record by default, and a regulated tenant's record is committed before its CONNECT is allowed. Adopted. The review replaced a refusal based on a stale buffer, which could not keep the promise that no connection goes unrecorded, and round 9 measured about 9 ms at p50. Rounds 11 and 12 added a stated cost: refusals in bursts when the database's disk stalls, measured between 0% and 0.74% on a laptop. The alternative, refusing for every tenant, makes all egress depend on the object store.
41. **Egress group assignment through the API and a stamped label**, which replaces the Helm-rendered grants of decision 18. Adopted, because a Helm rollout per customer does not scale for a SaaS operator, and no Lenny component writes NetworkPolicies either way. The review made a group exclusive by default, with explicit sharing and an address cool-off, because a reassigned address would otherwise let one tenant leave as another.
42. **A shadow policy on the tenant egress policy.** Adopted, time-limited, because tightening a policy blind is what makes customers leave it wide open. The review replaced report-only mode, which allowed what the active policy denied and so reopened denied hosts, with a candidate that is evaluated alongside the active policy and changes no outcome. The [security oversight design](security-oversight-design.md) can forbid it through the opt-in write path.

Decisions raised by revision 44, and settled in revision 47:

43. **A generic user token mechanism.** Owner decisions D1 and D2, recorded in [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) as [its decisions 1 and 2](user-credential-delivery-design.md#decisions). D1 replaced the provider-specific allowlist with one generic binding and request rules held as data, and D2 set the first token sources, `registered` and `oauth2Link`. This design carries the `userCredential` kind, the `userCredentialDelivery` gate, and the request inspection stage. `netagent` carries no provider code on this path, and presets are data.
44. **The git path.** Settled by the owner: **T with the bundle on VCS-covered hosts**, in the owner's words "go with T and your recommendations". Git keeps its remote URLs, `http.proxy` is `netagent`'s egress endpoint, and `netagent` terminates covered hosts, with `http.sslCAInfo` at the bundle. A spike on stock git behind a stub, which proves forwarding only, found T the only candidate where `gh`, `glab`, go-git, JGit, and proxied libgit2 all work, with enforcement on covered hosts holding with the config removed and for a token the runtime holds. The costs are about 35% more `netagent` CPU on git, public roots in git's trust, CA coverage of each VCS host before a warm fill, a libgit2 hang on a constraint mismatch, and **Uncovered git hosts**. [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Git remotes and client tools"](user-git-identity-design.md#git-remotes-and-client-tools) holds the evidence for the other candidates: L, the prior path, which rewrote remotes with `insteadOf` to a loopback endpoint, and P, which rewrote `https://` remotes to `http://` with a loopback `http.proxy`.

Decided by the owner on 2026-11-08, with [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decisions 31 to 37](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#decisions):

45. **One warm list per pool.** Every pre-filled repository store on a pod comes from one warm list rendered for the pool, under one rule set, and the rewrite of proposal 0111 defines it to the requirements that [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Relationship to draft proposal 0111"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#relationship-to-draft-proposal-0111) lists. Before a session binds, a pod's readable set is the pool's warm list plus the warm-up policy that applies to it, and the hosts of the warm list join the pre-session formulas. This replaces the union of a tenant-owned pod's warm list with the base's public `gitRepository` entries. The cost is that the rewrite of 0111 carries the store rules that the tenant-derived runtimes design first stated for warm clones. Under [RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods), the list is keyed on pool and revision, because part of it comes from a revision's `sharedAssets`, and the rule set is unchanged.
46. **Declarers.** A `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime is public only and reaches every pool of that runtime, tenant-owned pools included. A private entry is a `gitRepository` asset of a tenant-derived runtime, which reaches its tenant-owned pool alone; decision 67 retires the private operator entry on a tenant-owned pool's row that this decision first named. `destPath`, private entries on a Runtime, and rule 2's ownership key go. A platform entry whose host a tenant denies is left off that tenant's owned pool's warm list.
47. **Which sources a store serves.** Any `gitClone` source in the plan whose normalized URL matches a store on the pod, whoever wrote it, unless it sets `depth` or `submodules: true`. Restore completion still fetches only the URLs the gateway passes for the bind, which are the plan's repositories.
48. **Environment-scoped repositories.** A warm-list entry whose repository resolves at E = ∅ only to a scoped VCS pool is refused at write, and the PoolScalingController leaves it off the rendered list after a later VCS pool change, as [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 28](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) states. A public `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime is the exception at write: it reaches the pools of several tenants, so its write passes with a `Warning`, and the PoolScalingController checks it per owned pool at render, as [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Warm stores"](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#warm-stores) states.

[`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decision 38](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-08 as recommended, states that pods a platform-declared entry failed still count toward the tenant's creation bound and per-tenant fill limit, because those limits protect the cluster from runaway creation whoever's entry is broken.

Decided by the owner on 2026-11-09, in the answers to the design sync review of 2026-11-08:

49. **A base write that raises the setup bound.** The write is refused when any pool whose fetch retry window reads that bound, platform or tenant-owned, no longer fits its window, and the refusal names the pools and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` that fits. [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md) records it as an exception to its DP9. The clamp that let a template write raise the window and narrowed the owned pool's window becomes the same refusal, and a tenant's own raise stays refused. "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**, states the rule.
50. **Held stores past the UID budget.** A held mirror store whose fetch UIDs would pass `gitMirror.heldUidBudget` is re-chowned after the mirror records an ownership manifest, as [FE-2](forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md#fe-2-mirror-store-hold) states. The held-flag hook under "Reserved for other designs" and its tier 4 test adopt that exception.

Decided by the owner on 2026-11-12, in decisions recorded in the designs of the consistency pass, and applied here in revision 75:

51. **Git on slot repositories runs in `lenny-materialize`.** Settled as SI O11 and recorded as decision D9 of [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md): every git step on a slot repository (init, alternates, the fetch through `netagent`, the tree-size check, checkout, the index refresh more than 1 s after checkout, the capture copy before a checkpoint's first pass, and restore completion) runs as the slot UID, and the adapter runs no git on slot repositories. The owner accepted the materialization study "with its recommendations": a pinned static git staged read-only in `/lenny/bin` (study C), and gateway archive extraction moved into the helper (study A). Every workspace entry is slot-owned under SI decision D9, which settles draft 0111's open item on workspace ownership. "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**, and "Residual risks and limits", **Tree validation**, state the result.
52. **The fetch grant.** The owner accepted study F: the adapter registers a fetch grant with `netagent`, scoped to slot, URL, and time window, which this design owns together with SI O9. "Slot attribution and fetch grants" states the carrier `CH-FETCHGRANT`, the scope, `netagent`'s treatment of a granted fetch, and the attribution of a connection to its slot by owner UID, which answers SI O9 for the grant. Decision 60 extends attribution to each slot's scopes.
53. **The embedded deployment model is removed** (SI D11, owned by [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) as its D1). The "Embedded runtimes" subsection, the embedded rows of "The `netagent` container" and "Pod models", the embedded launch-variable sentence, and the embedded tests leave. The separate `netagent` container stays, for the reasons the remaining rows of its table give.
54. **Pre-launched runtimes are removed.** Every runtime launches at bind, after materialization and setup, and "warm pod" means infrastructure ready. `netagent` therefore issues the name-constrained trust anchor on every session-mode pod, and only a service-mode pod and a `type: mcp` pod carry an unconstrained anchor. Decision 62 removes those two cases. `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep` left with SI D4, so a recycled pod is no exception either.
55. **The fixed pod layout** ([`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) D2): `/workspace`, `/session`, `/tmp`, `/lenny/assets`, `/lenny/git-stores`, `/lenny/bin`, and `/run/lenny` in every slot container, with the adapter's mounts under `/adapter`. The adapter fills the repository stores on `lenny-git-stores` (that design's O5 (a)); decision 61 moves the fill to `lenny-store-fill`. The owner approved the warm-up checkout and the reflink rebuild, which [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) owns, and a staged tree from a withheld store is removed before the session starts.
56. **`workspaceTier` is retired** ([`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md) D1): the tenant settings replace it, the tenant key replaces "T4 KMS rights", and dedicated node tenancy per tenant replaces "T4 nodes host several T4 tenants" (that design's inventory row I24). The data classes are Public, Internal, Confidential, and Secret (owner decision N1).
57. **Placement** ([`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md)): system components run on the system node class with PM-8's priority classes, replicas, and disruption budgets, `lenny-agents-kata` is dropped (its O3 (a)), and user namespaces are on by default for the standard class (its O6). This design keeps the mirror's anti-affinity rationale, `gitMirror.hostUsers`, and the `lenny-mirror` namespace, and states the `proxied` DNS resolver as a second instance beside `lenny-agent-dns`.
58. **Renames**, straight with no alias: `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` becomes `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, service mode's `maxConcurrent` becomes `maxConcurrentRequestsPerPod` (which leaves with service mode under decision 62), and `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` becomes `acknowledgeSharedPod`, a pool setting required only when `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` is above 1. `recycle.allowCrossTenantReuse` is removed, because the tenant pin already keeps every pod on one tenant.

Adopted by this design in revision 75, on a request of another design:

59. **Git maintenance.** The rendered git configuration sets `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`, as [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md) requests under its D6, because this design owns the configuration and a repack makes the next checkpoint upload a repository's history again.

Raised by revision 75 and settled by the owner on 2026-11-13:

60. **Per-slot scopes on a pod with several slots.** Settled as option (a) on 2026-11-13; the decision log records it as "EG 60: (a). Per-slot netagent scopes. This unblocks UGI 52 and UCD 41." Slot attribution (decision 52) tells `netagent` which slot opened a connection, and every per-session input then applies per slot: each slot's connections use its own session's readable and writable sets, its own environment E, its own `egress.allowedHosts` and `egressLockHosts`, and its own credential bindings, `netagent` closes a session's tunnels and flushes its cached credentials at its slot's release, and evidence names the session. Where attribution is unavailable on a pool's runtime class, the pool falls back to the union scope with E = ∅ and the refusal of `egress.allowedHosts` and `egressLockHosts`, and `sessionIsolationLevel` reports it. The options were:
    - (a) per-slot scopes, as above;
    - (b) the union scope on every pod with several slots under `acknowledgeSharedPod`, with attribution used for the fetch grant alone.

    The reason for (a): each slot already has its own UID, container, runtime process, and per-slot credential volume, so the union was the one remaining reason a slot could use another slot's write scope or credential. It lets [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) drop its E = ∅ rule for pools with attribution, lifts the refusal of `egress.allowedHosts` and `egressLockHosts` on them, and gives [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) decision 52 and [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) decision 41 their ground. The cost is the dependence on a lookup that gVisor and Kata have not been shown to answer, which the fallback covers and "Validation still owed" lists. "Slot attribution and fetch grants", **Per-slot scopes (decision 60)**, "Effective policy", and "Pod models" state the result.
61. **The store fill runs in `lenny-store-fill`.** Settled on 2026-11-13, in the owner's words "if the separate container works from a permissions standpoint, then yes". Under [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) O5 (a) the adapter ran the pinned git to fill each repository store before READY, and a fill fetches and indexes packs from repositories that a platform admin or a tenant admin named, which broke [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway)'s rule that no platform-privileged process parses untrusted content. A separate container, `lenny-store-fill`, now fills the warm stores and builds their pristine trees under its own UID, with no credentials and the store volume as its only read-write mount, so WM-9 needs no store fill exception. The conditions under which it works, which "Validation still owed" lists:
    - the stores are readable by every slot UID, because `lenny-store-fill` writes them with the store group, a dedicated GID, the chart value `storeFill.gid`, that is the primary group of both `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter and of no other container, as the file group and a umask of `0002`, so directories are `0775`, object files are `0444`, and the other class can read and not write, and after each pristine tree's checkout it runs an explicit mode pass, so the tree's directories are `0775`, its files `0444`, and its executables `0555`, whatever modes git's checkout wrote, and clears every setuid and setgid bit;
    - it runs as an init container after the `netagent` native sidecar has started, so its fetches go through `netagent`;
    - `netagent` attributes its UID as the platform's warm fill and applies the fill's authorization, the warm-up policy and the warm list, and refuses that UID once the pod is ready;
    - the adapter, whose primary group is the store group, keeps read-write access to the store volume only to delete a withheld store, which it does through the group class with `openat2` and `unlinkat` relative to directory descriptors under `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`, so the deletion follows no symbolic link, and it never reads repository data or the pristine tree;
    - no slot container holds the store group, and every agent-pod container runs with `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, which `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/security_context.go` sets today, so a setuid or setgid bit in a repository has no effect even before the mode pass clears it;
    - on Kata `block-plain`, where the volume root carries the setgid bit and the dedicated volume GID, `lenny-store-fill` sets each store's directory and its pristine tree's directory to the store group and clears their setgid bit before it writes into them, as [PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction) states;
    - under user namespaces, its UID maps within the pod's range.

    The options were (a) the fill in the adapter as a stated exception, (b) a separate container, and (c) the fill in `netagent`. The owner's form of (b) is an init container rather than the native sidecar this design proposed, with the adapter deleting withheld stores itself rather than asking the fill container over a socket. (c) would have put a git parser in the container that holds the egress identity and every upstream token.

    The owner asked on 2026-11-13 whether writing with the adapter's group as the file group is secure, and accepted the hardening above in the words "ok but add an owed item to stress test this thoroughly (with actual tests)": a dedicated store group in place of the adapter's former GID, held by no slot container, the mode pass clearing setuid and setgid bits, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false` on every agent-pod container, and the no-follow deletion. "Validation still owed" lists the hostile-repository stress suite, and Tier 9 runs it. Kubernetes has no container-level supplementary groups, so the store group is the `runAsGroup` of both the adapter and `lenny-store-fill`, as [PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout) states, and `lenny-pod-security` refuses it as the `runAsGroup` or `runAsUser` of every other container, ephemeral containers included, and in `supplementalGroups`. Sharing the adapter's primary group with the fill exposes nothing else of the adapter's, because the fill mounts no adapter volume other than the store volume, its other mounts are `lenny-bin` and `/run/netagent` read-only, no slot holds the group, and the fill exits before the adapter starts. The owner confirmed this refinement on 2026-11-14, in the words "Ok on the rest".
62. **Proposal 0 is withdrawn, and every agent pod carries a name-constrained anchor.** Settled on 2026-11-13, when the owner decided to "remove non-agent runtimes altogether, in a new design: `type: mcp` and service mode (`executionMode: service`)", and settled that design's open decisions with "O5 (b), yes to all the others". [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) removes service mode in its proposal AR-B, which closes F-5.2.65 and F-5.2.66 by removal. The service-mode tenant pin, its arbitration, router, dial check, listener, network rows, register entry, and lifecycle rules leave this design, together with the service-mode rows of "Pod models", "Effective policy", the egress group label, and level 1's identities. With no runtime process starting before its session's binding, `netagent` issues the name-constrained trust anchor on every agent pod (ARR-8), and the TLS interception bundle stays per deployment or pool because warm-up work still runs before any tenant.
63. **Warm-up checkout on platform pools.** Changed by decision 67 on 2026-11-14, which replaces the target `path` with `prestage: true`. Settled as option (a) on 2026-11-13: the operator's `gitRepository` warm entries gain an optional target `path`, and an entry that names one is staged before the session like a default clone, at warm-up and after every slot release, by the helper of [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) ("WM-15"). An entry without `path` fills a store and stages nothing. "Terms" (Warm list) and "Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings", **Warm-up checkout and the reflink rebuild**, state it.

Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the answers to the consolidated list of open items across the designs:

64. **Platform connections on a pod with several slots.** A connection whose socket owner, as the kernel reports it, is the adapter UID or `netagent`'s UID is marked platform by `netagent` and allowed only to platform destinations: the gateway, object storage, and the git mirror. A slot cannot obtain the marker, because attribution is by the socket owner the kernel reports, and `netagent` strips the header from agent input. The owner asked for the mechanism to be elaborated, and then answered "ok". It fills the gap the cross-design review of 2026-11-13 found: on a pool with per-slot attribution, `lenny-egress-authz` refuses a CONNECT from a pod with several slots that names no listed session, and the adapter's and `netagent`'s own connections had no rule. "Slot attribution and fetch grants", **Platform connections (decision 64).**, states it.
65. **One commit identity per VCS pool.** A session whose repositories sit under several VCS pools needs the author and committer of each pool, which one identity per session configuration file cannot give. `netagent` writes one identity file per VCS pool on the slot's `slot-<n>-netcred`, and the session's include file selects it per repository with git's `includeIf "hasconfig:remote.*.url:<pattern>"`, which the pinned git supports. The owner answered "ok". "Git configuration" states it.
66. **Defaults of `netagent` and the mirror's held-UID budget.** `netagent.resources` defaults to a request of 100m CPU and 128 MiB with a 512 MiB memory limit and no CPU limit, `netagent`'s private emptyDir and its pod-wide volume take a `sizeLimit` of 16 MiB each, and `gitMirror.heldUidBudget` defaults to a quarter of the mirror's fetch-UID range. The owner confirmed the volume sizes, accepted the values, and asked to "record any owed validations", which "Validation still owed" lists: `netagent` under a Chromium clone and many concurrent tunnels, and the held-UID budget against concurrent fetches under holds.
67. **Warm stores come from `sharedAssets` alone.** Settled on 2026-11-14 as the owner's model for question 17, in the owner's words "sharedAssets should define any git stores to warm up. workspaceDefaults shouldn't also do that through `warm: true`" and "in sharedAssets, we should indicate whether we want a repo to be pre-staged". A `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets`, public on an operator's Runtime or a tenant's on a tenant-derived runtime ([`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decision 42](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#decisions)), is the only way to declare a store on the warm list, so the warm default clone, the cache-only entry, and the private operator entry on a tenant-owned pool's row leave. The entry carries `prestage: true` in place of decision 63's target `path`: the owner asked "does prestage need a path? Can't the path be determined at the time of copying (based on workspaceDefaults and the session plan)?", and the first `gitClone` source of the repository in the session's workspace adopts the staged tree at its own `path`, because a rename within the slot's volume keeps inode numbers and modification times, so the index stays valid, and a staged tree records no location of its own, its alternates naming the store by its absolute path under `/lenny/git-stores`. `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` has no `warm` field and is allowed on operator runtimes, and any `gitClone` source uses a store of its URL under decision 47, with the withheld-store rules unchanged. This design's rules on fills, fetch grants, and readable sets are unchanged, because a store's declarer and its fill are as before.
68. **The Kata guest kernel and `inet_diag`.** Settled on 2026-11-14 in the owner's words "the deployer guide would recommend building it with CONFIG_INET_DIAG and CONFIG_INET_TCP_DIAG", after the attribution spike found the Kata 4.3.1 guest kernel built without `CONFIG_INET_DIAG`, so `sock_diag` fails there with `ENOENT` and Kata pools attribute through `/proc/net/tcp`. The deployer guide recommends those two options for the Kata guest kernel, so that `sock_diag` answers in the guest as on runc. It is a recommendation rather than a requirement: without them `netagent` reads `/proc/net/tcp` once per batch of accepts, which accepts connections more slowly under a burst. "Slot attribution and fetch grants" and "Facts the pages state" state it.

The same answers settle rules this design applies from other designs:

- [`user-credential-delivery-design.md` decision 41](user-credential-delivery-design.md#decisions) and [`user-git-identity-design.md` decision 52](user-git-identity-design.md#decisions) are settled together: user-credential bindings, `lockEgressToHosts`, and user git identity are allowed on pools whose status records per-slot attribution, and refused on fallback pools and on recycled or used pods. The spike the owner asked for measured on 2026-11-14 which runtime classes answer attribution: runc through `sock_diag`, and gVisor and Kata through `/proc/net/tcp` ("Slot attribution and fetch grants").
- [`tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decision 41](tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md#decisions) refuses a session on a pod with several slots and attribution whose environment denies the host of a warm-list entry with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE`, and "Facts the pages state" lists that case under the code.
- A write that names a removed field is refused with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, the rule of AR O3, which the owner extended to every removed field on 2026-11-13. "Relationship to the current pool egress configuration" applies it to `deliveryMode`.

[`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md) D6, settled on 2026-11-13, adds to decision 59 that a runtime may override the rendered maintenance settings through its global or repository configuration or `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, at the cost of its own next checkpoint ("Git configuration", **Maintenance settings.**).

- Revisions 52 to 55 changed only the delegated child rule, the resolver hook, the `crossedFromEnvironment` envelope field, UG-1's dependency row, and a note under "Replaced pieces", after `tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 14.

The UG-3 row of the proposal dependencies gains EG-3 with git path T, as the VCS layer's own dependency table states.
- Revision 71, dated 2026-11-08, applies decisions 45 to 48, which the owner settled that day with `tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decisions 31 to 37, and changes these rules. Before a session binds, a pod's readable set is the pool's warm list plus the repositories of the warm-up policy that applies to it, in place of the union of a tenant-owned pod's warm list with the base's public `gitRepository` entries, and the unbound and tenant-bound formulas under "Effective policy" gain the warm list's hosts. The `gitAccess` formula's `git hosts` reads the pool's warm list. A `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime is public only, and a private store comes from a tenant-owned pool's warm list. A store serves any `gitClone` source whose URL matches, and the bullet **For a tenant's default clone** becomes **For the warm list and a tenant's default clone**. "Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings" points at the requirements for the rewrite of 0111, states that the rewrite drops the lineage rule if no case remains, and states that the calls carrying the restore set also carry the stores to withhold. "Terms", the Proposal 0111 row, the warm-up policy row of "Scopes and who manages them", the warm fill scenario, the tenant-owned pool row of "Pod models", the mirror's warm fill bullet, "Specification sections affected", and the Tier 5 warm-up test state these rules. Decision 38 of that design stays open with its recommendation.
- Revision 72 records that `tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` decision 38 is settled, and changed no rule.

It changes these rules:
  - The warm list is rendered per pool and revision, and the warm-list hosts in "Effective policy", the readable set before binding, and level 1's identity lists read the pod's pool and revision (RV-3).

  It also records the residual of MCP connector calls, which run on the gateway, from `connector-authorization-and-external-memory-design.md`, notes that `incremental-checkpoint-design.md` decision D6 is pending, cites §4.4.4 for the hard workspace size limit, names `user-git-identity-design.md` as the owner of `allowedPushOptions`, adds the dependency rows for TS-1, TS-3, FE-1, DR-4, DR-5, DR-9, DR-11, SO-5b, and OC-2, makes EG-12 depend on EG-3, lists the open requests of `tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` and `tenant-outbound-connector-design.md`, names the outbound gateway in "Tenant outbound connector", replaces "two gates" with "mint checks", and removes explicit counts, history sentences, and a garbled clause from body sections.
- Revision 74, dated 2026-11-09, applies the owner's decisions of that date on mirror fill causes and ref resolution, given in the owner's words "Adversarially review your recommendations and, if they pass, go with them", and applies the open request of `tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` on mirror fill causes, which leaves "Requests still open". It changes these rules:
  - A mirror fill names the pool and one cause: the UID of the pod whose fetch caused the fill, a gateway cause assertion, or a prefetch record. These replace the session cause and the warm fill cause.
  - The Token Service reads a pod's binding through its own `pkg/binding` instance, verifies a gateway cause assertion's signature, or reads the prefetch record itself. It refuses a cause that is not live or that belongs to another tenant, and a cause from another tenant also writes an audit event and raises an alert.
  - For a pod cause, E follows TS-1's rules, and for an assertion, E is the value it carries. The Token Service accepts only the pool the shared VCS pool selection function picks for the upstream and E, and for a prefetch cause only the pool the record names.
  - The cause is checked at a fill's first mint. The Token Service returns a fill ID bound to the mirror's identity, the tenant, the upstream, and the pool, and later mints that present it need no new check for the fill's lifetime, `tokenService.mirrorFillLifetimeSeconds` with a default of 3600, which "Configuration by scope" lists.
  - A prefetch record is live from its write until its fill completes or fails, and a change to the named pool's `environments` or prefixes, or its deletion, voids a pending record. Every prefetch record carries a pool, and the prefetch resource stays tenant-admin only.
  - Ref resolution runs after the post-chain point of RT-2 on every creation path, and before the pod claim where the plan arrives with the create request. At finalize, the pod was claimed at create under proposal 0007, so a bad ref fails the finalize on a claimed pod, and the assertion takes E from the session row.
  - The gateway's `ls-refs` carries a gateway cause assertion on the direct path and the mirror path: a JWT of type `lenny-cause+jwt` with the audience `lenny-cause`, valid for 60 seconds and signed with the gateway's signing key. The decision service verifies it and reads E from it, and the Token Service and the decision service accept only that type and audience for a cause, which a new Tier 9 test holds. "Terms", "Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**, **Where the pool checks run**, "Specification sections affected", the requests on the proposals, the Tier 4 tests, "Validation still owed", and a new residual, **Mirror fill causes**, state these rules.

- Revision 75, dated 2026-11-13, applies the owner's decisions of 2026-11-12 in the consistency pass across the designs, recorded as decisions 51 to 58, and the instructions that [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md), [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md), [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md), [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md), [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md), [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md), and [`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md) address to this design. It changes these rules:
  - `lenny-materialize` runs every git step on a slot repository as the slot UID with the pinned git, and the adapter runs no git on slot repositories; it keeps the pinned git for the store fill alone. The workspace ownership item of 0111 is settled, the tree-size check maps `git_tree_limit` to `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, the index refresh runs more than 1 s after checkout, and §13.4's archive sentence gives way to WM-9's rule (decision 51).
  - "Slot attribution and fetch grants" is new: `netagent` attributes a connection to its slot by owner UID, and the adapter's fetch grant over `CH-FETCHGRANT` gives a helper run's fetches the materialization rules (decision 52). Per-slot scopes are open decision 60, and the store fill's parsing at the adapter UID is open decision 61.
  - The embedded deployment model and pre-launched runtimes leave every table, test, and statement, and the name-constrained trust anchor applies on every session-mode pod, with service mode and `type: mcp` as the remaining cases without constraints (decisions 53 and 54).
  - The paths follow the fixed layout, the stores are on `lenny-git-stores`, per-slot `delivery: file` volumes replace the shared one, and the warm-up checkout and the reflink rebuild join the warm-clone text, with a withheld store's staged tree removed (decision 55).

  - The renames apply, and `allowCrossTenantReuse` is gone (decision 58). `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` join the rendered git configuration (decision 59).

"Labels from other designs" gains the designs of the pass. "Instructions for reviewers of other designs" is new, and EG-3 is ordered before RP-B.

The §13.4 text drops the adapter's fill as a third parser.

  - Every per-session value lives on the slot's own `slot-<n>-netcred`: the binding list carries the session launch variables and the applied coordination generation, which `netagent` also sends to the adapter in an `applied` message on `CH-FETCHGRANT`; the pod-wide git configuration includes `/run/netagent/credentials/gitconfig`, which each slot resolves on its own mount and which carries the session's commit identity and the `Lenny-Session` commit template, applying the open request of `user-git-identity-design.md` for UG-3; a `federation` binding's file variable and Azure's identifiers are session launch variables that the supervisor sets at launch, which lifts the earlier limit of Azure `federation` to tenant-owned pools; `NETRC` is set on every pod to a file on the slot's volume that is empty without a Basic binding; and a binding has no placeholder of its own, the profile's constant `lenny-injected` being the only one. "Injected configuration", "Delivery modes", "Git configuration", the scenarios, the SDK accessor, "Facts the pages state", and the Tier 1 and Tier 2 tests follow.
  - The refusal of a connection whose owner neither reader finds, and the refusal of a CONNECT naming no listed session, apply only on a pool whose status records per-slot attribution. **Fallback pools.** is new: the attribution status is set from a pod's first start report and cleared when the runtime class or the `netagent` image changes, an `onRequest` or `poolAttach` binding is refused at the post-chain point with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` rather than admitted with no credential, a `scope: always` binding applies pod-wide, a pod with no reader on an attributing pool is replaced under backoff with the `NetagentAttributionUnavailable` alert, and a fetch grant names the pod. The carrier of the start report to the controller is owed to EG-3.

"Credentials", **The gateway runs no git.**, states that `lenny-store-fill`, rather than the adapter, fills the stores.
  - `netagent`'s private emptyDir, its pod-wide volume, and each `slot-<n>-netcred` are memory-backed with proposed sizes of 16 MiB, 16 MiB, and 4 MiB, which wait on the owner with `netagent.resources`. "Validation still owed" adds the store fill's 1 GiB memory limit against the largest store and pristine tree a pool admits.

"The `netagent` container", "Delivery modes", the Tier 2 test, and the status line follow.

On Kata `block-plain`, the fill sets each store's directory and its pristine tree's directory to the store group before writing, as PL-5 now states, in place of the pool's `fsGroup`.

On a pool with per-slot attribution, a CONNECT that names no listed session is refused unless it carries the marker.

**Review of revision 50.** A stop check found no Major or higher issue and 13 Minors, all of which held, and it traced the last round's changes rather than reading end to end. The pool key and closure, and `netagent` asking I8 before a 401, are retagged **[design]**. A route write closes pooled upstream connections to the hosts it changes, the push list gains the platform default and target writes, the decision's git list omits `direct` and mirror routes, and `default` names the platform default.

| Finding | Outcome | Resolution |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| N1, route lists | Holds. | Reduced to calls carrying a user token or a client secret, with a fall-through and a named list. The refusal of an unasserted Token Service CONNECT is removed, because it cannot be expressed by host. |

| N3, suspended binding | Holds. | The hook row and test except the qualification. |

| Run: per-object `error` | Stopped git-lfs 3.6.0. | Behaviour stated, including clone's output naming no host. |
| Run: 3-failure refusal | It caps transfers on a 401, resets per batch, and never fires on a 500. | **Kept, with its limit stated,** because a batch-time `error` cannot cover a deliverable transfer a store refuses. |
| Minors 1, 2, 3, 7, 10, and 11 | Hold. | Corrected. |

None was rejected. An unasserted CONNECT on a host with both lists naming different targets keeps the existing first-match lookup, which is older than this mechanism, and no documented topology has it.
